<span class="atom-split" data-atom="hamlet" data-title="HAMLET" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro"></span>


[[Hamlet|HAMLET]]
SEVERAL references from 1589 onwards witness the existence of a play about [[Hamlet]], but Francis Meres did not attribute a play with this title to Shakespeare in 1598. The first clear reference to Shakespeare’s play is its entry in the Stationers’ Register on 26 July 1602 as The [[Revenge]] of [[Hamlet]] Prince [of] [[Denmark]], when it was said to have been ‘lately acted by the Lord Chamberlain his servants’. It survives in three versions; their relationship is a matter of dispute on which views about when Shakespeare wrote his play, and in what form, depend. In 1603 appeared an inferior text apparently assembled from actors’ memories; it has only about 2,200 lines. In the following year, as if to put the record straight, James Roberts (to whom the play had been entered in 1602) published it as ‘newly imprinted and enlarged to almost as much again as it was, according to the true and perfect copy’. At about 3,800 lines, this is the longest version. The 1623 Folio offers a still different text, some 230 lines shorter than the 1604 version, differing verbally from that at many points, and including about 70 additional lines. It is our belief that Shakespeare wrote [[Hamlet]] about 1600, and revised it later; that the 1604 edition was printed from his original papers; that the Folio represents the revised version; and that the 1603 edition represents a very imperfect report of an abridged version of the revision. So our text is based on the Folio; passages present in the 1604 quarto but absent from the Folio are printed as Additional Passages because we believe that, however fine they may be in themselves, Shakespeare decided that the play as a whole would be better without them.
The plot of [[Hamlet]] originates in a Scandinavian folk-tale told in the twelfth-century Danish [[history|History]] written in Latin by the Danish Saxo Grammaticus. François de Belleforest retold it in the fifth volume (1570) of his Histoires Tragiques, not translated into English until 1608. Saxo, through Belleforest, provided the basic story of a Prince of [[Denmark]] committed to [[Revenge|revenge]] his father’s murder by his own brother ([[Claudius]]) who has married the dead man’s widow ([[Gertrude]]). As in Shakespeare, [[Hamlet]] pretends to be mad, kills his uncle’s counsellor ([[Polonius]]) while he is eavesdropping, rebukes his mother, is sent to England under the escort of two retainers ([[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]]) who bear orders that he be put to [[Death|death]] on arrival, finds the letter containing the orders and alters it so that it is the retainers who are executed, returns to [[Denmark]], and kills the [[King]].
Belleforest’s story differs at some points from Shakespeare’s, and Shakespeare elaborates it, adding, for example, the [[Ghost]] of [[Hamlet]]’s father, the coming of the actors to Elsinore, the performance of the play through which [[Hamlet]] tests his uncle’s [[Guilt|guilt]], [[Ophelia]]’s [[Madness|madness]] and [[Death|death]], [[Laertes]]’ plot to [[Revenge|revenge]] his father’s [[Death|death]], the [[Grave|grave]]-digger, [[Ophelia]]’s funeral, and the characters of Osric and Fortinbras. How much he owed to the lost [[Hamlet]] play we cannot tell; what is certain is that Shakespeare used his mastery of a wide range of diverse styles in both verse and prose, and his genius for dramatic effect, to create from these and other sources the most complex, varied, and exciting drama that had ever been seen on the English stage. Its popularity was instant and enduring. The play has had a profound influence on Western culture, and Shakespeare’s [[Hamlet]] has himself entered the world of myth.

THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY

The [[Tragedy]] of [[Hamlet]], Prince of [[Denmark]]

### Atto 1, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Barnardo and Francisco, two sentinels, at several doors |
| BARNARDO | Who’s there? |
| FRANCISCO | Nay, answer me. Stand and unfold yourself. |
| BARNARDO | Long live the [[King]]! |
| FRANCISCO | Barnardo? |
| BARNARDO | He. |
| FRANCISCO | You come most carefully upon your hour. |
| BARNARDO | ’Tis now struck twelve. Get thee to bed, Francisco. |
| FRANCISCO | For this relief much thanks. ’Tis bitter cold,<br>And I am sick at heart. |
| BARNARDO | Have you had quiet guard? |
| FRANCISCO | Not a mouse stirring. |
| BARNARDO | [[Well]], good [[Night\|night]].<br>If you do meet [[Horatio]] and Marcellus,<br>The rivals of my watch, bid them make haste. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]] and Marcellus |
| FRANCISCO | I think I hear them.—Stand! Who’s there? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Friends to this ground. |
| MARCELLUS | And liegemen to the Dane. |
| FRANCISCO | Give you good [[Night\|night]]. |
| MARCELLUS | O farewell, honest soldier. Who hath relieved you? |
| FRANCISCO | Barnardo has my place. Give you good [[Night\|night]]. Exit |
| MARCELLUS | Holla, Barnardo! |
| BARNARDO | Say—what, is [[Horatio]] there? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A piece of him. |
| BARNARDO | Welcome, [[Horatio]]. Welcome, good Marcellus. |
| MARCELLUS | What, has this thing appeared again tonight? |
| BARNARDO | I have seen nothing. |
| MARCELLUS | [[Horatio]] says ’tis but our fantasy,<br>And will not let belief take hold of him<br>Touching this dreaded sight twice seen of us.<br>Therefore I have entreated him along<br>With us to watch the minutes of this [[Night\|night]],<br>That if again this apparition come<br>He may approve our eyes and speak to it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Tush, tush, ’twill not appear. |
| BARNARDO | Sit down a while,<br>And let us once again assail your ears,<br>That are so fortified against our story,<br>What we two nights have seen. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | [[Well]], sit we down,<br>And let us hear Barnardo speak of this. |
| BARNARDO | Last [[Night\|night]] of all,<br>When yon same star that’s westward from the pole<br>Had made his course t’illume that part of heaven<br>Where now it burns, Marcellus and myself,<br>The bell then beating one–– |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]] in complete armour, holding a truncheon, with his beaver up |
| MARCELLUS | Peace, break thee off. Look where it comes again. |
| BARNARDO | In the same figure like the [[King]] that’s dead. |
| MARCELLUS | (to [[Horatio]])<br>Thou art a scholar—speak to it, [[Horatio]]. |
| BARNARDO | Looks it not like the [[King]]?—Mark it, [[Horatio]]. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Most like. It harrows me with [[Fear\|fear]] and [[Wonder\|wonder]]. |
| BARNARDO | It would be spoke to. |
| MARCELLUS | Question it, [[Horatio]]. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to the [[Ghost]])<br>What art thou that usurp’st this [[Time\|time]] of [[Night\|night]],<br>Together with that fair and warlike form<br>In which the majesty of buried [[Denmark]]<br>Did sometimes march? By heaven, I charge thee speak. |
| MARCELLUS | It is offended. |
| BARNARDO | See, it stalks away. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to the [[Ghost]])<br>Stay, speak, speak, I charge thee speak. Exit [[Ghost]] |
| MARCELLUS | ’Tis gone, and will not answer. |
| BARNARDO | How now, [[Horatio]]? You tremble and look pale.<br>Is not this something more than fantasy?<br>What think you on’t? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Before my God, I might not this believe<br>Without the sensible and true avouch<br>Of mine own eyes. |
| MARCELLUS | Is it not like the [[King]]? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | As thou art to thyself.<br>Such was the very armour he had on<br>When he th‘ambitious Norway combated.<br>So frowned he once when in an angry parley<br>He smote the sledded Polacks on the ice.<br>’Tis strange. |
| MARCELLUS | Thus twice before, and just at this dead hour,<br>With martial stalk hath he gone by our watch. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | In what particular thought to work I know not,<br>But in the gross and scope of my opinion<br>This bodes some strange eruption to our state. |
| MARCELLUS | Good now, sit down, and tell me, he that knows,<br>Why this same strict and most observant watch<br>So nightly toils the subject of the land,<br>And why such daily cast of brazen cannon,<br>And foreign mart for implements of war,<br>Why such impress of shipwrights, whose sore task<br>Does not divide the [[Sunday]] from the week:<br>What might be toward that this sweaty haste<br>Doth make the [[Night\|night]] joint-labourer with the day,<br>Who is’t that can inform me? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | That can I—<br>At least the whisper goes so: our last [[King\|king]],<br>Whose image even but now appeared to us,<br>Was as you know by Fortinbras of Norway,<br>Thereto pricked on by a most emulate [[Pride\|pride]],<br>Dared to the combat; in which our valiant [[Hamlet]]—<br>For so this side of our known world esteemed him—<br>Did slay this Fortinbras, who by a sealed compact<br>[[Well]] ratified by law and heraldry<br>Did forfeit with his life all those his lands<br>Which he stood seized on to the conqueror;<br>Against the which a moiety competent<br>Was gaged by our [[King]], which had returned<br>To the inheritance of Fortinbras<br>Had he been vanquisher, as by the same cov‘nant<br>And carriage of the article designed<br>His fell to [[Hamlet]]. Now sir, young Fortinbras,<br>Of unimproved mettle hot and full,<br>Hath in the skirts of Norway here and there<br>Sharked up a list of landless resolutes<br>For food and diet to some enterprise<br>That hath a stomach in’t, which is no other—<br>And it doth [[Well\|well]] appear unto our state—<br>But to recover of us by strong hand<br>And terms compulsative those foresaid lands<br>So by his father lost. And this, I take it,<br>Is the main motive of our preparations,<br>The source of this our watch, and the chief head<br>Of this post-haste and rummage in the land. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]], as before |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | But soft, behold—lo where it comes again!<br>I’ll [[Cross\|cross]] it though it blast me.—Stay, illusion.<br>The [[Ghost]] spreads his arms<br>If thou hast any sound or use of voice,<br>Speak to me.<br>If there be any good thing to be done<br>That may to thee do ease and grace to me,<br>Speak to me.<br>If thou art privy to thy country’s fate<br>Which happily foreknowing may avoid,<br>O speak!<br>Or if thou hast uphoarded in thy life<br>Extorted treasure in the womb of earth—<br>For which, they say, you spirits oft walk in [[Death\|death]]—<br>The cock crows<br>Speak of it, stay and speak.—Stop it, Marcellus. |
| MARCELLUS | Shall I strike at it with my partisan? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Do, if it will not stand. |
| BARNARDO | ’Tis here. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | ’Tis here. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Ghost]] |
| MARCELLUS | ’Tis gone.<br>We do it wrong, being so majestical,<br>To offer it the show of violence,<br>For it is as the air invulnerable,<br>And our vain blows malicious mockery. |
| BARNARDO | It was about to speak when the cock crew. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | And then it started like a guilty thing<br>Upon a fearful summons. I have heard<br>The cock, that is the trumpet to the morn,<br>Doth with his lofty and shrill-sounding throat<br>Awake the god of day, and at his warning,<br>Whether in sea or [[Fire\|fire]], in earth or air,<br>Th’extravagant and erring spirit hies<br>To his confine; and of the [[Truth\|truth]] herein<br>This present object made probation. |
| MARCELLUS | It faded on the crowing of the cock.<br>Some say that ever ’gainst that season comes<br>Wherein our saviour’s birth is celebrated<br>The bird of dawning singeth all [[Night\|night]] long;<br>And then, they say, no spirit can walk abroad,<br>The nights are wholesome; then no planets strike,<br>No fairy takes, nor [[Witch\|witch]] hath power to charm,<br>So hallowed and so gracious is the [[Time\|time]]. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | So have I heard, and do in part believe it.<br>But look, the morn in russet mantle clad<br>Walks o’er the dew of yon high eastern [[Hill\|hill]].<br>Break we our watch up, and by my advice<br>Let us impart what we have seen tonight<br>Unto young [[Hamlet]]; for upon my life,<br>This spirit, dumb to us, will speak to him.<br>Do you consent we shall acquaint him with it,<br>As needful in our loves, fitting our duty? |
| MARCELLUS | Let’s do’t, I pray; and I this morning know<br>Where we shall find him most conveniently. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[Claudius]], [[King]] of [[Denmark]], [[Gertrude]] the [[Queen]], members of the Council, such as [[Polonius]], his son [[Laertes]] and daughter [[Ophelia]], Prince [[Hamlet]] dressed in black, with others |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Though yet of [[Hamlet]] our dear brother’s [[Death\|death]]<br>The [[Memory\|memory]] be green, and that it us befitted<br>To bear our hearts in grief and our whole kingdom<br>To be contracted in one brow of woe,<br>Yet so far hath discretion fought with [[Nature\|nature]]<br>That we with wisest sorrow think on him<br>Together with remembrance of ourselves.<br>Therefore our sometime sister, now our [[Queen\|queen]],<br>Th‘imperial jointress of this warlike state,<br>Have we as ’twere with a defeated joy,<br>With one auspicious and one dropping eye,<br>With mirth in funeral and with [[Dirge\|dirge]] in [[Marriage\|marriage]],<br>In equal scale weighing delight and dole,<br>Taken to wife. Nor have we herein barred<br>Your better wisdoms, which have freely gone<br>With this affair along. For all, our thanks.<br>Now follows that you know young Fortinbras,<br>Holding a weak supposal of our worth,<br>Or thinking by our late dear brother’s [[Death\|death]]<br>Our state to be disjoint and out of frame,<br>Co-leaguèd with the dream of his advantage,<br>He hath not failed to pester us with message<br>Importing the surrender of those lands<br>Lost by his father, with all bonds of law,<br>To our most valiant brother. So much for him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Valtemand and Cornelius |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Now for ourself, and for this [[Time\|time]] of meeting,<br>Thus much the business is: we have here writ<br>To Norway, uncle of young Fortinbras—<br>Who, impotent and bed-rid, scarcely hears<br>Of this his nephew’s purpose—to suppress<br>His further gait herein, in that the levies,<br>The lists, and full proportions are all made<br>Out of his subject; and we here dispatch<br>You, good Cornelius, and you, Valtemand,<br>For bearers of this greeting to old Norway,<br>Giving to you no further personal power<br>To business with the [[King]] more than the scope<br>Of these dilated articles allow.<br>Farewell, and let your haste commend your duty. |
| VALTEMAND | In that and all things will we show our duty. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | We [[Doubt\|doubt]] it nothing, heartily farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Valtemand and Cornelius |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | And now, [[Laertes]], what’s the news with you?<br>You told us of some suit. What is’t, [[Laertes]]?<br>You cannot speak of reason to the Dane<br>And lose your voice. What wouldst thou beg, [[Laertes]],<br>That shall not be my offer, not thy asking?<br>The head is not more native to the heart,<br>The hand more instrumental to the mouth,<br>Than is the throne of [[Denmark]] to thy father.<br>What wouldst thou have, [[Laertes]]? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Dread my lord,<br>Your leave and favour to return to France,<br>From whence though willingly I came to [[Denmark]]<br>To show my duty in your coronation,<br>Yet now I must confess, that duty done,<br>My thoughts and wishes bend again towards France<br>And bow them to your gracious leave and pardon. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Have you your father’s leave? What says [[Polonius]]? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | He hath, my lord, wrung from me my slow leave<br>By laboursome petition, and at last<br>Upon his will I sealed my hard consent.<br>I do beseech you give him leave to go. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Take thy fair hour, [[Laertes]]. [[Time]] be thine,<br>And thy best graces spend it at thy will.<br>But now, my cousin [[Hamlet]], and my son— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A little more than kin and less than kind. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How is it that the clouds still hang on you? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Not so, my lord, I am too much i’th’ sun. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Good [[Hamlet]], cast thy nightly colour off,<br>And let thine eye look like a friend on [[Denmark]].<br>Do not for ever with thy vailèd lids<br>Seek for thy noble father in the [[Dust\|dust]].<br>Thou know‘st ’tis common—all that lives must die,<br>Passing through [[Nature\|nature]] to [[Eternity\|eternity]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, madam, it is common. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | If it be,<br>Why seems it so particular with thee? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Seems, madam? Nay, it is. I know not ‘seems’.<br>‘Tis not [[Alone\|alone]] my inky cloak, good-mother,<br>Nor customary suits of solemn black,<br>Nor windy suspiration of forced breath,<br>No, nor the fruitful [[River\|river]] in the eye,<br>Nor the dejected haviour of the visage,<br>Together with all forms, moods, shows of grief<br>That can denote me truly. These indeed ‘seem’,<br>For they are actions that a man might play;<br>But I have that within which passeth show—<br>These but the trappings and the suits of woe. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | ‘Tis sweet and commendable in your [[Nature\|nature]], [[Hamlet]],<br>To give these mourning duties to your father;<br>But you must know your father lost a father;<br>That father lost, lost his; and the survivor bound<br>In filial obligation for some term<br>To do obsequious sorrow. But to persever<br>In obstinate condolement is a course<br>Of impious stubbornness, ’tis unmanly grief,<br>It shows a will most incorrect to heaven,<br>A heart unfortified, a mind impatient,<br>An understanding simple and unschooled;<br>For what we know must be, and is as common<br>As any the most vulgar thing to sense,<br>Why should we in our peevish opposition<br>Take it to heart? Fie, ‘tis a fault to heaven,<br>A fault against the dead, a fault to [[Nature\|nature]],<br>To reason most absurd, whose common theme<br>Is [[Death\|death]] of fathers, and who still hath cried<br>From the first corpse till he that died today,<br>’This must be so’. We pray you throw to earth<br>This unprevailing woe, and think of us<br>As of a father; for let the world take note<br>You are the most immediate to our throne,<br>And with no less nobility of [[Love\|love]]<br>Than that which dearest father bears his son<br>Do I impart towards you. For your intent<br>In going back to school in Wittenberg,<br>It is most retrograde to our [[Desire\|desire]],<br>And we beseech you bend you to remain<br>Here in the cheer and comfort of our eye,<br>Our chiefest courtier, cousin, and our son. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Let not thy mother lose her prayers, [[Hamlet]].<br>I pray thee stay with us, go not to Wittenberg. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I shall in all my best obey you, madam. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Why, ’tis a loving and a fair reply.<br>Be as ourself in [[Denmark]]. (To [[Gertrude]]) Madam, come.<br>This gentle and unforced accord of [[Hamlet]]<br>Sits smiling to my heart; in grace whereof,<br>No jocund health that [[Denmark]] drinks today<br>But the great cannon to the clouds shall tell,<br>And the [[King]]’s rouse the heavens shall bruit again,<br>Re-speaking earthly thunder. Come, away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. pmlmmmExeunt all but [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O that this too too solid flesh would melt,<br>Thaw, and resolve itself into a dew,<br>Or that the Everlasting had not fixed<br>His canon ‘gainst [[Self\|self]]-slaughter! O God, O God,<br>How weary, stale, flat, and unprofitable<br>Seem to me all the uses of this world!<br>Fie on’t, ah fie, fie! ’Tis an unweeded [[Garden\|garden]]<br>That grows to seed; things rank and gross in [[Nature\|nature]]<br>Possess it merely. That it should come to this—<br>But two months dead—nay, not so much, not two—<br>So excellent a [[King\|king]], that was to this<br>[[Hyperion]] to a satyr, so loving to my mother<br>That he might not beteem the winds of heaven<br>Visit her face too roughly! Heaven and earth,<br>Must I remember? Why, she would hang on him<br>As if increase of appetite had grown<br>By what it fed on, and yet within a month—<br>Let me not think on’t; frailty, thy name is woman—<br>A little month, or ere those shoes were old<br>With which she followed my poor father’s [[Body\|body]],<br>Like Niobe, all [[Tears\|tears]], why she, even she—<br>O God, a beast that wants discourse of reason<br>Would have mourned longer!—married with mine<br>uncle,<br>My father’s brother, but no more like my father<br>Than I to Hercules; within a month,<br>Ere yet the salt of most unrighteous [[Tears\|tears]]<br>Had left the flushing of her gallèd eyes,<br>She married. O most wicked [[Speed\|speed]], to post<br>With such dexterity to incestuous sheets!<br>It is not, nor it cannot come to good.<br>But break, my heart, for I must hold my tongue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]], Marcellus, and Barnardo |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Hail to your lordship. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am glad to see you [[Well\|well]].<br>[[Horatio]]—or I do forget myself. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | The same, my lord, and your poor servant ever. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, my good friend; I’ll change that name with you.<br>And what make you from Wittenberg, [[Horatio]]?—<br>Marcellus. |
| MARCELLUS | My good lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am very glad to see you. (To Barnardo) Good even,<br>sir.—<br>But what in [[Faith\|faith]] make you from Wittenberg? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A truant disposition, good my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I would not have your enemy say so,<br>Nor shall you do mine ear that violence<br>To make it truster of your own report<br>Against yourself. I know you are no truant.<br>But what is your affair in Elsinore?<br>We’ll teach you to drink deep ere you depart. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | My lord, I came to see your father’s funeral. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I prithee do not mock me, fellow-student;<br>I think it was to see my mother’s wedding. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Indeed, my lord, it followed hard upon. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thrift, thrift, [[Horatio]]. The funeral baked meats<br>Did coldly furnish forth the [[Marriage\|marriage]] tables.<br>Would I had met my dearest foe in heaven<br>Ere I had ever seen that day, [[Horatio]].<br>My father—methinks I see my father. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | O where, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | In my mind’s eye, [[Horatio]]. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I saw him once. A was a goodly [[King\|king]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A was a man. Take him for all in all,<br>I shall not look upon his like again. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | My lord, I think I saw him yesternight. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Saw? Who? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | My lord, the [[King]] your father. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The [[King]] my father? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Season your admiration for a while<br>With an attent ear till I may deliver,<br>Upon the witness of these gentlemen,<br>This marvel to you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | For God’s [[Love\|love]] let me hear! |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Two nights together had these gentlemen,<br>Marcellus and Barnardo, on their watch,<br>In the dead waste and middle of the [[Night\|night]],<br>Been thus encountered. A figure like your father,<br>Armed at all points exactly, cap-à-pie,<br>Appears before them, and with solemn march<br>Goes slow and stately by them. Thrice he walked<br>By their oppressed and [[Fear\|fear]]-surprised eyes |
| *(didascalia)* | Within his truncheon’s length, whilst they distilled |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Almost to jelly with the act of [[Fear\|fear]]<br>Stand dumb and speak not to him. This to me<br>In dreadful secrecy impart they did,<br>And I with them the third [[Night\|night]] kept the watch,<br>Where, as they had delivered, both in [[Time\|time]],<br>Form of the thing, each word made true and good,<br>The apparition comes. I knew your father;<br>These hands are not more like. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | But where was this? |
| MARCELLUS | My lord, upon the platform where we watched. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Did you not speak to it? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | My lord, I did,<br>But answer made it none; yet once methought<br>It lifted up it head and did address<br>Itself to motion like as it would speak,<br>But even then the morning cock crew loud,<br>And at the sound it shrunk in haste away<br>And vanished from our sight. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ’Tis very strange. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | As I do live, my honoured lord, ’tis true,<br>And we did think it writ down in our duty<br>To let you know of it. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Indeed, indeed, sirs; but this troubles me.—<br>Hold you the watch tonight? |
| BARNARDO | and MARCELLUS<br>We do, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Armed, say you? |
| BARNARDO | and MARCELLUS Armed, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | From top to toe? |
| BARNARDO | and MARCELLUS<br>My lord, from head to foot. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then saw you not his face. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | O yes, my lord, he wore his beaver up. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What looked he? Frowningly? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A countenance more<br>In sorrow than in anger. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Pale or red? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Nay, very pale. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And fixed his eyes upon you? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Most constantly. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I would I had been there. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | It would have much amazed you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Very like, very like. Stayed it long? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | While one with moderate haste might tell a hundred. |
| BARNARDO | and MARCELLUS Longer, longer. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Not when I saw’t. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | His beard was grizzly, no? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | It was as I have seen it in his life,<br>A sable silvered. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I’ll watch tonight. Perchance<br>‘Twill walk again. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I warrant you it will. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | If it assume my noble father’s person<br>I’ll speak to it though hell itself should gape<br>And bid me hold my peace. I pray you all,<br>If you have hitherto concealed this sight,<br>Let it be treble in your [[Silence\|silence]] still,<br>And whatsoever else shall hap tonight,<br>Give it an understanding but no tongue.<br>I will requite your loves. So fare ye [[Well\|well]].<br>Upon the platform ’twixt eleven and twelve<br>I’ll visit you. |
| ALL THREE | Our duty to your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Your [[Love\|love]], as mine to you. Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | My father’s spirit in arms! All is not [[Well\|well]].<br>I [[Doubt\|doubt]] some foul play. Would the [[Night\|night]] were come.<br>Till then, sit still, my [[Soul\|soul]]. Foul deeds will rise,<br>Though all the earth o’erwhelm them, to men’s eyes. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 1, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Laertes]] and [[Ophelia]], his sister |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | My necessaries are inbarqued. Farewell.<br>And, sister, as the winds give benefit<br>And convoy is assistant, do not [[Sleep\|sleep]]<br>But let me hear from you. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Do you [[Doubt\|doubt]] that? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | For [[Hamlet]] and the trifling of his favour,<br>Hold it a fashion and a toy in [[Blood\|blood]],<br>A violet in the youth of primy [[Nature\|nature]],<br>Forward not permanent, sweet not lasting,<br>The perfume and suppliance of a minute,<br>No more. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | No more but so? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Think it no more.<br>For [[Nature\|nature]] crescent does not grow [[Alone\|alone]]<br>In thews and bulk, but as his temple waxes<br>The inward service of the mind and [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Grows wide withal. Perhaps he loves you now,<br>And now no soil nor cautel doth besmirch<br>The virtue of his will; but you must [[Fear\|fear]],<br>His [[Greatness\|greatness]] weighed, his will is not his own,<br>For he himself is subject to his birth.<br>He may not, as unvalued persons do,<br>Carve for himself, for on his choice depends<br>The [[Sanity\|sanity]] and health of the whole state;<br>And therefore must his choice be circumscribed<br>Unto the voice and yielding of that [[Body\|body]]<br>Whereof he is the head. Then if he says he loves you,<br>It fits your wisdom so far to believe it<br>As he in his peculiar sect and force<br>May give his saying deed, which is no further<br>Than the main voice of [[Denmark]] goes withal.<br>Then weigh what loss your [[Honour\|honour]] may sustain<br>If with too credent ear you list his [[songs]],<br>Or lose your heart, or your chaste treasure open<br>To his unmastered importunity.<br>[[Fear]] it, [[Ophelia]], [[Fear\|fear]] it, my dear sister,<br>And keep within the rear of your affection,<br>Out of the shot and danger of [[Desire\|desire]].<br>The chariest maid is prodigal enough<br>If she unmask her [[Beauty\|beauty]] to the [[Moon\|moon]].<br>Virtue itself scapes not calumnious strokes.<br>The canker galls the infants of the [[Spring\|spring]]<br>Too oft before their buttons be disclosed,<br>And in the morn and liquid dew of youth<br>Contagious blastments are most imminent.<br>Be wary then; best safety lies in [[Fear\|fear]];<br>Youth to itself rebels, though none else near. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I shall th’effect of this good lesson keep<br>As watchman to my heart; but, good my brother,<br>Do not, as some ungracious pastors do,<br>Show me the steep and thorny way to heaven<br>Whilst like a puffed and reckless libertine<br>Himself the primrose path of dalliance treads<br>And recks not his own rede. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O [[Fear\|fear]] me not. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]] |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I stay too long—but here my father comes.<br>A [[Double\|double]] blessing is a [[Double\|double]] grace;<br>Occasion smiles upon a second leave. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Yet here, [[Laertes]]? Aboard, aboard, for shame!<br>The [[Wind\|wind]] sits in the shoulder of your sail,<br>And you are stayed for. There—my blessing with thee,<br>And these few precepts in thy [[Memory\|memory]]<br>See thou character. Give thy thoughts no tongue,<br>Nor any unproportioned thought his act.<br>Be thou familiar but by no means vulgar.<br>The friends thou hast, and their adoption tried,<br>Grapple them to thy [[Soul\|soul]] with hoops of steel,<br>But do not dull thy palm with entertainment<br>Of each new-hatched unfledged comrade. Beware<br>Of entrance to a quarrel, but being in,<br>Bear’t that th’opposèd may beware of thee.<br>Give every man thine ear but few thy voice.<br>Take each man’s censure, but reserve thy judgement.<br>Costly thy habit as thy purse can buy,<br>But not expressed in fancy; rich not gaudy;<br>For the apparel oft proclaims the man,<br>And they in France of the best rank and station<br>Are of all most select and generous chief in that.<br>Neither a borrower nor a lender be,<br>For loan oft loses both itself and friend,<br>And borrowing dulls the edge of husbandry.<br>This above all—to thine own [[Self\|self]] be true,<br>And it must follow, as the [[Night\|night]] the day,<br>Thou canst not then be false to any man.<br>Farewell—my blessing season this in thee. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Most humbly do I take my leave, my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | The [[Time\|time]] invites you. Go; your servants tend. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Farewell, [[Ophelia]], and remember [[Well\|well]]<br>What I have said to you. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | ’Tis in my [[Memory\|memory]] locked,<br>And you yourself shall keep the key of it. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What is’t, [[Ophelia]], he hath said to you? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | So please you, something touching the Lord [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Marry, [[Well\|well]] bethought.<br>‘Tis told me he hath very oft of late<br>Given private [[Time\|time]] to you, and you yourself<br>Have of your audience been most free and bounteous.<br>If it be so—as so ’tis put on me,<br>And that in way of caution—I must tell you<br>You do not understand yourself so clearly<br>As it behoves my daughter and your [[Honour\|honour]].<br>What is between you? Give me up the [[Truth\|truth]]. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | He hath, my lord, of late made many tenders<br>Of his affection to me. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Affection, pooh! You speak like a green girl<br>Unsifted in such perilous circumstance.<br>Do you believe his ‘tenders’ as you call them? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I do not know, my lord, what I should think. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Marry, I’ll teach you: think yourself a baby<br>That you have ta’en his tenders for true pay,<br>Which are not sterling. Tender yourself more dearly,<br>Or—not to crack the [[Wind\|wind]] of the poor phrase,<br>Running it thus—you’ll tender me a [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My lord, he hath importuned me with [[Love\|love]]<br>In honourable fashion— |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Ay, fashion you may call it. Go to, go to. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | And hath given countenance to his speech, my lord,<br>With all the vows of heaven. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Ay, springes to catch woodcocks. I do know<br>When the [[Blood\|blood]] burns how prodigal the [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Lends the tongue vows. These blazes, daughter,<br>Giving more light than heat, extinct in both<br>Even in their promise as it is a-making,<br>You must not take for [[Fire\|fire]]. From this [[Time\|time]], daughter,<br>Be somewhat scanter of your maiden presence.<br>Set your entreatments at a higher rate<br>Than a command to parley. For Lord [[Hamlet]],<br>Believe so much in him, that he is young,<br>And with a larger tether may he walk<br>Than may be given you. In few, [[Ophelia]],<br>Do not believe his vows, for they are brokers,<br>Not of the dye which their investments show,<br>But mere imploratators of unholy suits,<br>Breathing like sanctified and pious bawds<br>The better to beguile. This is for all—<br>I would not, in plain terms, from this [[Time\|time]] forth<br>Have you so slander any moment leisure<br>As to give words or talk with the Lord [[Hamlet]].<br>Look to’t, I charge you. Come your ways. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I shall obey, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]], [[Horatio]], and Marcellus |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The air bites shrewdly, it is very cold. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | It is a nipping and an eager air. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What hour now? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I think it lacks of twelve. |
| MARCELLUS | No, it is struck. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Indeed? I heard it not. Then it draws near the season<br>Wherein the spirit held his wont to walk. |
| *(didascalia)* | A flourish of trumpets, and two pieces of ordnance goes off |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What does this mean, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The [[King]] doth wake tonight and takes his rouse,<br>Keeps wassail, and the swagg’ring upspring reels,<br>And as he drains his draughts of Rhenish down<br>The kettle-drum and trumpet thus bray out<br>The triumph of his pledge. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Is it a custom? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, marry is’t,<br>And to my mind, though I am native here<br>And to the manner born, it is a custom<br>More honoured in the breach than the observance. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]], as before |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Look, my lord, it comes. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Angels and ministers of grace defend us!<br>Be thou a spirit of health or goblin damned,<br>Bring with thee airs from heaven or blasts from hell,<br>Be thy intents wicked or charitable,<br>Thou com’st in such a questionable shape<br>That I will speak to thee. I’ll call thee [[Hamlet]],<br>[[King]], father, royal Dane. O answer me!<br>Let me not burst in ignorance, but tell<br>Why thy canonized bones, hearsèd in [[Death\|death]],<br>Have burst their cerements, why the sepulchre<br>Wherein we saw thee quietly enurned<br>Hath oped his ponderous and marble jaws<br>To cast thee up again. What may this mean,<br>That thou, dead corpse, again in complete steel,<br>Revisitst thus the glimpses of the [[Moon\|moon]],<br>Making [[Night\|night]] hideous, and we fools of [[Nature\|nature]]<br>So horridly to shake our disposition<br>With thoughts beyond the reaches of our souls?<br>Say, why is this? Wherefore? What should we do?<br>The [[Ghost]] beckons [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | It beckons you to go away with it<br>As if it some impartment did [[Desire\|desire]]<br>To you [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| MARCELLUS | (to [[Hamlet]]) Look with what courteous action<br>It wafts you to a more removed ground.<br>But do not go with it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to [[Hamlet]])<br>No, by no means. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It will not speak. Then will I follow it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Do not, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, what should be the [[Fear\|fear]]?<br>I do not set my life at a pin’s fee,<br>And for my [[Soul\|soul]], what can it do to that,<br>Being a thing immortal as itself?<br>The [[Ghost]] beckons [[Hamlet]]<br>It waves me forth again. I’ll follow it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What if it tempt you toward the flood, my lord,<br>Or to the dreadful summit of the cliff<br>That beetles o’er his base into the sea,<br>And there assume some other horrible form<br>Which might deprive your sovereignty of reason<br>And draw you into [[Madness\|madness]]? Think of it.<br>The [[Ghost]] beckons [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It wafts me still. (To the [[Ghost]]) Go on, I’ll follow thee. |
| MARCELLUS | You shall not go, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Hold off your hand. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Be ruled. You shall not go. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | My fate cries out,<br>And makes each petty artere in this [[Body\|body]]<br>As hardy as the Nemean [[Lion\|lion]]’s nerve.<br>The [[Ghost]] beckons [[Hamlet]]<br>Still am I called. Unhand me, gentlemen.<br>By heav’n, I’ll make a [[Ghost\|ghost]] of him that lets me.<br>I say, away! (To the [[Ghost]]) Go on, I’ll follow thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt the [[Ghost]] and [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | He waxes desperate with [[Imagination\|imagination]]. |
| MARCELLUS | Let’s follow.’Tis not fit thus to obey him. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Have after. To what issue will this come? |
| MARCELLUS | Something is rotten in the state of [[Denmark]]. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Heaven will direct it. |
| MARCELLUS | Nay, let’s follow him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]], and Prince [[Hamlet]] following |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Whither wilt thou lead me? Speak. I’ll go no further. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Mark me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I will. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | My hour is almost come<br>When I to sulph’rous and tormenting flames<br>Must render up myself. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Alas, poor [[Ghost\|ghost]]! |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Pity me not, but lend thy serious hearing<br>To what I shall unfold. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | So art thou to [[Revenge\|revenge]] when thou shalt hear. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What? |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | I am thy father’s spirit,<br>Doomed for a certain term to walk the [[Night\|night]],<br>And for the day confined to fast in fires<br>Till the foul crimes done in my days of [[Nature\|nature]]<br>Are burnt and purged away. But that I am forbid<br>To tell the [[Secrets\|secrets]] of my [[Prison\|prison]]-[[house]]<br>I could a tale unfold whose lightest word<br>Would harrow up thy [[Soul\|soul]], freeze thy young [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Make thy two eyes like [[Stars\|stars]] start from their spheres,<br>Thy knotty and combined locks to part,<br>And each particular hair to stand on end<br>Like quills upon the fretful porcupine.<br>But this eternal blazon must not be<br>To ears of flesh and [[Blood\|blood]]. List, [[Hamlet]], list, O list!<br>If thou didst ever thy dear father [[Love\|love]]— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O God! |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | [[Revenge]] his foul and most unnatural murder. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Murder? |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Murder most foul, as in the best it is,<br>But this most foul, strange, and unnatural. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Haste, haste me to know it, that with wings as swift<br>As meditation or the thoughts of [[Love\|love]]<br>May sweep to my [[Revenge\|revenge]]. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | I find thee apt,<br>And duller shouldst thou be than the fat weed<br>That rots itself in ease on Lethe wharf<br>Wouldst thou not stir in this. Now, [[Hamlet]], hear.<br>’Tis given out that, sleeping in mine orchard,<br>A serpent stung me. So the whole ear of [[Denmark]]<br>Is by a forged process of my [[Death\|death]]<br>Rankly abused. But know, thou noble youth,<br>The serpent that did sting thy father’s life<br>Now wears his [[Crown\|crown]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O my prophetic [[Soul\|soul]]! Mine uncle? |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Ay, that incestuous, that adulterate beast,<br>With witchcraft of his wit, with traitorous gifts—<br>O wicked wit and gifts, that have the power<br>So to seduce!—won to his shameful lust<br>The will of my most seeming-virtuous [[Queen\|queen]].<br>O [[Hamlet]], what a falling off was there!—<br>From me, whose [[Love\|love]] was of that dignity<br>That it went hand-in-hand even with the vow<br>I made to her in [[Marriage\|marriage]], and to decline<br>Upon a wretch whose natural gifts were poor<br>To those of mine.<br>But virtue, as it never will be moved,<br>Though lewdness [[Court\|court]] it in a shape of heaven,<br>So lust, though to a radiant angel linked,<br>Will sate itself in a celestial bed,<br>And prey on garbage.<br>But soft, methinks I scent the morning’s air.<br>Brief let me be. Sleeping within mine orchard,<br>My custom always in the afternoon,<br>Upon my secure hour thy uncle stole<br>With juice of cursed hebenon in a vial,<br>And in the porches of mine ears did pour<br>The leperous distilment, whose effect<br>Holds such an enmity with [[Blood\|blood]] of man<br>That swift as quicksilver it courses through<br>The natural gates and alleys of the [[Body\|body]],<br>And with a sudden vigour it doth posset<br>And curd, like eager droppings into milk,<br>The thin and wholesome [[Blood\|blood]]. So did it mine;<br>And a most instant tetter barked about,<br>Most lazar-like, with vile and loathsome crust,<br>All my smooth [[Body\|body]].<br>Thus was I, sleeping, by a brother’s hand<br>Of life, of [[Crown\|crown]], of [[Queen\|queen]] at once dispatched,<br>Cut off even in the blossoms of my sin,<br>Unhouseled, dis-appointed, unaneled,<br>No reck’ning made, but sent to my account<br>With all my imperfections on my head.<br>O horrible, O horrible, most horrible!<br>If thou hast [[Nature\|nature]] in thee, bear it not.<br>Let not the royal bed of [[Denmark]] be<br>A couch for luxury and damned incest.<br>But howsoever thou pursuest this act,<br>Taint not thy mind, nor let thy [[Soul\|soul]] contrive<br>Against thy mother aught. Leave her to heaven,<br>And to those thorns that in her bosom lodge<br>To prick and sting her. Fare thee [[Well\|well]] at once.<br>The glow-worm shows the matin to be near,<br>And gins to pale his uneffectual [[Fire\|fire]].<br>Adieu, adieu, [[Hamlet]]. Remember me. Exit |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O all you host of heaven! Oearth! What else?<br>And shall I couple hell? O fie! Hold, hold, my heart,<br>And you, my sinews, grow not instant old,<br>But bear me stiffly up. Remember thee?<br>Ay, thou poor [[Ghost\|ghost]], while [[Memory\|memory]] holds a seat<br>In this distracted globe. Remember thee?<br>Yea, from the table of my [[Memory\|memory]]<br>I’ll wipe away all trivial fond records,<br>All saws of books, all forms, all pressures past,<br>That youth and observation copied there,<br>And thy commandment all [[Alone\|alone]] shall live |
| *(didascalia)* | Within the book and volume of my brain |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Unmixed with baser matter. Yes, yes, by heaven.<br>O most pernicious woman!<br>O [[Villain\|villain]], [[Villain\|villain]], smiling, damned [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>My tables,<br>My tables—meet it is I set it down<br>That one may smile and smile and be a [[Villain\|villain]].<br>At least I’m sure it may be so in [[Denmark]].<br>He writes<br>So, uncle, there you are. Now to my word:<br>It is ‘Adieu, adieu, remember me’.<br>I have sworn’t. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | and MARCELLUS (within) My lord, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]] and Marcellus |
| MARCELLUS | (calling) Lord [[Hamlet]]! 115 |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Heaven secure him. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | So be it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (calling) Illo, ho, ho, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Hillo, ho, ho, boy; come, bird, come. |
| MARCELLUS | How is’t, my noble lord? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) What news, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O wonderful! |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Good my lord, tell it. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, you’ll reveal it. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Not I, my lord, by heaven. |
| MARCELLUS | Nor I, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How say you then, would heart of man once think it?<br>But you’ll be secret? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | and MARCELLUS Ay, by heav’n, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | There’s ne’er a [[Villain\|villain]] dwelling in all [[Denmark]]<br>But he’s an arrant knave. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | There needs no [[Ghost\|ghost]], my lord, come from the [[Grave\|grave]]<br>To tell us this. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, right, you are i’th’ right,<br>And so without more circumstance at all<br>I hold it fit that we shake hands and part,<br>You as your business and desires shall point you—<br>For every man has business and [[Desire\|desire]],<br>Such as it is—and for mine own poor part,<br>Look you, I’ll go pray. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | These are but wild and whirling words, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I’m sorry they offend you, heartily,<br>Yes, [[Faith\|faith]], heartily. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | There’s no offence, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Yes, by Saint Patrick, but there is, [[Horatio]],<br>And much offence, too. Touching this vision here,<br>It is an honest [[Ghost\|ghost]], that let me tell you.<br>For your [[Desire\|desire]] to know what is between us,<br>O’ermaster’t as you may. And now, good friends,<br>As you are friends, scholars, and soldiers,<br>Give me one poor request. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What is’t, my lord? We will. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Never make known what you have seen tonight. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | and MARCELLUS<br>My lord, we will not. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, but swear’t. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | In [[Faith\|faith]], my lord, not I. |
| MARCELLUS | Nor I, my lord, in [[Faith\|faith]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Upon my [[Sword\|sword]]. |
| MARCELLUS | We have sworn, my lord, already. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Indeed, upon my [[Sword\|sword]], indeed.<br>The [[Ghost]] cries under the stage |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Swear. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ah ha, boy, sayst thou so? Art thou there, truepenny?—<br>Come on. You hear this fellow in the cellarage.<br>Consent to swear. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Propose the oath, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Never to speak of this that you have seen,<br>Swear by my [[Sword\|sword]]. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | (under the stage) Swear. |
| *(didascalia)* | They swear |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Hic et ubique? Then we’ll shift our ground.—<br>Come hither, gentlemen,<br>And lay your hands again upon my [[Sword\|sword]].<br>Never to speak of this that you have heard,<br>Swear by my [[Sword\|sword]]. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | (under the stage) Swear. |
| *(didascalia)* | They swear |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[Well]] said, old mole. Canst work i’th’ earth so fast?<br>A worthy pioneer.—Once more remove, good friends. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | O day and [[Night\|night]], but this is wondrous strange! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And therefore as a stranger give it welcome.<br>There are more things in heaven and earth, [[Horatio]],<br>Than are dreamt of in our [[philosophy]]. But come,<br>Here as before, never, so help you [[Mercy\|mercy]],<br>How strange or odd soe‘er I bear myself—<br>As I perchance hereafter shall think meet<br>To put an antic disposition on—<br>That you at such [[Time\|time]] seeing me never shall,<br>With arms encumbered thus, or this headshake,<br>Or by pronouncing of some doubtful phrase<br>As ‘[[Well]], we know’ or ‘We could an if we would’,<br>Or ‘If we list to speak’, or ‘There be, an if they might’,<br>Or such ambiguous giving out, to note<br>That you know aught of me—this not to do,<br>So grace and [[Mercy\|mercy]] at your most need help you, swear. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | (under the stage) Swear. |
| *(didascalia)* | They swear |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Rest, rest, perturbed spirit.—So, gentlemen,<br>With all my [[Love\|love]] I do commend me to you,<br>And what so poor a man as [[Hamlet]] is<br>May do t’express his [[Love\|love]] and friending to you,<br>God willing, shall not lack. Let us go in together,<br>And still your fingers on your lips, I pray.<br>The [[Time\|time]] is out of joint. O cursed spite<br>That ever I was born to set it right!<br>Nay, come, let’s go together. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter old [[Polonius]] with his man Reynaldo |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Give him this money and these notes, Reynaldo. |
| REYNALDO | I will, my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | You shall do marv’lous wisely, good Reynaldo,<br>Before you visit him to make enquire<br>Of his behaviour. |
| REYNALDO | My lord, I did intend it. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Marry, [[Well\|well]] said, very [[Well\|well]] said. Look you, sir,<br>Enquire me first what Danskers are in [[Paris]],<br>And how, and who, what means, and where they keep,<br>What company, at what expense; and finding<br>By this encompassment and drift of question<br>That they do know my son, come you more nearer<br>Than your particular demands will touch it.<br>Take you, as ‘twere, some distant knowledge of him,<br>As thus: ‘I know his father and his friends,<br>And in part him’—do you mark this, Reynaldo? |
| REYNALDO | Ay, very [[Well\|well]], my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | ‘And in part him, but’, you may say, ‘not [[Well\|well]],<br>But if’t be he I mean, he’s very wild,<br>Addicted so and so’; and there put on him<br>What forgeries you please—marry, none so rank<br>As may dishonour him, take heed of that—<br>But, sir, such wanton, wild, and usual slips<br>As are companions noted and most known<br>To youth and liberty. |
| REYNALDO | As gaming, my lord? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Ay, or drinking, fencing, swearing,<br>Quarrelling, drabbing—you may go so far. |
| REYNALDO | My lord, that would dishonour him. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | [[Faith]], no, as you may season it in the charge.<br>You must not put another scandal on him,<br>That he is open to incontinency.<br>That’s not my meaning—but breathe his faults so<br>quaintly<br>That they may seem the taints of liberty,<br>The flash and outbreak of a fiery mind,<br>A savageness in unreclaimed [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Of general assault. |
| REYNALDO | But, my good lord— |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Wherefore should you do this? |
| REYNALDO | Ay, my lord.<br>I would know that. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Marry, sir, here’s my drift,<br>And I believe it is a fetch of warrant:<br>You laying these slight sullies on my son,<br>As ‘twere a thing a little soiled i’th’ working,<br>Mark you, your party in converse, him you would<br>sound,<br>Having ever seen in the prenominate crimes<br>The youth you breathe of guilty, be assured<br>He closes with you in this consequence:<br>‘Good sir’, or so, or ‘friend’, or ‘gentleman’,<br>According to the phrase and the addition<br>Of man and country. |
| REYNALDO | Very good, my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | And then, sir, does a this—a does—<br>what was I about to say? By the mass, I was about to<br>say something. Where did I leave? |
| REYNALDO | At ‘closes in the consequence’, at ‘friend,<br>Or so’, and ‘gentleman’. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | At ‘closes in the consequence’—ay, marry,<br>He closes with you thus: ‘I know the gentleman,<br>I saw him yesterday’—or t‘other day,<br>Or then, or then—’with such and such, and, as you<br>say,<br>There was a gaming, there o‘ertook in ’s rouse,<br>There falling out at tennis’, or perchance<br>‘I saw him enter such a [[house]] of sale’,<br>Videlicet, a brothel, or so forth. See you now,<br>Your bait of falsehood takes this carp of [[Truth\|truth]];<br>And thus do we of wisdom and of reach<br>With windlasses and with assays of bias<br>By indirections find directions out.<br>So, by my former lecture and advice,<br>Shall you my son. You have me, have you not? |
| REYNALDO | My lord, I have. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | God b’wi’ ye. Fare ye [[Well\|well]]. |
| REYNALDO | Good my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Observe his inclination in yourself. |
| REYNALDO | I shall, my lord. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | And let him ply his music. |
| REYNALDO | [[Well]], my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Ophelia]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Reynaldo |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | How now, [[Ophelia]], what’s the matter? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Alas, my lord, I have been so affrighted. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | With what, i’th’ name of God? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My lord, as I was sewing in my chamber,<br>Lord [[Hamlet]], with his doublet all unbraced,<br>No hat upon his head, his stockings fouled,<br>Ungartered, and down-gyvèd to his ankle,<br>Pale as his shirt, his knees knocking each other,<br>And with a look so piteous in purport<br>As if he had been loosed out of hell<br>To speak of horrors, he comes before me. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Mad for thy [[Love\|love]]? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My lord, I do not know,<br>But truly I do [[Fear\|fear]] it. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What said he? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | He took me by the wrist and held me hard,<br>Then goes he to the length of all his arm,<br>And with his other hand thus o’er his brow<br>He falls to such perusal of my face<br>As a would draw it. Long stayed he so.<br>At last, a little shaking of mine arm,<br>And thrice his head thus waving up and down,<br>He raised a sigh so piteous and profound<br>That it did seem to shatter all his bulk<br>And end his being. That done, he lets me go,<br>And, with his head over his shoulder turned,<br>He seemed to find his way without his eyes,<br>For out o’ doors he went without their help,<br>And to the last bended their light on me. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Come, go with me. I will go seek the [[King]].<br>This is the very ecstasy of [[Love\|love]],<br>Whose violent property fordoes itself<br>And leads the will to desperate undertakings<br>As oft as any passion under heaven<br>That does afflict our natures. I am sorry—<br>What, have you given him any hard words of late? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | No, my good lord, but as you did command<br>I did repel his letters and denied<br>His access to me. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | That hath made him mad.<br>I am sorry that with better [[Speed\|speed]] and judgement<br>I had not quoted him. I feared he did but trifle<br>And meant to wreck thee. But beshrew my [[Jealousy\|jealousy]]!<br>By heaven, it is as proper to our age<br>To cast beyond ourselves in our opinions<br>As it is common for the younger sort<br>To lack discretion. Come, go we to the [[King]].<br>This must be known, which, being kept close, might<br>move<br>More grief to hide than hate to utter [[Love\|love]]. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 2.2 Flourish. Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] and [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]], [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]], with others |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Welcome, dear [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]].<br>Moreover that we much did long to see you,<br>The need we have to use you did provoke<br>Our hasty sending. Something have you heard<br>Of [[Hamlet]]’s transformation—so I call it,<br>Since not th‘exterior nor the inward man<br>Resembles that it was. What it should be,<br>More than his father’s [[Death\|death]], that thus hath put him<br>So much from th’understanding of himself,<br>I cannot deem of. I entreat you both<br>That, being of so young days brought up with him,<br>And since so neighboured to his youth and [[Humour\|humour]],<br>That you vouchsafe your rest here in our [[Court\|court]]<br>Some little [[Time\|time]], so by your companies<br>To draw him on to pleasures, and to gather,<br>So much as from occasions you may glean,<br>Whether aught to us unknown afflicts him thus<br>That, opened, lies within our remedy. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Good gentlemen, he hath much talked of you,<br>And sure I am two men there is not living<br>To whom he more adheres. If it will please you<br>To show us so much gentry and good will<br>As to expend your [[Time\|time]] with us a while<br>For the supply and profit of our [[Hope\|hope]],<br>Your visitation shall receive such thanks<br>As fits a [[King\|king]]’s remembrance. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Both your majesties<br>Might, by the sovereign power you have of us,<br>Put your dread pleasures more into command<br>Than to entreaty. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | But we both obey,<br>And here give up ourselves in the full bent<br>To lay our service freely at your feet<br>To be commanded. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Thanks, [[Rosencrantz]] and gentle [[Guildenstern]]. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Thanks, [[Guildenstern]] and gentle [[Rosencrantz]].<br>And I beseech you instantly to visit<br>My too-much changed son.—Go, some of ye,<br>And bring the gentlemen where [[Hamlet]] is. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Heavens make our presence and our practices<br>Pleasant and helpful to him. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Ay, amen! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] with others Enter [[Polonius]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Th’ambassadors from Norway, my good lord,<br>Are joyfully returned. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Thou still hast been the father of good news. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Have I, my lord? Assure you, my good liege,<br>I hold my duty, as I hold my [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Both to my God and to my gracious [[King]].<br>And I do think—or else this brain of mine<br>Hunts not the trail of policy so sure<br>As it hath used to do—that I have found<br>The very cause of [[Hamlet]]’s lunacy. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O speak of that, that I do long to hear! |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Give first admittance to th’ambassadors.<br>My news shall be the fruit to that great feast. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Thyself do grace to them, and bring them in. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Polonius]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | He tells me, my sweet [[Queen\|queen]], that he hath found<br>The head and source of all your son’s distemper. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I [[Doubt\|doubt]] it is no other but the main—<br>His father’s [[Death\|death]] and our o’er-hasty [[Marriage\|marriage]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Well]], we shall sift him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]], Valtemand, and Cornelius Welcome, my good friends. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Say, Valtemand, what from our brother Norway? |
| VALTEMAND | Most fair return of greetings and desires.<br>Upon our first he sent out to suppress<br>His nephew’s levies, which to him appeared<br>To be a preparation ‘gainst the Polack;<br>But better looked into, he truly found<br>It was against your highness; whereat grieved<br>That so his sickness, age, and impotence<br>Was falsely borne in hand, sends out arrests<br>On Fortinbras, which he, in brief, obeys,<br>Receives rebuke from Norway, and, in fine,<br>Makes vow before his uncle never more<br>To give th’[[Essay\|essay]] of arms against your majesty;<br>Whereon old Norway, overcome with joy,<br>Gives him three thousand crowns in annual fee<br>And his commission to employ those soldiers<br>So levied as before, against the Polack,<br>With an entreaty herein further shown,<br>He gives a letter to [[Claudius]]<br><br>That it might please you to give quiet pass<br>Through your dominions for his enterprise<br>On such regards of safety and allowance<br>As therein are set down. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | It likes us [[Well\|well]],<br>And at our more considered [[Time\|time]] we’ll read,<br>Answer, and think upon this business.<br>Meantime we thank you for your [[Well\|well]]-took labour.<br>Go to your rest; at [[Night\|night]] we’ll feast together.<br>Most welcome [[Home\|home]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Valtemand and Cornelius |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | This business is very [[Well\|well]] ended.<br>My liege, and madam, to expostulate<br>What majesty should be, what duty is,<br>Why day is day, [[Night\|night]] [[Night\|night]], and [[Time\|time]] is [[Time\|time]],<br>Were nothing but to waste [[Night\|night]], day, and [[Time\|time]].<br>Therefore, since brevity is the [[Soul\|soul]] of wit,<br>And tediousness the limbs and outward flourishes,<br>I will be brief. Your noble son is mad—<br>‘Mad’ call I it, for to define true [[Madness\|madness]],<br>What is’t but to be nothing else but mad?<br>But let that go. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | More matter with less art. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Madam, I swear I use no art at all.<br>That he is mad, ‘tis true; ’tis true ‘tis pity,<br>And pity ’tis ‘tis true—a foolish figure,<br>But farewell it, for I will use no art.<br>Mad let us grant him, then; and now remains<br>That we find out the cause of this effect—<br>Or rather say ’the cause of this defect‘,<br>For this effect defective comes by cause.<br>Thus it remains, and the remainder thus.<br>Perpend.<br>I have a daughter—have whilst she is mine—<br>Who in her duty and obedience, mark,<br>Hath given me this. Now gather and surmise.<br>He reads a letter<br><br>’To the celestial and my [[Soul\|soul]]’s idol, the most beautified<br>[[Ophelia]]‘—that’s an ill phrase, a vile phrase, ‘beautified’<br>is a vile phrase. But you shall hear—‘these in her<br>excellent white bosom, these’. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Came this from [[Hamlet]] to her? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Good madam, stay a while. I will be faithful.<br>‘[[Doubt]] thou the [[Stars\|stars]] are [[Fire\|fire]],<br>[[Doubt]] that the sun doth move,<br>[[Doubt]] [[Truth\|truth]] to be a liar,<br>But never [[Doubt\|doubt]] I [[Love\|love]].<br>O dear [[Ophelia]], I am ill at these numbers. I have not<br>art to reckon my groans. But that I [[Love\|love]] thee best, O<br>most best, believe it. Adieu.<br>Thine evermore, most dear lady, whilst this<br>[[Machine\|machine]] is to him,<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>This in obedience hath my daughter showed me,<br>And more above hath his solicitings,<br>As they fell out by [[Time\|time]], by means, and place,<br>All given to mine ear. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | But how hath she<br>Received his [[Love\|love]]? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What do you think of me? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | As of a man faithful and honourable. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I would fain prove so. But what might you think,<br>When I had seen this hot [[Love\|love]] on the wing,<br>As I perceived it—I must tell you that—<br>Before my daughter told me, what might you,<br>Or my dear majesty your [[Queen\|queen]] here, think,<br>If I had played the desk or table-book,<br>Or given my heart a winking mute and dumb,<br>Or looked upon this [[Love\|love]] with idle sight—<br>What might you think? No, I went round to work,<br>And my young mistress thus I did bespeak:<br>‘Lord [[Hamlet]] is a prince out of thy star.<br>This must not be’. And then I precepts gave her,<br>That she should lock herself from his resort,<br>Admit no messengers, receive no tokens;<br>Which done, she took the fruits of my advice,<br>And he, repulsèd-a short tale to make—<br>Fell into a sadness, then into a fast,<br>Thence to a watch, thence into a weakness,<br>Thence to a lightness, and, by this declension,<br>Into the [[Madness\|madness]] wherein now he raves,<br>And all we wail for. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) Do you think ’tis this? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | It may be; very likely. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Hath there been such a [[Time\|time]]—I’d fain know that—<br>That I have positively said ‘ ’Tis so’<br>When it proved otherwise? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Not that I know. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (touching his head, then his shoulder)<br>Take this from this if this be otherwise.<br>If circumstances lead me I will find<br>Where [[Truth\|truth]] is hid, though it were hid indeed |
| *(didascalia)* | Within the centre. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How may we try it further? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | You know sometimes he walks four hours together<br>Here in the lobby. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | So he does indeed. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | At such a [[Time\|time]] I’ll loose my daughter to him.<br>(To [[Claudius]]) Be you and I behind an arras then.<br>Mark the encounter. If he [[Love\|love]] her not,<br>And be not from his reason fall’n thereon,<br>Let me be no assistant for a state,<br>But keep a farm and carters. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | We will try it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]], madly attired, reading on a book |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | But look where sadly the poor wretch comes reading. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Away, I do beseech you both, away.<br>I’ll board him presently. O give me leave. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Claudius]] and [[Gertrude]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | How does my good Lord [[Hamlet]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[Well]], God-‘a’-[[Mercy\|mercy]]. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Do you know me, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Excellent, excellent [[Well\|well]]. You’re a fishmonger. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Not I, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then I would you were so honest a man. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Honest, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, sir. To be honest, as this world goes, is to be one man picked out of ten thousand. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | That’s very true, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | For if the sun breed maggots in a dead dog, being a good kissing carrion—have you a daughter? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I have, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Let her not walk i’th’ sun. Conception is a blessing, but not as your daughter may conceive. Friend, look to’t. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (aside) How say you by that? Still harping on my daughter. Yet he knew me not at first—a said I was a fishmonger. A is far gone, far gone, and truly, in my youth I suffered much extremity for [[Love\|love]], very near this. I’ll speak to him again.—What do you read, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Words, words, words. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What is the matter, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Between who? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I mean the matter you read, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Slanders, sir; for the satirical slave says here that old men have grey beards, that their faces are wrinkled, their eyes purging thick amber, or plum-tree gum, and that they have a plentiful lack of wit, together with most weak hams. All which, sir, though I most powerfully and potently believe, yet I hold it not honesty to have it thus set down; for you yourself, sir, should be old as I am—if, like a crab, you could go backward. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (aside) Though this be [[Madness\|madness]], yet there is method in’t.—Will you walk out of the air, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Into my [[Grave\|grave]]. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Indeed, that is out o’th’ air. (Aside) How pregnant sometimes his replies are! A happiness that often [[Madness\|madness]] hits on, which reason and [[Sanity\|sanity]] could not so prosperously be delivered of. I will leave him, and suddenly contrive the means of meeting between him and my daughter.—My lord, I will take my leave of you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | You cannot, sir, take from me anything that I will more willingly part withal—except my life, my life, my life. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (going) Fare you [[Well\|well]], my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] These tedious old fools! Enter [[Guildenstern]] and [[Rosencrantz]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | You go to seek the Lord [[Hamlet]]. There he is. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | God save you, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] to [[Polonius]] Mine honoured lord. Exit [[Polonius]] |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) My most dear lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | My ex’llent good friends. How dost thou, [[Guildenstern]]? Ah, [[Rosencrantz]]—good lads, how do ye both? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | As the indifferent children of the earth. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Happy in that we are not over-happy,<br>On Fortune’s cap we are not the very button. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nor the soles of her shoe? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Neither, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then you live about her waist, or in the middle of her favour? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | [[Faith]], her privates we. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | In the secret parts of Fortune? O, most true, she is a strumpet. What’s the news? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | None, my lord, but that the world’s grown honest. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then is doomsday near. But your news is not true. Let me question more in particular. What have you, my good friends, deserved at the hands of Fortune that she sends you to [[Prison\|prison]] hither? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | [[Prison]], my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[Denmark]]’s a [[Prison\|prison]]. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Then is the world one. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A goodly one, in which there are many confines, wards, and dungeons, [[Denmark]] being one o’th’ worst. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | We think not so, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, then ’tis none to you, for there is nothing either good or bad but thinking makes it so. To me it is a [[Prison\|prison]]. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Why, then your [[Ambition\|ambition]] makes it one; ’tis too narrow for your mind. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O God, I could be bounded in a nutshell and count myself a [[King\|king]] of infinite space, were it not that I have bad [[Dreams\|dreams]]. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Which [[Dreams\|dreams]] indeed are [[Ambition\|ambition]]; for the very substance of the ambitious is merely the [[Shadow\|shadow]] of [[A Dream\|a dream]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[A Dream\|A dream]] itself is but a [[Shadow\|shadow]]. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Truly, and I hold [[Ambition\|ambition]] of so airy and light a quality that it is but a [[Shadow\|shadow]]’s [[Shadow\|shadow]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then are our beggars bodies, and our monarchs and outstretched heroes the beggars’ shadows. Shall we to th’ [[Court\|court]]? For, by my fay, I cannot reason. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | and [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] We’ll wait upon you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No such matter. I will not sort you with the rest of my servants, for, to speak to you like an honest man, I am most dreadfully attended. But in the beaten way of [[Friendship\|friendship]], what make you at Elsinore? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | To visit you, my lord, no other occasion. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Beggar that I am, I am even poor in thanks, but I thank you; and sure, dear friends, my thanks are too dear a halfpenny. Were you not sent for? Is it your own inclining? Is it a free visitation? Come, deal justly with me. Come, come. Nay, speak. 278 |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | What should we say, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, anything—but to th’ purpose. You were sent for, and there is a kind of confession in your looks which your modesties have not craft enough to colour. I know the good [[King]] and [[Queen]] have sent for you. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | To what end, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That you must teach me. But let me conjure you by the rights of our fellowship, by the consonancy of our youth, by the obligation of our ever-preserved [[Love\|love]], and by what more dear a better proposer could charge you withal, be even and direct with me whether you were sent for or no. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | (to [[Guildenstern]]) What say you? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay then, I have an eye of you—if you [[Love\|love]] me, hold not off. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | My lord, we were sent for. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I will tell you why. So shall my anticipation prevent your discovery, and your secrecy to the [[King]] and [[Queen]] moult no feather. I have of late—but wherefore I know not—tost all my mirth, forgone all custom of exercise; and indeed it goes so heavily with my disposition that this goodly frame, the earth, seems to me a sterile promontory. This most excellent canopy the air, look you, this brave o’erhanging, this majestical roof fretted with golden [[Fire\|fire]]—why, it appears no other thing to me than a foul and pestilent congregation of vapours. What a piece of work is a man! How noble in reason, how infinite in faculty, in form and moving how express and admirable, in action how like an angel, in apprehension how like a god—the [[Beauty\|beauty]] of the world, the paragon of [[Animals\|animals]]! And yet to me what is this quintessence of [[Dust\|dust]]? Man delights not me—no, nor woman neither, though by your smiling you seem to say so. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | My lord, there was no such stuff in my thoughts. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why did you laugh, then, when I said ‘Man delights not me’? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | To think, my lord, if you delight not in man what lenten entertainment the players shall receive from you. We coted them on the way, and hither are they coming to offer you service. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | He that plays the [[King]] shall be welcome; his majesty shall have tribute of me. The adventurous Knight shall use his foil and target, the [[Lover]] shall not sigh gratis, the Humorous Man shall end his part in peace, the Clown shall make those laugh whose lungs are tickled o’th’ sear, and the Lady shall say her mind freely, or the [[Blank Verse\|blank verse]] shall halt for’t. What players are they? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Even those you were wont to take delight in, the tragedians of the [[City\|city]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How chances it they travel? Their residence both in reputation and profit was better both ways. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | I think their inhibition comes by the means of the late innovation. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do they hold the same estimation they did when I was in the [[City\|city]]? Are they so followed? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | No, indeed, they are not. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How comes it? Do they grow rusty? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Nay, their endeavour keeps in the wonted pace. But there is, sir, an eyrie of children, little eyases, that cry out on the top of question and are most tyrannically clapped for’t. These are now the fashion, and so berattle the common stages—so they call them—that many wearing rapiers are afraid of goose-quills, and dare scarce come thither. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What, are they children? Who maintains ’em? How are they escoted? Will they pursue the quality no longer than they can sing? Will they not say afterwards, if they should grow themselves to common players—as it is like most will, if their means are not better—their writers do them wrong to make them exclaim against their own succession? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | [[Faith]], there has been much to-do on both sides, and the nation holds it no sin to tarre them to controversy. There was for a while no money bid for argument unless the poet and the player went to cuffs in the question. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Is’t possible? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | O, there has been much throwing about of brains. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do the boys carry it away? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Ay, that they do, my lord, Hercules and his load too. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It is not strange; for mine uncle is [[King]] of [[Denmark]], and those that would make mows at him while my father lived give twenty, forty, an hundred ducats apiece for his picture in little. ’Sblood, there is something in this more than natural, if [[philosophy]] could find it out. |
| *(didascalia)* | A flourish for the Players |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | There are the players. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Gentlemen, you are welcome to Elsinore. Your hands, come. Th’appurtenance of welcome is fashion and ceremony. Let me comply with you in the garb, lest my extent to the players—which, I tell you, must show fairly outward—shoutd more appear like entertainment than yours. |
| *(didascalia)* | He shakes hands with them |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | You are welcome. But my uncle-father and aunt-mother are deceived. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | In what, my dear lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am but mad north-north-west; when the [[Wind\|wind]] is southerly, I know a hawk from a handsaw. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | [[Well]] be with you, gentlemen. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside) Hark you, [[Guildenstern]], and you too—at each ear a hearer—that great baby you see there is not yet out of his swathing-clouts. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | (aside) Haply he’s the second [[Time\|time]] come to them, for they say an old man is twice a [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside) I will prophesy he comes to tell me of the players. Mark it.—You say right, sir, for o’ Monday morning, ’twas so indeed. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | My lord, I have news to tell you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | My lord, I have news to tell you. When Roscius was an actor in Rome— |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | The actors are come hither, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | BUZZ, buzz. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Upon mine [[Honour\|honour]]— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then came each actor on his ass. |
