| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | The best actors in the world, either for [[Tragedy\|tragedy]], [[Comedy\|comedy]], [[history]], [[Pastoral\|pastoral]], pastorical-comical, historical-[[Pastoral\|pastoral]], tragical-historical, tragical-comical-historical-[[Pastoral\|pastoral]], scene individable or poem unlimited. Seneca cannot be too heavy, nor Plautus too light. For the law of writ and the liberty, these are the only men. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O Jephthah, judge of Israel, what a treasure hadst thou! |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What a treasure had he, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why,<br>‘One fair daughter and no more,<br>The which he loved passing [[Well\|well]]’. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (aside) Still on my daughter. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Am I not i’th’ right, old Jephthah? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | If you call me Jephthah, my lord, I have a daughter that I [[Love\|love]] passing [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, that follows not. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | What follows then, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why<br>‘As by lot<br>God wot’,<br>and then you know<br>‘It came to pass<br>As most like it was’—<br>the first row of the pious chanson will show you more, for look where my abridgements come. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter four or five Players |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | You’re welcome, masters, welcome all.—Iam glad to see thee [[Well\|well]].—Welcome, good friends.—O, my old friend! Thy face is valanced since I saw thee last. Com‘st thou to beard me in [[Denmark]]?—What, my young lady and mistress. By’r Lady, your ladyship is nearer heaven than when I saw you last by the altitude of a chopine. Pray God your voice, like a piece of uncurrent gold, be not cracked within the ring.—Masters, you are all welcome. We’ll e‘en to’t like French falc’ners, fly at anything we see. We’ll have a speech straight. Come, give us a taste of your quality. Come, a passionate speech. |
| FIRST PLAYER | What speech, my good lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I heard thee speak me a speech once, but it was never acted, or, if it was, not above once; for the play, I remember, pleased not the million. ‘Twas caviare to the general. But it was—as I received it, and others whose judgements in such matters cried in the top of mine—an excellent play, [[Well\|well]] digested in the scenes, set down with as much modesty as cunning. I remember one said there was no sallets in the lines to make the matter savoury, nor no matter in the phrase that might indict the author of affectation, but called it an honest method, as wholesome as sweet, and by very much more handsome than fine. One speech in it I chiefly loved, ’twas Aeneas’ tale to Dido, and thereabout of it especially where he speaks of Priam’s slaughter. If it live in your [[Memory\|memory]], begin at this line—let me see, let me see: ‘The rugged Pyrrhus, like th’Hyrcanian beast‘—’tis not so. It begins with Pyrrhus—‘The rugged Pyrrhus, he whose sable arms, Black as his purpose, did the [[Night\|night]] resemble When he lay couched in the ominous [[Horse\|horse]], Hath now this dread and black complexion smeared With heraldry more dismal. Head to foot Now is he total gules, horridly tricked With [[Blood\|blood]] of fathers, mothers, daughters, sons, Baked and impasted with the parching streets, That lend a tyrranous and damned light To their vile murders. Roasted in wrath and [[Fire\|fire]], And thus o’er-sizèd with coagulate gore, With eyes like carbuncles the hellish Pyrrhus Old grandsire Priam seeks’ So, proceed you. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Fore God, my lord, [[Well\|well]] spoken, with good accent and good discretion. |
| FIRST PLAYER | ‘Anon he finds him,<br>Striking too short at Greeks. His antique [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Rebellious to his arm, lies where it falls,<br>Repugnant to command. Unequal match,<br>Pyrrhus at Priam drives, in rage strikes wide;<br>But with the whiff and [[Wind\|wind]] of his fell [[Sword\|sword]]<br>Th‘unnervèd father falls. Then senseless Ilium,<br>Seeming to feel his blow, with flaming top<br>Stoops to his base, and with a hideous crash<br>Takes prisoner Pyrrhus’ ear. For lo, his [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Which was declining on the milky head<br>Of reverend Priam, seemed i’th’ air to stick.<br>So, as a painted [[Tyrant\|tyrant]], Pyrrhus stood,<br>And, like a neutral to his will and matter,<br>Did nothing.<br>But as we often see against some [[Storm\|storm]]<br>A [[Silence\|silence]] in the heavens, the rack stand still,<br>The bold winds speechless, and the orb below<br>As hush as [[Death\|death]], anon the dreadful thunder<br>Doth rend the region: so, after Pyrrhus’ pause,<br>A rousèd vengeance sets him new a-work;<br>And never did the Cyclops’ hammers fall<br>On Mars his armour, forged for proof eterne,<br>With less remorse than Pyrrhus’ bleeding [[Sword\|sword]]<br>Now falls on Priam.<br>Out, out, thou strumpet Fortune! All you gods,<br>In general synod, take away her power,<br>Break all the spokes and fellies from her [[Wheel\|wheel]],<br>And bowl the round nave down the [[Hill\|hill]] of heaven,<br>As low as to the fiends!’ |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | This is too long. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It shall to the barber’s, with your beard. (To First Player) Prithee, say on. He’s for a jig or a tale of bawdry, or he sleeps. Say on, come to Hecuba. |
| FIRST PLAYER | ‘But who, O who had seen the mobbled [[Queen\|queen]]’— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ‘The mobbled [[Queen\|queen]]’? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | That’s good; ‘mobbled [[Queen\|queen]]’ is good. |
| FIRST PLAYER | ‘Run barefoot up and down, threat’ning the flames<br>With bisson rheum; a clout upon that head<br>Where late the diadem stood, and for a robe,<br>About her lank and all o‘er-teemèd loins,<br>A blanket in th’alarm of [[Fear\|fear]] caught up—<br>Who this had seen, with tongue in venom steeped,<br>‘Gainst Fortune’s state would treason have pronounced.<br>But if the gods themselves did see her then,<br>When she saw Pyrrhus make malicious sport<br>In mincing with his [[Sword\|sword]] her husband’s limbs,<br>The instant burst of clamour that she made—<br>Unless things mortal move them not at all—<br>Would have made milch the burning eyes of heaven,<br>And passion in the gods.’ |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Look whe’er he has not turned his colour, and has [[Tears\|tears]] in ’s eyes. (To First Player) Prithee, no more. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to First Player) ’Tis [[Well\|well]]. I’ll have thee speak out the rest soon. (To [[Polonius]]) Good my lord, will you see the players [[Well\|well]] bestowed? Do ye hear?—let them be [[Well\|well]] used, for they are the abstracts and brief chronicles of the [[Time\|time]]. After your [[Death\|death]] you were better have a bad epitaph than their ill report while you live. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | My lord, I will use them according to their [[Desert\|desert]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | God’s bodykins, man, much better. Use every man after his [[Desert\|desert]], and who should scape whipping? Use them after your own [[Honour\|honour]] and dignity—the less they deserve, the more merit is in your bounty. Take them in. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (to Players) Come, sirs. Exit |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to Players) Follow him, friends. We’ll hear a play tomorrow. Dost thou hear me, old friend? Can you play the murder of Gonzago? |
| *(didascalia)* | PLAYERS Ay, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | We’ll ha’t tomorrow [[Night\|night]]. You could for a need study a speech of some dozen or sixteen lines which I would set down and insert in’t, could ye not? |
| *(didascalia)* | PLAYERS Ay, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] Very [[Well\|well]]. Follow that lord, and look you mock him not. Exeunt Players My good friends, I’ll leave you till [[Night\|night]]. You are welcome to Elsinore. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Good my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, so. God b‘wi’ ye. Exeunt all but [[Hamlet]]<br>Now I am [[Alone\|alone]].<br>O, what a rogue and peasant slave am Il<br>Is it not monstrous that this player here,<br>But in a fiction, in [[A Dream\|a dream]] of passion,<br>Could force his [[Soul\|soul]] so to his whole conceit<br>That from her working all his visage wanned,<br>[[Tears]] in his eyes, distraction in ’s aspect,<br>A broken voice, and his whole function suiting<br>With forms to his conceit? And all for nothing.<br>For Hecuba!<br>What’s Hecuba to him, or he to Hecuba,<br>That he should weep for her? What would he do<br>Had he the motive and the cue for passion<br>That I have? He would drown the stage with [[Tears\|tears]],<br>And cleave the general ear with horrid speech,<br>Make mad the guilty and appal the free,<br>Confound the ignorant, and amaze indeed<br>The very faculty of eyes and ears. Yet I,<br>A dull and muddy-mettled rascal, peak<br>Like John-a-[[Dreams\|dreams]], unpregnant of my cause,<br>And can say nothing—no, not for a [[King\|king]]<br>Upon whose property and most dear life<br>A damned defeat was made. Am I a coward?<br>Who calls me [[Villain\|villain]], breaks my pate across,<br>Plucks off my beard and blows it in my face,<br>Tweaks me by th’ nose, gives me the lie i’th’ throat<br>As deep as to the lungs? Who does me this?<br>Ha? ‘Swounds, I should take it; for it cannot be<br>But I am pigeon-livered and lack gall<br>To make oppression bitter, or ere this<br>I should ’a’ fatted all the region kites<br>With this slave’s offal. Bloody, bawdy [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>Remorseless, treacherous, lecherous, kindless [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>O, vengeance!—<br>Why, what an ass am I? Ay, sure, this is most brave,<br>That I, the son of the dear murdered,<br>Prompted to my [[Revenge\|revenge]] by heaven and hell,<br>Must, like a whore, unpack my heart with words<br>And fall a-cursing like a very drab,<br>A scullion! Fie upon‘t, foh!—About, my brain.<br>I have heard that guilty creatures sitting at a play<br>Have by the very cunning of the scene<br>Been struck so to the [[Soul\|soul]] that presently<br>They have proclaimed their malefactions;<br>For murder, though it have no tongue, will speak<br>With most miraculous organ. I’ll have these players<br>Play something like the murder of my father<br>Before mine uncle. I’ll observe his looks,<br>I’ll tent him to the quick. If a but blench,<br>I know my course. The spirit that I have seen<br>May be the devil, and the devil hath power<br>T’assume a pleasing shape; yea, and perhaps,<br>Out of my weakness and my [[Melancholy\|melancholy]]—<br>As he is very potent with such spirits—<br>Abuses me to damn me. I’ll have grounds<br>More relative than this. The play’s the thing<br>Wherein I’ll catch the [[Conscience\|conscience]] of the [[King]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]], [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]], [[Polonius]], [[Ophelia]], [[Rosencrantz]], [[Guildenstern]], and lords |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]])<br>And can you by no drift of circumstance<br>Get from him why he puts on this confusion,<br>Grating so harshly all his days of quiet<br>With turbulent and dangerous lunacy? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | He does confess he feels himself distracted,<br>But from what cause a will by no means speak. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Nor do we find him forward to be sounded,<br>But with a crafty [[Madness\|madness]] keeps aloof<br>When we would bring him on to some confession<br>Of his true state. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Did he receive you [[Well\|well]]? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Most like a gentleman. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | But with much forcing of his disposition. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Niggard of question, but of our demands<br>Most free in his reply. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Did you assay him<br>To any pastime? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Madam, it so fell out that certain players<br>We o’er-raught on the way. Of these we told him,<br>And there did seem in him a kind of joy<br>To hear of it. They are about the [[Court\|court]],<br>And, as I think, they have already order<br>This [[Night\|night]] to play before him. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | ’Tis most true, And he beseeched me to entreat your majesties<br>To hear and see the matter. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | With all my heart; and it doth much content me<br>To hear him so inclined.—Good gentlemen,<br>Give him a further edge, and drive his purpose on<br>To these delights. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | We shall, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Sweet [[Gertrude]], leave us too,<br>For we have closely sent for [[Hamlet]] hither,<br>That he, as ‘twere by accident, may here<br>Affront [[Ophelia]].<br>Her father and myself, lawful espials,<br>Will so bestow ourselves that, seeing unseen,<br>We may of their encounter frankly judge,<br>And gather by him, as he is behaved,<br>If’t be th’affliction of his [[Love\|love]] or no<br>That thus he suffers for. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I shall obey you.<br>And for your part, [[Ophelia]], I do wish<br>That your good beauties be the happy cause<br>Of [[Hamlet]]’s wildness; so shall I [[Hope\|hope]] your virtues<br>Will bring him to his wonted way again,<br>To both your honours. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Madam, I wish it may. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Gertrude]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | [[Ophelia]], walk you here.—Gracious, so please you,<br>We will bestow ourselves.—Read on this book,<br>That show of such an exercise may colour<br>Your [[Loneliness\|loneliness]]. We are oft to blame in this:<br>‘Tis too much proved that with devotion’s visage<br>And pious action we do sugar o’er<br>The devil himself. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, ’tis too true.<br>(Aside) How smart a lash that speech doth give my<br>[[Conscience\|conscience]].<br>The harlot’s cheek, beautied with plast’ring art,<br>Is not more ugly to the thing that helps it<br>Than is my deed to my most painted word.<br>O heavy burden! |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I hear him coming. Let’s withdraw, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Claudius]] and [[Polonius]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | To be, or not to be; that is the question:<br>Whether ‘tis nobler in the mind to suffer<br>The slings and arrows of outrageous fortune,<br>Or to take arms against a sea of troubles,<br>And, by opposing, end them. To die, to [[Sleep\|sleep]]—<br>No more, and by a [[Sleep\|sleep]] to say we end<br>The heartache and the thousand natural shocks<br>That flesh is heir to—’tis a consummation<br>Devoutly to be wished. To die, to [[Sleep\|sleep]].<br>To [[Sleep\|sleep]], perchance to dream. Ay, there’s the rub,<br>For in that [[Sleep\|sleep]] of [[Death\|death]] what [[Dreams\|dreams]] may come<br>When we have shuffled off this mortal coil<br>Must give us pause. There’s the respect<br>That makes calamity of so long life,<br>For who would bear the whips and scorns of [[Time\|time]],<br>Th‘oppressor’s wrong, the proud man’s contumely,<br>The pangs of disprized [[Love\|love]], the law’s delay,<br>The insolence of office, and the spurns<br>That patient merit of th’unworthy takes,<br>When he himself might his quietus make<br>With a bare bodkin? Who would these fardels bear,<br>To grunt and sweat under a weary life,<br>But that the dread of something after [[Death\|death]],<br>The undiscovered country from whose bourn<br>No traveller returns, puzzles the will,<br>And makes us rather bear those ills we have<br>Than fly to others that we know not of?<br>Thus [[Conscience\|conscience]] does make cowards of us all,<br>And thus the native hue of resolution<br>Is sicklied o’er with the pale cast of thought,<br>And enterprises of great pith and moment<br>With this regard their currents turn awry,<br>And lose the name of action. Soft you, now,<br>The fair [[Ophelia]]!—Nymph, in thy orisons<br>Be all my sins remembered. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Good my lord,<br>How does your [[Honour\|honour]] for this many a day? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I humbly thank you, [[Well\|well]], [[Well\|well]], [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My lord, I have remembrances of yours<br>That I have longed long to redeliver.<br>I pray you now receive them. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, no, I never gave you aught. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My honoured lord, you know right [[Well\|well]] you did,<br>And with them words of so sweet breath composed<br>As made the things more rich. Their perfume lost,<br>Take these again; for to the noble mind<br>Rich gifts wax poor when givers prove unkind.<br>There, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ha, ha? Are you honest? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | My lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Are you fair? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | What means your lordship? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That if you be honest and fair, your honesty should admit no discourse to your [[Beauty\|beauty]]. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Could [[Beauty\|beauty]], my lord, have better commerce than with honesty? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, truly, for the power of [[Beauty\|beauty]] will sooner transform honesty from what it is to a bawd than the force of honesty can translate [[Beauty\|beauty]] into his likeness. This was sometime a [[Paradox\|paradox]], but now the [[Time\|time]] gives it proof. I did [[Love\|love]] you once. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Indeed, my lord, you made me believe so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | You should not have believed me, for virtue cannot so inoculate our old stock but we shall relish of it. I loved you not. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I was the more deceived. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Get thee to a nunnery. Why wouldst thou be a breeder of sinners? I am myself indifferent honest, but yet I could accuse me of such things that it were better my mother had not borne me. I am very proud, revengeful, ambitious, with more offences at my beck than I have thoughts to put them in, [[Imagination\|imagination]] to give them shape, or [[Time\|time]] to act them in. What should such fellows as I do crawling between heaven and earth? We are arrant knaves, all. Believe none of us. Go thy ways to a nunnery. Where’s your father? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | At [[Home\|home]], my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Let the doors be shut upon him, that he may play the [[Fool\|fool]] nowhere but in ’s own [[house]]. Farewell. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | O help him, you sweet heavens! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | If thou dost marry, I’ll give thee this plague for thy dowry: be thou as chaste as ice, as pure as [[Snow\|snow]], thou shalt not escape calumny. Get thee to a nunnery, go, farewell. Or if thou wilt needs marry, marry a [[Fool\|fool]]; for wise men know [[Well\|well]] enough what monsters you make of them. To a nunnery, go, and quickly, too. Farewell. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | O heavenly powers, restore him! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I have heard of your paintings, too, [[Well\|well]] enough. God hath given you one face, and you make yourselves another. You jig, you amble, and you lisp, and nickname God’s creatures, and make your wantonness your ignorance. Go to, I’ll no more on’t. It hath made me mad. I say we will have no more marriages. Those that are married already—all but one—shall live. The rest shall keep as they are. To a nunnery, go. Exit |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | O what a noble mind is here o‘erthrown!<br>The courtier’s, soldier‘s, scholar’s eye, tongue, [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Th’expectancy and [[Rose\|rose]] of the fair state,<br>The glass of fashion and the mould of form,<br>Th‘observed of all observers, quite, quite, down!<br>And I, of ladies most deject and wretched,<br>That sucked the honey of his music vows,<br>Now see that noble and most sovereign reason<br>Like sweet [[Bells\|bells]] jangled out of tune and harsh;<br>That unmatched form and feature of blown youth<br>Blasted with ecstasy. O woe is me,<br>T’have seen what I have seen, see what I see! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] and [[Polonius]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Love]]? His affections do not that way tend,<br>Nor what he spake, though it lacked form a little,<br>Was not like [[Madness\|madness]]. There’s something in his [[Soul\|soul]]<br>O’er which his [[Melancholy\|melancholy]] sits on brood,<br>And I do [[Doubt\|doubt]] the hatch and the disclose<br>Will be some danger; which to prevent<br>I have in quick determination<br>Thus set it down: he shall with [[Speed\|speed]] to England<br>For the demand of our neglected tribute.<br>Haply the seas and countries different,<br>With variable objects, shall expel<br>This something-settled matter in his heart,<br>Whereon his brains still beating puts him thus<br>From fashion of himself. What think you on’t? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | It shall do [[Well\|well]]. But yet do I believe<br>The origin and commencement of this grief<br>Sprung from neglected [[Love\|love]].—How now, [[Ophelia]]?<br>You need not tell us what Lord [[Hamlet]] said;<br>We heard it all.—My lord, do as you please,<br>But, if you hold it fit, after the play<br>Let his [[Queen\|queen]] mother all [[Alone\|alone]] entreat him<br>To show his griefs. Let her be round with him,<br>And I’ll be placed, so please you, in the ear<br>Of all their conference. If she find him not,<br>To England send him, or confine him where<br>Your wisdom best shall think. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | It shall be so.<br>[[Madness]] in great ones must not unwatched go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] and two or three of the Players |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Speak the speech, I pray you, as I pronounced it to you—trippingly on the tongue; but if you mouth it, as many of your players do, I had as lief the town-crier had spoke my lines. Nor do not saw the air too much with your hand, thus, but use all gently; for in the very torrent, tempest, and as I may say the whirlwind of your passion, you must acquire and beget a temperance that may give it smoothness. O, it offends me to the [[Soul\|soul]] to hear a robustious, periwig-pated fellow tear a passion to tatters, to very rags, to split the ears of the groundlings, who for the most part are capable of nothing but inexplicable dumb shows and noise. I would have such a fellow whipped for o’erdoing Termagant. It out-Herods Herod. Pray you avoid it.<br>A PLAYER I warrant your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Be not too tame, neither; but let your own discretion be your tutor. Suit the action to the word, the word to the action, with this special observance: that you o‘erstep not the modesty of [[Nature\|nature]]. For anything so overdone is from the purpose of playing, whose end, both at the first and now, was and is to hold as ’twere the [[Mirror\|mirror]] up to [[Nature\|nature]], to show virtue her own feature, scorn her own image, and the very age and [[Body\|body]] of the [[Time\|time]] his form and pressure. Now this overdone, or come tardy off, though it make the unskilful laugh, cannot but make the judicious grieve; the censure of the which one must in your allowance o’erweigh a whole theatre of others. O, there be players that I have seen play, and heard others praise, and that highly, not to speak it profanely, that neither having the accent of Christians nor the gait of Christian, pagan, nor no man, have so strutted and bellowed that I have thought some of [[Nature\|nature]]’s journeymen had made men, and not made them [[Well\|well]], they imitated humanity so abominably.<br>A PLAYER I [[Hope\|hope]] we have reformed that indifferently with us, sir. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, [[Reform\|reform]] it altogether. And let those that play your clowns speak no more than is set down for them; for there be of them that will themselves laugh to set on some quantity of barren spectators to laugh too, though in the mean [[Time\|time]] some necessary question of the play be then to be considered. That’s villainous, and shows a most pitiful [[Ambition\|ambition]] in the [[Fool\|fool]] that uses it. Go make you ready. Exeunt Players |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]], [[Guildenstern]], and [[Rosencrantz]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (To [[Polonius]]) How now, my lord? Will the [[King]] hear this piece of work? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | And the [[Queen]] too, and that presently. [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] Bid the players make haste. Exit [[Polonius]]<br>Will you two help to hasten them? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | and [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] We will, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What ho, [[Horatio]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]] |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Here, sweet lord, at your service. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[Horatio]], thou art e‘en as just a man<br>As e’er my conversation coped withal. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | O my dear lord— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, do not think I flatter;<br>For what advancement may I [[Hope\|hope]] from thee,<br>That no revenue hast but thy good spirits<br>To feed and clothe thee? Why should the poor be<br>flattered?<br>No, let the candied tongue lick absurd pomp,<br>And crook the pregnant hinges of the knee<br>Where thrift may follow feigning. Dost thou hear?—<br>Since my dear [[Soul\|soul]] was mistress of her choice<br>And could of men distinguish, her election<br>Hath sealed thee for herself; for thou hast been<br>As one in suff‘ring all that suffers nothing,<br>A man that Fortune’s buffets and rewards<br>Hath ta’en with equal thanks; and blest are those<br>Whose [[Blood\|blood]] and judgement are so [[Well\|well]] commingled<br>That they are not a pipe for Fortune’s finger<br>To sound what stop she please. Give me that man<br>That is not passion’s slave, and I will wear him<br>In my heart’s core, ay, in my heart of heart,<br>As I do thee. Something too much of this.<br>There is a play tonight before the [[King]].<br>One scene of it comes near the circumstance<br>Which I have told thee of my father’s [[Death\|death]].<br>I prithee, when thou seest that act afoot,<br>Even with the very comment of thy [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Observe mine uncle. If his occulted [[Guilt\|guilt]]<br>Do not itself unkennel in one speech,<br>It is a damned [[Ghost\|ghost]] that we have seen,<br>And my imaginations are as foul<br>As Vulcan’s stithy. Give him heedful note,<br>For I mine eyes will rivet to his face,<br>And after, we will both our judgements join<br>To censure of his seeming. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | [[Well]], my lord.<br>If a steal aught the whilst this play is playing<br>And scape detecting, I will pay the theft. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a flourish |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | They are coming to the play. I must be idle.<br>Get you a place. |
| *(didascalia)* | Danish march. Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]], [[Queen]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | [[Gertrude]], [[Polonius]], [[Ophelia]], [[Rosencrantz]],<br><br>[[Guildenstern]], and other lords attendant, with the |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King]]’s guard carrying torches |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How fares our cousin [[Hamlet]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Excellent, i’[[Faith\|faith]], of the chameleon’s dish. I eat the air, promise-crammed. You cannot feed capons so. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I have nothing with this answer, [[Hamlet]].<br>These words are not mine. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, nor mine now. (To [[Polonius]]) My lord, you played once i’th’ university, you say. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | That I did, my lord, and was accounted a good actor. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And what did you enact? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I did enact [[Julius Caesar]]. I was killed i’th’ Capitol. [[Brutus]] killed me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It was a brute part of him to kill so capital a calf there.—Be the players ready? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Ay, my lord, they stay upon your patience. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Come hither, my good [[Hamlet]]. Sit by me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, good-mother, here’s mettle more attractive.<br>He sits by [[Ophelia]] |
| POLONTUS | (aside) O ho, do you mark that? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Ophelia]]) Lady, shall I lie in your lap? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | No, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I mean my head upon your lap? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do you think I meant country matters? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I think nothing, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That’s a fair thought to lie between maids’ legs. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | What is, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No thing. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You are merry, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Who, I? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O God, your only jig-maker! What should a man do but be merry? For look you how cheerfully my mother looks, and my father died within ’s two hours. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Nay, ’tis twice two mqnths, my lord. 122 |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | So long? Nay then, let the devil wear black, for I’ll have a suit of sables. O heavens, die two months ago and not forgotten yet! Then there’s [[Hope\|hope]] a great man’s [[Memory\|memory]] may outlive his life half a year. But, by‘r Lady, a must build churches then, or else shall a suffer not thinking on, with the hobby-[[Horse\|horse]], whose epitaph is ‘For O, for O, the hobby-[[Horse\|horse]] is forgot.’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Hautboys play. The dumb show enters. Enter a [[King]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | and a [[Queen]] very lovingly, the [[Queen]] embracing him.<br>She kneels and makes show of protestation unto him.<br>He takes her up and declines his head upon her neck.<br>He lays him down upon a bank of [[Flowers\|flowers]]. She, seeing<br>him asleep, [[Leaves\|leaves]] him. Anon comes in a fellow, takes<br>off his [[Crown\|crown]], kisses it, and pours [[Poison\|poison]] in the [[King]]’s<br>ears, and exits. The [[Queen]] returns, finds<br>the [[King]] dead, and makes passionate action. The<br>poisoner, with some two or three mutes, comes in<br>again, seeming to lament with her. The dead [[Body\|body]] is<br>carried away. The poisoner woos the [[Queen]] with<br>gifts. She seems loath and unwilling a while, but in<br>the end accepts his [[Love\|love]]. Exeunt the Players |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | What means this, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Marry, this is miching malhecho. That means mischief. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Belike this show imports the argument of the play. Enter Prologue |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | We shall know by this fellow. The players cannot keep counsel, they’ll tell all. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Will a tell us what this show meant? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, or any show that you’ll show him. Be not you ashamed to show, he’ll not shame to tell you what it means. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You are naught, you are naught. I’ll mark the play. |
| PROLOGUE | For us and for our [[Tragedy\|tragedy]]<br>Here stooping to your clemency,<br>We beg your hearing patiently. Exit |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Is this a prologue, or the posy of a ring? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | ’Tis brief, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | As woman’s [[Love\|love]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Player [[King]] and his [[Queen]] |
| PLAYER [[King\|KING]] | Full thirty times hath Phoebus’ cart gone round<br>Neptune’s salt wash and Tellus’ orbèd ground,<br>And thirty dozen moons with borrowed sheen<br>About the world have times twelve thirties been<br>Since [[Love\|love]] our hearts and Hymen did our hands<br>Unite commutual in most sacred bands. |
| PLAYER [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | So many journeys may the sun and [[Moon\|moon]]<br>Make us again count o’er ere [[Love\|love]] be done.<br>But woe is me, you are so sick of late,<br>So far from cheer and from your former state,<br>That I distrust you. Yet, though I distrust,<br>Discomfort you my lord it nothing must.<br>For women’s [[Fear\|fear]] and [[Love\|love]] holds quantity,<br>In neither aught, or in extremity.<br>Now what my [[Love\|love]] is, proof hath made you know,<br>And as my [[Love\|love]] is sized, my [[Fear\|fear]] is so. |
| PLAYER [[King\|KING]] | [[Faith]], I must leave thee, [[Love\|love]], and shortly too.<br>My operant powers their functions leave to do,<br>And thou shalt live in this fair world behind,<br>Honoured, beloved; and haply one as kind<br>For husband shalt thou— |
| PLAYER [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | O, confound the rest!<br>Such [[Love\|love]] must needs be treason in my breast.<br>In second husband let me be accurst;<br>None wed the second but who killed the first. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Wormwood, wormwood. |
| PLAYER [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | The instances that second [[Marriage\|marriage]] move<br>Are base respects of thrift, but none of [[Love\|love]].<br>A second [[Time\|time]] I kill my husband dead<br>When second husband kisses me in bed. |
| PLAYER [[King\|KING]] | I do believe you think what now you speak;<br>But what we do determine oft we break.<br>Purpose is but the slave to [[Memory\|memory]],<br>Of violent birth but poor validity,<br>Which now like fruit unripe sticks on the tree,<br>But fall unshaken when they mellow be.<br>Most necessary ‘tis that we forget<br>To pay ourselves what to ourselves is debt.<br>What to ourselves in passion we propose,<br>The passion ending, doth the purpose lose.<br>The violence of either grief or joy<br>Their own enactures with themselves destroy.<br>Where joy most revels, grief doth most lament;<br>Grief joys, joy grieves, on slender accident.<br>This world is not for aye, nor ’tis not strange<br>That even our loves should with our fortunes change;<br>For ’tis a question left us yet to prove<br>Whether [[Love\|love]] lead fortune or else fortune [[Love\|love]].<br>The great man down, you mark his favourite flies;<br>The poor advanced makes friends of enemies.<br>And hitherto doth [[Love\|love]] on fortune tend,<br>For who not needs shall never lack a friend,<br>And who in want a hollow friend doth try<br>Directly [[Seasons\|seasons]] him his enemy.<br>But orderly to end where I begun,<br>Our wills and fates do so contrary run<br>That our devices still are overthrown;<br>Our thoughts are ours, their ends none of our own.<br>So think thou wilt no second husband wed;<br>But die thy thoughts when thy first lord is dead. |
| PLAYER [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | Nor earth to me give food, nor heaven light,<br>Sport and repose lock from me day and [[Night\|night]],<br>Each opposite that blanks the face of joy<br>Meet what I would have [[Well\|well]] and it destroy,<br>Both here and hence pursue me lasting strife<br>If, once a widow, ever I be wife. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | If she should break it now! |
| PLAYER [[King\|KING]] | (to Player [[Queen]])<br>’Tis deeply sworn. Sweet, leave me here a while.<br>My spirits grow dull, and fain I would beguile<br>The tedious day with [[Sleep\|sleep]]. |
| PLAYER [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | [[Sleep]] rock thy brain,<br>And never come mischance between us twain.<br>Player [[King]] sleeps. Player [[Queen]] exits |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) Madam, how like you this play? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | The lady protests too much, methinks. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, but she’ll keep her word. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Have you heard the argument? Is there no offence in’t? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, no, they do but jest, [[Poison\|poison]] in jest. No offence i’th’ world. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What do you call the play? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The Mousetrap. Marry, how? Tropically. This play is the image of a murder done in Vienna. Gonzago is the Duke’s name, his wife [[Baptista]]. You shall see anon. ’Tis a knavish piece of work; but what o’ that? Your majesty, and we that have free souls, it touches us not. Let the galled jade wince, our withers are unwrung. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Player Lucianus |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | This is one Lucianus, nephew to the [[King]]. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You are as good as a [[Chorus\|chorus]], my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I could interpret between you and your [[Love\|love]] if I could see the puppets dallying. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You are keen, my lord, you are keen. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It would cost you a groaning to take off mine edge. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Still better, and worse. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | So you mis-take your husbands. (To Lucianus) Begin, murderer. Pox, leave thy damnable faces and begin. Come: ‘the croaking raven doth bellow for [[Revenge\|revenge]]’. |
| PLAYER LUCIANUS | Thoughts black, hands apt, drugs fit, and [[Time\|time]]<br>agreeing,<br>Confederate season, else no creature seeing;<br>Thou mixture rank of midnight weeds collected,<br>With Hecate’s ban thrice blasted, thrice infected,<br>Thy natural [[Magic\|magic]] and dire property<br>On wholesome life usurp immediately.<br>He pours the [[Poison\|poison]] in the Player [[King]]’s ear |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A poisons him i’th’ [[Garden\|garden]] for ’s estate. His name’s Gonzago. The story is extant, and writ in choice Italian. You shall see anon how the murderer gets the [[Love\|love]] of Gonzago’s wife. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | The [[King]] rises. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What, frighted with false [[Fire\|fire]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Claudius]]) How fares my lord?<br>POLONIUSGive o’er the play. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] Give me some light. Away. COURTIERS Lights, lights, lights! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, let the stricken deer go weep,<br>The hart ungallèd play,<br>For some must watch, while some must [[Sleep\|sleep]],<br>So runs the world away.<br>Would not this, sir, and a forest of feathers, if the rest<br>of my fortunes turn Turk with me, with two Provençal<br>roses on my razed shoes, get me a fellowship in a cry<br>of players, sir? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Half a share. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A whole one, I.<br>For thou dost know, O Damon dear,<br>This realm dismantled was<br>Of Jove himself, and now reigns here<br>A very, very—pajock. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | You might have rhymed. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O good [[Horatio]], I’ll take the [[Ghost]]’s word for a thousand pound. Didst perceive? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Very [[Well\|well]], my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Upon the talk of the pois’ning? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I did very [[Well\|well]] note him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ah ha! Come, some music, come, the recorders,<br>For if the [[King]] like not the [[Comedy\|comedy]],<br>Why then, belike he likes it not, pardie.<br>Come, some music. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Good my lord, vouchsafe me a word with you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, a whole [[history]]. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | The [[King]], sir— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, sir, what of him? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Is in his retirement marvellous distempered. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | With drink, sir? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | No, my lord, rather with choler. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Your wisdom should show itself more richer to signify this to his doctor, for for me to put him to his purgation would perhaps plunge him into far more choler. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Good my lord, put your discourse into some frame, and start not so wildly from my affair. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am tame, sir. Pronounce. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | The [[Queen]] your mother, in most great affliction of spirit, hath sent me to you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | You are welcome. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Nay, good my lord, this courtesy is not of the right breed. If it shall please you to make me a wholesome answer, I will do your mother’s commandment; if not, your pardon and my return shall be the end of my business. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, I cannot. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | What, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Make you a wholesome answer. My wit’s diseased. But, sir, such answers as I can make, you shall command; or rather, as you say, my mother. Therefore no more, but to the matter. My mother, you say? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Then thus she says: your behaviour hath struck her into amazement and admiration. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O wonderful son, that can so astonish a mother! But is there no sequel at the heels of this mother’s admiration? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | She desires to speak with you in her closet ere you go to bed. 319 |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | We shall obey, were she ten times our mother. Have you any further trade with us? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | My lord, you once did [[Love\|love]] me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | So I do still, by these pickers and stealers. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Good my lord, what is your cause of distemper? You do freely bar the door of your own liberty if you deny your griefs to your friend. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, I lack advancement. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | How can that be when you have the voice of the [[King]] himself for your succession in [[Denmark]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, but ‘while the grass grows ...’—the proverb is something musty. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter one with a recorder |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, the recorder. Let me see. (To [[Rosencrantz]] and<br><br>[[Guildenstern]], taking them aside) To withdraw with you,<br>why do you go about to recover the [[Wind\|wind]] of me as if<br>you would drive me into a toil? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | O my lord, if my duty be too bold, my [[Love\|love]] is too unmannerly. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I do not [[Well\|well]] understand that. Will you play upon this pipe? |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | My lord, I cannot. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I pray you. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | Believe me, I cannot. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I do beseech you. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | I know no touch of it, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ’Tis as easy as lying. Govern these ventages with your fingers and thumb, give it breath with your mouth, and it will discourse most excellent music. Look you, these are the stops. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | But these cannot I command to any utterance of harmony. I have not the skill. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, look you now, how unworthy a thing you make of me! You would play upon me, you would seem to know my stops, you would pluck out the heart of my mystery, you would sound me from my lowest note to the top of my compass; and there is much music, excellent voice in this little organ, yet cannot you make it speak. ’Sblood, do you think I am easier to be played on than a pipe? Call me what instrument you will, though you can fret me, you cannot play upon me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | God bless you, sir. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | My lord, the [[Queen]] would speak with you, and presently. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do you see yonder cloud that’s almost in shape of a camel? |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | By th’ mass, and ’tis: like a camel, indeed. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Methinks it is like a weasel. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | It is backed like a weasel. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Or like a whale. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | Very like a whale. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Then will I come to my mother by and by. (Aside) They [[Fool\|fool]] me to the top of my bent. (To [[Polonius]]) I will come by and by. |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | I will say so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ’By and by’ is easily said. Exit [[Polonius]]<br>Leave me, friends. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ’Tis now the very witching [[Time\|time]] of [[Night\|night]],<br>When churchyards yawn, and hell itself breathes out<br>Contagion to this world. Now could I drink hot [[Blood\|blood]],<br>And do such bitter business as the day<br>Would quake to look on. Soft, now to my mother.<br>O heart, lose not thy [[Nature\|nature]]! Let not ever<br>The [[Soul\|soul]] of Nero enter this firm bosom.<br>Let me be cruel, not unnatural.<br>I will speak daggers to her, but use none.<br>My tongue and [[Soul\|soul]] in this be hypocrites—<br>How in my words somever she be shent,<br>To give them seals never my [[Soul\|soul]] consent. Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]], [[Rosencrantz]], and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I like him not, nor stands it safe with us<br>To let his [[Madness\|madness]] range. Therefore prepare you.<br>I your commission will forthwith dispatch,<br>And he to England shall along with you.<br>The terms of our estate may not endure<br>Hazard so dangerous as doth hourly grow<br>Out of his lunacies. |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | We will ourselves provide.<br>Most holy and religious [[Fear\|fear]] it is<br>To keep those many many bodies safe<br>That live and feed upon your majesty. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | The single and peculiar life is bound<br>With all the strength and armour of the mind<br>To keep itself from noyance; but much more<br>That spirit upon whose weal depends and rests<br>The lives of many. The cease of majesty |
| *(didascalia)* | Dies not [[Alone\|alone]], but like a gulf doth draw |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | What’s near it with it. It is a massy [[Wheel\|wheel]]<br>Fixed on the summit of the highest mount,<br>To whose huge spokes ten thousand lesser things<br>Are mortised and adjoined, which when it falls<br>Each small annexment, petty consequence,<br>Attends the boist’rous ruin. Never [[Alone\|alone]]<br>Did the [[King]] sigh, but with a general groan. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Arm you, I pray you, to this speedy voyage,<br>For we will fetters put upon this [[Fear\|fear]]<br>Which now goes too free-footed. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | and [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]]<br>We will haste us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt both |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Polonius]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | My lord, he’s going to his mother’s closet.<br>Behind the arras I’ll convey myself<br>To hear the process. I’ll warrant she’ll tax him [[Home\|home]].<br>And, as you said—and wisely was it said—<br>‘Tis meet that some more audience than a mother,<br>Since [[Nature\|nature]] makes them partial, should o’erhear<br>The speech of vantage. Fare you [[Well\|well]], my liege.<br>I’ll call upon you ere you go to bed,<br>And tell you what I know. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Thanks, dear my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Polonius]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, my offence is rank! It smells to heaven.<br>It hath the primal eldest curse upon‘t,<br>A brother’s murder. Pray can I not.<br>Though inclination be as sharp as will,<br>My stronger [[Guilt\|guilt]] defeats my strong intent,<br>And like a man to [[Double\|double]] business bound<br>I stand in pause where I shall first begin,<br>And both neglect. What if this cursed hand<br>Were thicker than itself with brother’s [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Is there not rain enough in the sweet heavens<br>To wash it white as [[Snow\|snow]]? Whereto serves [[Mercy\|mercy]]<br>But to confront the visage of offence?<br>And what’s in [[prayer]] but this twofold force,<br>To be forestalled ere we come to fall,<br>Or pardoned being down? Then I’ll look up.<br>My fault is past-but O, what form of [[prayer]]<br>Can serve my turn? ‘Forgive me my foul murder’?<br>That cannot be, since I am still possessed<br>Of those effects for which I did the murder—<br>My [[Crown\|crown]], mine own [[Ambition\|ambition]], and my [[Queen\|queen]].<br>May one be pardoned and retain th’offence?<br>In the corrupted currents of this world<br>Offence’s gilded hand may shove by justice,<br>And oft ‘tis seen the wicked prize itself<br>Buys out the law. But ’tis not so above.<br>There is no shuffling, there the action lies<br>In his true [[Nature\|nature]], and we ourselves compelled<br>Even to the teeth and forehead of our faults<br>To give in evidence. What then? What rests?<br>Try what repentance can. What can it not?<br>Yet what can it when one cannot repent?<br>O wretched state, O bosom black as [[Death\|death]],<br>O limed [[Soul\|soul]] that, struggling to be free,<br>Art more engaged! Help, angels! Make assay.<br>Bow, stubborn knees; and heart with strings of steel,<br>Be soft as sinews of the new-born babe.<br>All may be [[Well\|well]].<br>He kneels. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] behind him |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Now might I do it pat, now a is praying,<br>And now I’ll do‘t, |
| *(didascalia)* | He draws his [[Sword\|sword]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | and so a goes to heaven,<br>And so am I revenged. That would be scanned.<br>A [[Villain\|villain]] kills my father, and for that<br>I, his sole son, do this same [[Villain\|villain]] send<br>To heaven.<br>O, this is hire and salary, not [[Revenge\|revenge]]!<br>A took my father grossly, full of bread,<br>With all his crimes broad blown, as flush as May;<br>And how his audit stands, who knows save heaven?<br>But in our circumstance and course of thought<br>’Tis heavy with him. And am I then revenged<br>To take him in the purging of his [[Soul\|soul]],<br>When he is fit and seasoned for his passage?<br>No.<br>He sheathes his [[Sword\|sword]]<br>Up, [[Sword\|sword]], and know thou a more horrid hint.<br>When he is drunk asleep, or in his rage,<br>Or in th‘incestuous pleasure of his bed,<br>At gaming, swearing, or about some act<br>That has no relish of salvation in’t,<br>Then trip him that his heels may kick at heaven,<br>And that his [[Soul\|soul]] may be as damned and black<br>As hell whereto it goes. My mother stays.<br>This physic but prolongs thy sickly days. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | My words fly up, my thoughts remain below.<br>Words without thoughts never to heaven go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] and [[Polonius]] |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | A will come straight. Look you lay [[Home\|home]] to him.<br>Tell him his pranks have been too broad to bear with,<br>And that your grace hath screened and stood between<br>Much heat and him. I’ll [[Silence\|silence]] me e’en here.<br>Pray you be round with him. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (within) Mother, mother, mother! |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I’ll warr’nt you. [[Fear]] me not. Withdraw; I hear him<br>coming.<br>[[Polonius]] hides behind the arras. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Now, mother, what’s the matter? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | [[Hamlet]], thou hast thy father much offended,. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Mother, you have my father much offended. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Come, come, you answer with an idle tongue. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Go, go, you question with a wicked tongue. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Why, how now, [[Hamlet]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What’s the matter now? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Have you forgot me? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, by the rood, not so.<br>You are the [[Queen]], your husband’s brother’s wife. But—would you were not so—you are my mother. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Nay, then, I’ll set those to you that can speak. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Come, come, and sit you down. You shall not budge.<br>You go not till I set you up a glass<br>Where you may see the inmost part of you. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | What wilt thou do? Thou wilt not murder me?<br>Help, help, ho! |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | (behind the arras) What ho! Help, help, help! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How now, a rat? Dead for a ducat, dead.<br>He thrusts his [[Sword\|sword]] through the arras |
| [[Polonius\|POLONIUS]] | O, I am slain! |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) O me, what hast thou done? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, I know not. Is it the [[King]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O, what a rash and bloody deed is this! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A bloody deed—almost as bad, good-mother,<br>As kill a [[King\|king]] and marry with his brother. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | As kill a [[King\|king]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, lady, ’twas my word.<br>(To [[Polonius]]) Thou wretched, rash, intruding [[Fool\|fool]],<br>farewell.<br>I took thee for thy better. Take thy fortune.<br>Thou find’st to be too busy is some danger.—<br>Leave wringing of your hands. Peace, sit you down,<br>And let me wring your heart; for so I shall<br>If it be made of penetrable stuff,<br>If damned custom have not brassed it so<br>That it is proof and bulwark against sense. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | What have I done, that thou dar’st wag thy tongue<br>In noise so rude against me? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Such an act<br>That blurs the grace and blush of modesty,<br>Calls virtue hypocrite, takes off the [[Rose\|rose]]<br>From the fair forehead of an [[Innocent\|innocent]] [[Love\|love]]<br>And sets a blister there, makes [[Marriage\|marriage]] vows<br>As false as dicers’ oaths—O, such a deed<br>As from the [[Body\|body]] of contraction plucks<br>The very [[Soul\|soul]], and sweet religion makes<br>A rhapsody of words. Heaven’s face doth glow,<br>Yea, this solidity and compound mass<br>With tristful visage, as against the doom,<br>Is thought-sick at the act. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Ay me, what act,<br>That roars so loud and thunders in the index? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Look here upon this picture, and on this,<br>The counterfeit presentment of two brothers.<br>See what a grace was seated on this brow—<br>[[Hyperion]]’s curls, the front of Jove himself,<br>An eye like Mars, to threaten or command,<br>A station like the herald Mercury<br>New lighted on a heaven-kissing [[Hill\|hill]];<br>A combination and a form indeed<br>Where every god did seem to set his seal<br>To give the world assurance of a man.<br>This was your husband. Look you now what follows.<br>Here is your husband, like a mildewed ear<br>Blasting his wholesome brother. Have you eyes?<br>Could you on this fair mountain leave to feed,<br>And batten on this moor? Ha, have you eyes?<br>You cannot call it [[Love\|love]], for at your age<br>The heyday in the [[Blood\|blood]] is tame, it’s humble,<br>And waits upon the judgement; and what judgement<br>Would step from this to this? What devil was’t<br>That thus hath cozened you at hood-man blind?<br>O shame, where is thy blush? Rebellious hell,<br>If thou canst mutine in a matron’s bones,<br>To flaming youth let virtue be as wax<br>And melt in her own [[Fire\|fire]]. Proclaim no shame<br>When the compulsive ardour gives the charge,<br>Since [[Frost\|frost]] itself as actively doth burn,<br>And reason panders will. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O [[Hamlet]], speak no more!<br>Thou turn’st mine eyes into my very [[Soul\|soul]],<br>And there I see such black and grained spots<br>As will not leave their tinct. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, but to live<br>In the rank sweat of an enseamèd bed,<br>Stewed in corruption, honeying and making [[Love\|love]]<br>Over the nasty sty— |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O, speak to me no more!<br>These words like daggers enter in mine ears.<br>No more, sweet [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A murderer and a [[Villain\|villain]],<br>A slave that is not twenti’th part the tithe<br>Of your precedent lord, a vice of kings,<br>A cutpurse of the empire and the rule,<br>That from a shelf the precious diadem stole<br>And put it in his pocket— |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | No more. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A [[King\|king]] of shreds and patches— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]] in his nightgown |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Save me and hover o’er me with your wings,<br>You heavenly guards! (To the [[Ghost]]) What would<br>you, gracious figure? 95 |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, he’s mad. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to the [[Ghost]])<br>Do you not come your tardy son to chide,<br>That, lapsed in [[Time\|time]] and passion, lets go by<br>Th’important acting of your dread command?<br>O, say! |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] | Do not forget. This visitation<br>Is but to whet thy almost blunted purpose.<br>But look, amazement on thy mother sits.<br>O, step between her and her fighting [[Soul\|soul]].<br>Conceit in weakest bodies strongest works.<br>Speak to her, [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How is it with you, lady? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, how is’t with you,<br>That you do bend your eye on vacancy,<br>And with th‘incorporal air do hold discourse?<br>Forth at your eyes your spirits wildly peep,<br>And, as the sleeping soldiers in th’alarm,<br>Your bedded hair, like life in excrements,<br>Start up and stand on end. O gentle son,<br>Upon the heat and flame of thy distemper<br>Sprinkle cool patience! Whereon do you look? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | On him, on him. Look you how pale he glares.<br>His form and cause conjoined, preaching to stones,<br>Would make them capable. (To the [[Ghost]]) Do not look<br>upon me,<br>Lest with this piteous action you [[convert]]<br>My stern effects. Then what I have to do<br>Will want true colour—[[Tears\|tears]] perchance for [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | To whom do you speak this? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do you see nothing there? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Nothing at all, yet all that is I see. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nor did you nothing hear? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | No, nothing but ourselves. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, look you there. Look how it steals away.<br>My father, in his habit as he lived.<br>Look where he goes even now out at the portal. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the [[Ghost]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | This is the very coinage of your brain.<br>This bodiless creation ecstasy<br>Is very cunning in. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ecstasy?<br>My pulse as yours doth temperately keep [[Time\|time]],<br>And makes as healthful music. It is not [[Madness\|madness]]<br>That I have uttered. Bring me to the test,<br>And I the matter will reword, which [[Madness\|madness]]<br>Would gambol from. Mother, for [[Love\|love]] of grace<br>Lay not a flattering unction to your [[Soul\|soul]]<br>That not your trespass but my [[Madness\|madness]] speaks.<br>It will but skin and film the ulcerous place<br>Whilst rank corruption, mining all within,<br>Infects unseen. Confess yourself to heaven;<br>Repent what’s past, avoid what is to come,<br>And do not spread the compost o’er the weeds<br>To make them ranker. Forgive me this my virtue,<br>For in the fatness of these pursy times<br>Virtue itself of vice must pardon beg,<br>Yea, curb and woo for leave to do him good. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O [[Hamlet]], thou hast cleft my heart in twain! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, throw away the worser part of it,<br>And live the purer with the other half!<br>Good [[Night\|night]]—but go not to mine uncle’s bed.<br>Assume a virtue if you have it not.<br>Refrain tonight,<br>And that shall lend a kind of easiness<br>To the next abstinence. Once more, good [[Night\|night]];<br>And when you are desirous to be blest,<br>I’ll blessing beg of you. For this same lord,<br>I do repent. But heaven hath pleased it so<br>To punish me with this, and this with me,<br>That I must be their scourge and minister.<br>I will bestow him, and will answer [[Well\|well]]<br>The [[Death\|death]] I gave him. So, again, good [[Night\|night]].<br>I must be cruel only to be kind.<br>Thus bad begins, and worse remains behind. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | What shall I do? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Not this, by no means, that I bid you do:<br>Let the bloat [[King]] tempt you again to bed,<br>Pinch wanton on your cheek, call you his mouse,<br>And let him for a pair of reechy kisses,<br>Or paddling in your neck with his damned fingers,<br>Make you to ravel all this matter out,<br>That I essentially am not in [[Madness\|madness]],<br>But mad in craft. ’Twere good you let him know,<br>For who that’s but a [[Queen\|queen]], fair, sober, wise,<br>Would from a paddock, from a bat, a gib,<br>Such dear concernings hide? Who would do so?<br>No, in despite of sense and secrecy,<br>Unpeg the basket on the [[house]]’s top,<br>Let the [[Birds\|birds]] fly, and, like the famous ape,<br>To try conclusions in the basket creep,<br>And break your own neck down. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Be thou assured, if words be made of breath,<br>And breath of life, I have no life to breathe<br>What thou hast said to me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I must to England.<br>You know that? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alack, I had forgot.<br>’Tis so concluded on. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | This man shall set me packing.<br>I’ll lug the guts into the neighbour room.<br>Mother, good [[Night\|night]] indeed. This counsellor<br>Is now most still, most secret, and most [[Grave\|grave]],<br>Who was in life a foolish prating knave.—<br>Come, sir, to draw toward an end with you.—<br>Good [[Night\|night]], mother. Exit, tugging in [[Polonius]] |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] to [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | There’s matter in these sighs, these profound heaves;<br>You must translate. ’Tis fit we understand them.<br>Where is your son? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Ah, my good lord, what have I seen tonight! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What, [[Gertrude]]? How does [[Hamlet]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Mad as the sea and [[Wind\|wind]] when both contend<br>Which is the mightier. In his lawless fit,<br>Behind the arras hearing something stir,<br>He whips his rapier out and cries ‘A rat, a ratl’,<br>And in his brainish apprehension kills<br>The unseen good old man. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O heavy deed!<br>It had been so with us had we been there.<br>His liberty is full of threats to all—<br>To you yourself, to us, to everyone.<br>Alas, how shall this bloody deed be answered?<br>It will be laid to us, whose [[Providence\|providence]]<br>Should have kept short, restrained, and out of haunt<br>This mad young man. But so much was our [[Love\|love]],<br>We would not understand what was most fit,<br>But, like the owner of a foul disease,<br>To keep it from divulging, let it feed<br>Even on the pith of life. Where is he gone? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | To draw apart the [[Body\|body]] he hath killed,<br>O’er whom—his very [[Madness\|madness]], like some ore<br>Among a mineral of metals base,<br>Shows itself pure—a weeps for what is done. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O [[Gertrude]], come away!<br>The sun no sooner shall the [[Mountains\|mountains]] touch<br>But we will [[Ship\|ship]] him hence; and this vile deed<br>We must with all our majesty and skill<br>Both countenance and excuse.—Ho, Guildensternl |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Friends both, go join you with some further aid.<br>[[Hamlet]] in [[Madness\|madness]] hath [[Polonius]] slain,<br>And from his mother’s closet hath he dragged him.<br>Go seek him out, speak fair, and bring the [[Body\|body]]<br>Into the chapel. I pray you haste in this. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Come, [[Gertrude]], we’ll call up our wisest friends<br>To let them know both what we mean to do<br>And what’s untimely done. O, come away!<br>My [[Soul\|soul]] is full of discord and dismay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Safely stowed. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | and [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] (within)<br>[[Hamlet]], Lord [[Hamlet]]! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What noise? Who calls on [[Hamlet]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, here they come. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | What have you done, my lord, with the dead [[Body\|body]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Compounded it with [[Dust\|dust]], whereto ’tis kin. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Tell us where ’tis, that we may take it thence<br>And bear it to the chapel. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Do not believe it. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Believe what? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That I can keep your counsel and not mine own. Besides, to be demanded of a sponge—what replication should be made by the son of a [[King\|king]]? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Take you me for a sponge, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, sir, that soaks up the [[King]]’s countenance, his rewards, his authorities. But such officers do the [[King]] best service in the end. He keeps them, like an ape an apple in the corner of his jaw, first mouthed to be last swallowed. When he needs what you have gleaned, it is but squeezing you, and, sponge, you shall be dry again. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | I understand you not, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am glad of it. A knavish speech sleeps in a foolish ear. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | My lord, you must tell us where the [[Body\|body]] is, and go with us to the [[King]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The [[Body\|body]] is with the [[King]], but the [[King]] is not with the [[Body\|body]]. The [[King]] is a thing— |
| [[Guildenstern\|GUILDENSTERN]] | A thing, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Of nothing. Bring me to him. Hide fox, and all after. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit running, pursued by the others |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I have sent to seek him, and to find the [[Body\|body]].<br>How dangerous is it that this man goes loose!<br>Yet must not we put the strong law on him.<br>He’s loved of the distracted multitude,<br>Who like not in their judgement but their eyes,<br>And where ‘tis so, th’offender’s scourge is weighed,<br>But never the offence. To bear all smooth and even,<br>This sudden sending him away must seem<br>Deliberate pause. Diseases desperate grown<br>By desperate appliance are relieved,<br>Or not at all. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Rosencrantz]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How now, what hath befall’n? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Where the dead [[Body\|body]] is bestowed, my lord,<br>We cannot get from him. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | But where is he? |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Without, my lord, guarded to know your pleasure. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Bring him before us. |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Ho, [[Guildenstern]]! Bring in my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] and [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Now, [[Hamlet]], where’s [[Polonius]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | At supper. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | At supper? Where? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Not where he eats, but where a is eaten. A certain convocation of politic worms are e’en at him. Your worm is your only emperor for diet. We fat all creatures else to fat us, and we fat ourselves for maggots. Your fat [[King\|king]] and your lean beggar is but variable service—two dishes, but to one table. That’s the end. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Alas, alas! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A man may fish with the worm that hath eat of a [[King\|king]], and eat of the fish that hath fed of that worm. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What dost thou mean by this? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nothing but to show you how a [[King\|king]] may go a progress through the guts of a beggar. 31 |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Where is [[Polonius]]? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | In heaven. Send thither to see. If your messenger find him not there, seek him i’th’ other place yourself. But indeed, if you find him not this month, you shall nose him as you go up the stairs into the lobby. 36 |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] to [[Rosencrantz]] Go seek him there. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] to [[Rosencrantz]] A will stay till ye come. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Rosencrantz]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Hamlet]], this deed of thine, for thine especial safety—<br>Which we do tender as we dearly grieve<br>For that which thou hast done—must send thee hence<br>With fiery quickness. Therefore prepare thyself.<br>The barque is ready, and the [[Wind\|wind]] at help,<br>Th’associates tend, and everything is bent<br>For England. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | For England? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Ay, [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Good. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | So is it if thou knew’st our purposes. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I see a cherub that sees them. But come, for<br>England. Farewell, dear mother. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Thy loving father, [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | My mother. Father and mother is man and wife, man and wife is one flesh, and so my mother. Come, for England. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] to [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Follow him at foot. Tempt him with [[Speed\|speed]] aboard. 56<br>Delay it not. I’ll have him hence tonight.<br>Away, for everything is sealed and done<br>That else leans on th’affair. Pray you, make haste. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And, England, if my [[Love\|love]] thou hold‘st at aught—<br>As my great power thereof may give thee sense,<br>Since yet thy cicatrice looks raw and red<br>After the Danish [[Sword\|sword]], and thy free awe<br>Pays homage to us—thou mayst not coldly set<br>Our sovereign process, which imports at full,<br>By letters conjuring to that effect,<br>The present [[Death\|death]] of [[Hamlet]]. Do it, England,<br>For like the hectic in my [[Blood\|blood]] he rages,<br>And thou must cure me. Till I know ’tis done,<br>Howe‘er my haps, my joys were ne’er begun. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 4, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Fortinbras with an army over the stage |
| FORTINBRAS | Go, captain, from me greet the Danish [[King\|king]].<br>Tell him that by his licence Fortinbras<br>Claims the conveyance of a promised march<br>Over his kingdom. You know the rendezvous.<br>If that his majesty would aught with us,<br>We shall express our duty in his eye,<br>And let him know so. |
| CAPTAIN | I will do’t, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| FORTINBRAS | Go safely on. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt marching |

### Atto 4, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] and [[Horatio]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I will not speak with her. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | She is importunate,<br>Indeed distraught. Her mood will needs be pitied. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | What would she have? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | She speaks much of her father, says she hears<br>There’s tricks i’th’ world, and hems, and beats her<br>heart,<br>Spurns enviously at straws, speaks things in [[Doubt\|doubt]]<br>That carry but half sense. Her speech is nothing,<br>Yet the unshapèd use of it doth move<br>The hearers to collection. They aim at it,<br>And botch the words up fit to their own thoughts,<br>Which, as her winks and nods and gestures yield them,<br>Indeed would make one think there might be thought,<br>Though nothing sure, yet much unhappily. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | ’Twere good she were spoken with, for she may strew<br>Dangerous conjectures in ill-breeding minds.<br>Let her come in. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Horatio]] withdraws to admit [[Ophelia]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | To my sick [[Soul\|soul]], as sin’s true [[Nature\|nature]] is,<br>Each toy seems prologue to some great amiss.<br>So full of artless [[Jealousy\|jealousy]] is [[Guilt\|guilt]],<br>It spills itself in fearing to be spilt. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Ophelia]] mad, her hair down, with a lute |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Where is the beauteous majesty of [[Denmark]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | How now, [[Ophelia]]? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>How should I your true [[Love\|love]] know<br>From another one?—<br>By his cockle hat and staff,<br>And his sandal shoon. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, sweet lady, what imports this [[Song\|song]]? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Say you? Nay, pray you, mark. (Sings)<br>He is dead and gone, lady,<br>He is dead and gone.<br>At his head a grass-green turf,<br>At his heels a stone. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Nay, but [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Pray you, mark.<br>(Sings)<br>White his shroud as the mountain [[Snow\|snow]]— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, look here, my lord. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>Larded with sweet [[Flowers\|flowers]],<br>Which bewept to the [[Grave\|grave]] did—not—go<br>With true-[[Love\|love]] showers. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How do ye, pretty lady? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | [[Well]], God’ield you. They say the owl was a baker’s daughter. Lord, we know what we are, but know not what we may be. God be at your table! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) Conceit upon her father. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Pray you, let’s have no words of this, but when they ask you what it means, say you this. (Sings)<br>Tomorrow is Saint [[Valentine]]’s day,<br>All in the morning betime,<br>And I a maid at your [[Window\|window]]<br>To be your [[Valentine]].<br>Then up he [[Rose\|rose]], and donned his clothes,<br>And dupped the chamber door;<br>Let in the maid, that out a maid<br>Never departed more. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Pretty [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Indeed, la? Without an oath, I’ll make an end on’t.<br>(Sings) By Gis, and by Saint [[Charity]],<br>Alack, and fie for shame!<br>Young men will do’t if they come to‘t,<br>By Cock, they are to blame.<br>Quoth she ‘Before you tumbled me,<br>You promised me to wed.’<br>So would I ‘a’ done, by yonder sun,<br>An thou hadst not come to my bed. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) How long hath she been thus? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I [[Hope\|hope]] all will be [[Well\|well]]. We must be patient. But I cannot choose but weep to think they should lay him i’th’ cold ground. My brother shall know of it. And so I thank you for your good counsel. Come, my coach! Good [[Night\|night]], ladies, good [[Night\|night]], sweet ladies, good [[Night\|night]], good [[Night\|night]]. Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Horatio]])<br>Follow her close. Give her good watch, I pray you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Horatio]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, this is the [[Poison\|poison]] of deep grief! It springs<br>All from her father’s [[Death\|death]]. O [[Gertrude]], [[Gertrude]],<br>When sorrows come they come not single spies,<br>But in battalions. First, her father slain;<br>Next, your son gone, and he most violent author<br>Of his own just remove; the people muddied,<br>Thick and unwholesome in their thoughts and whispers<br>For good [[Polonius]]’ [[Death\|death]]; and we have done but<br>greenly<br>In hugger-mugger to inter him; poor [[Ophelia]]<br>Divided from herself and her fair judgement,<br>Without the which we are pictures or mere beasts;<br>Last, and as much containing as all these,<br>Her brother is in secret come from France,<br>Feeds on this [[Wonder\|wonder]], keeps himself in clouds,<br>And wants not buzzers to infect his ear<br>With pestilent speeches of his father’s [[Death\|death]];<br>Wherein necessity, of matter beggared,<br>Will nothing stick our persons to arraign<br>In ear and ear. O my dear [[Gertrude]], this,<br>Like to a murd’ring-piece, in many places<br>Gives me superfluous [[Death\|death]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alack, what noise is this? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Where is my Switzers? Let them guard the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What is the matter? |
| MESSENGER | Save yourself, my lord.<br>The ocean, overpeering of his list,<br>Eats not the flats with more impetuous haste<br>Than young [[Laertes]], in a riotous head,<br>O‘erbears your officers. The rabble call him lord,<br>And, as the world were now but to begin,<br>Antiquity forgot, custom not known,<br>The ratifiers and props of every word,<br>They cry ‘Choose we! [[Laertes]] shall be [[King\|king]].’<br>Caps, hands, and tongues applaud it to the clouds,<br>‘[[Laertes]] shall be [[King\|king]], [[Laertes]] [[King\|king]].’ |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | How cheerfully on the false trail they cry! |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O, this is counter, you false Danish [[dogs]]! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | The doors are broke. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Laertes]] with his followers at the door |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Where is the [[King]]?—Sirs, stand you all without. |
| ALL HIS FOLLOWERS | No, let’s come in. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I pray you, give me leave. |
| ALL HIS FOLLOWERS | We will, we will. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I thank you. Keep the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | exeunt followers |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O thou vile [[King\|king]],<br>Give me my father. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Calmly, good [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | That drop of [[Blood\|blood]] that’s calm proclaims me bastard,<br>Cries cuckold to my father, brands the harlot<br>Even here between the chaste unsmirchèd brow<br>Of my true mother. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What is the cause, [[Laertes]],<br>That thy rebellion looks so giant-like?—<br>Let him go, [[Gertrude]]. Do not [[Fear\|fear]] our person.<br>There’s such divinity doth hedge a [[King\|king]]<br>That treason can but peep to what it would,<br>Acts little of his will.—Tell me, [[Laertes]],<br>Why thou art thus incensed.—Let him go, [[Gertrude]].—<br>Speak, man. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Where is my father? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Dead. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>But not by him. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let him demand his fill. |
