| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | How came he dead? I’ll not be juggled with.<br>To hell, allegiance! Vows to the blackest devil!<br>[[Conscience]] and grace to the profoundest pit!<br>I dare damnation. To this point I stand,<br>That both the worlds I give to negligence,<br>Let come what comes. Only I’ll be revenged<br>Most throughly for my father. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Who shall stay you? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | My will, not all the world;<br>And for my means, I’ll husband them so [[Well\|well]]<br>They shall go far with little. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Good [[Laertes]],<br>If you [[Desire\|desire]] to know the certainty<br>Of your dear father’s [[Death\|death]], is’t writ in your [[Revenge\|revenge]]<br>That, sweepstake, you will draw both friend and foe,<br>Winner and loser? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | None but his enemies. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Will you know them then? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | To his good friends thus wide I’ll ope my arms,<br>And, like the kind life-rend’ring pelican,<br>Repast them with my [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Why, now you speak<br>Like a good [[Child\|child]] and a true gentleman.<br>That I am guiltless of your father’s [[Death\|death]],<br>And am most sensibly in grief for it,<br>It shall as level to your judgement pierce<br>As day does to your eye. |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| VOICES | (within) Let her come in. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | How now, what noise is that? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Ophelia]] as before |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O heat dry up my brains! [[Tears]] seven times salt<br>Burn out the sense and virtue of mine eye!<br>By heaven, thy [[Madness\|madness]] shall be paid by weight<br>Till our scale turns the beam. O [[Rose\|rose]] of May,<br>Dear maid, kind sister, sweet [[Ophelia]]!<br>O heavens, is’t possible a young maid’s wits<br>Should be as mortal as an old man’s life?<br>[[Nature]] is fine in [[Love\|love]], and where ’tis fine<br>It sends some precious instance of itself<br>After the thing it loves. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>They bore him barefaced on the bier,<br>Hey non nony, nony, hey nony,<br>And on his [[Grave\|grave]] rained many a tear—<br>Fare you [[Well\|well]], my dove. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Hadst thou thy wits and didst persuade [[Revenge\|revenge]],<br>It could not move thus. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You must sing ‘Down, a-down’, and you, ‘Call him a-down-a’. O, how the [[Wheel\|wheel]] becomes it! It is the false steward that stole his master’s daughter. [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] This nothing’s more than matter. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | There’s rosemary, that’s for remembrance. Pray, [[Love\|love]], remember. And there is pansies; that’s for thoughts. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | A document in [[Madness\|madness]]—thoughts and remembrance fitted. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | There’s fennel for you, and columbines. There’s rue for you, and here’s some for me. We may call it herb-grace o’ Sundays. O, you must wear your rue with a difference. There’s a daisy. I would give you some violets, but they withered all when my father died. They say a made a good end.<br>(Sings) For bonny sweet Robin is all my joy. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Thought and affliction, passion, hell itself<br>She turns to favour and to prettiness. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>And will a not come again,<br>And will a not come again?<br>No, no, he is dead,<br>Go to thy [[Death\|death]]-bed,<br>He never will come again.<br>His beard as white as [[Snow\|snow]],<br>All flaxen was his poll.<br>He is gone, he is gone,<br>And we cast away moan.<br>God ‘a’ [[Mercy\|mercy]] on his [[Soul\|soul]].<br>And of all Christian souls, I pray God. God b’wi’ ye. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Ophelia]] and [[Gertrude]] |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Do you see this, O God? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], I must commune with your grief,<br>Or you deny me right. Go but apart,<br>Make choice of whom your wisest friends you will,<br>And they shall hear and judge ’twixt you and me.<br>If by direct or by collateral hand<br>They find us touched, we will our kingdom give,<br>Our [[Crown\|crown]], our life, and all that we call ours,<br>To you in satisfaction. But if not,<br>Be you content to lend your patience to us,<br>And we shall jointly labour with your [[Soul\|soul]]<br>To give it due content. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Let this be so.<br>His means of [[Death\|death]], his obscure burial—<br>No trophy, [[Sword\|sword]], nor hatchment o‘er his bones,<br>No noble rite nor formal ostentation—<br>Cry to be heard, as ’twere from heaven to earth,<br>That I must call’t in question. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | So you shall;<br>And where th’offence is, let the great axe fall.<br>I pray you go with me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]] with a Servant |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What are they that would speak with me? |
| SERVANT | Sailors, sir. They say they have letters for you. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Let them come in. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Servant |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I do not know from what part of the world<br>I should be greeted if not from Lord [[Hamlet]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Sailors |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A SAILOR God bless you, sir. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Let him bless thee too.<br>A SAILOR A shall, sir, an’t please him. There’s a letter for you, sir. It comes from th’ambassador that was bound for England—if your name be [[Horatio]], as I am let to know it is. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (reads) ‘[[Horatio]], when thou shalt have overlooked this, give these fellows some means to the [[King]]. They have letters for him. Ere we were two days old at sea, a pirate of very warlike appointment gave us chase. Finding ourselves too slow of sail, we put on a compelled valour, and in the grapple I boarded them. On the instant they got clear of our [[Ship\|ship]], so I [[Alone\|alone]] became their prisoner. They have dealt with me like thieves of [[Mercy\|mercy]]; but they knew what they did: I am to do a good turn for them. Let the [[King]] have the letters I have sent, and repair thou to me with as much haste as thou wouldst fly [[Death\|death]]. I have words to speak in thine ear will make thee dumb, yet are they much too light for the bore of the matter. These good fellows will bring thee where I am. [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] hold their course for England. Of them I have much to tell thee. Farewell.<br>He that thou knowest thine,<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>Come, I will give you way for these your letters,<br>And do’t the speedier that you may direct me<br>To him from whom you brought them. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] and [[Laertes]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Now must your [[Conscience\|conscience]] my acquittance seal,<br>And you must put me in your heart for friend,<br>Sith you have heard, and with a knowing ear,<br>That he which hath your noble father slain<br>Pursued my life. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | It [[Well\|well]] appears. But tell me<br>Why you proceeded not against these feats,<br>So crimeful and so capital in [[Nature\|nature]],<br>As by your safety, wisdom, all things else,<br>You mainly were stirred up. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, for two special reasons,<br>Which may to you perhaps seem much unsinewed,<br>And yet to me they’re strong. The [[Queen]] his mother<br>Lives almost by his looks; and for myself—<br>My virtue or my plague, be it either which—<br>She’s so conjunctive to my life and [[Soul\|soul]]<br>That, as the star moves not but in his sphere,<br>I could not but by her. The other motive<br>Why to a public count I might not go<br>Is the great [[Love\|love]] the general gender bear him,<br>Who, dipping all his faults in their affection,<br>Would, like the [[Spring\|spring]] that turneth wood to stone,<br>[[convert\|Convert]] his guilts to graces; so that my arrows,<br>Too slightly timbered for so loud a [[Wind\|wind]],<br>Would have reverted to my bow again,<br>And not where I had aimed them. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | And so have I a noble father lost,<br>A sister driven into desp’rate terms,<br>Who has, if praises may go back again,<br>Stood challenger, on mount, of all the age<br>For her perfections. But my [[Revenge\|revenge]] will come. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Break not your sleeps for that. You must not think<br>That we are made of stuff so flat and dull<br>That we can let our beard be shook with danger,<br>And think it pastime. You shortly shall hear more.<br>I loved your father, and we [[Love\|love]] ourself.<br>And that, I [[Hope\|hope]], will teach you to imagine— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger with letters |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How now? What news? |
| MESSENGER | Letters, my lord, from [[Hamlet]].<br>This to your majesty; this to the [[Queen]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | From [[Hamlet]]? Who brought them? |
| MESSENGER | Sailors, my lord, they say. I saw them not.<br>They were given me by [[Claudio]]. He received them. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], you shall hear them.—Leave us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Messenger |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (Reads) ‘High and mighty, you shall know I am set naked on your kingdom. Tomorrow shall I beg leave to see your kingly eyes, when I shall, first asking your pardon, thereunto recount th’occasions of my sudden and more strange return.<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>What should this mean? Are all the rest come back? Or is it some abuse, and no such thing? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Know you the hand? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | ’Tis [[Hamlet]]’s character.<br>‘Naked’—and in a postscript here he says<br>‘[[Alone]]’. Can you advise me? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I’m lost in it, my lord. But let him come.<br>It warms the very sickness in my heart<br>That I shall live and tell him to his teeth,<br>‘Thus diddest thou’. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | If it be so, [[Laertes]]—<br>As how should it be so, how otherwise?—<br>Will you be ruled by me? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | If so you’ll not o’errule me to a peace. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | To thine own peace. If he be now returned,<br>As checking at his voyage, and that he means<br>No more to undertake it, I will work him<br>To an exploit, now ripe in my device,<br>Under the which he shall not choose but fall;<br>And for his [[Death\|death]] no [[Wind\|wind]] of blame shall breathe;<br>But even his mother shall uncharge the practice<br>And call it accident. Some two months since<br>Here was a gentleman of Normandy.<br>I’ve seen myself, and served against, the French,<br>And they can [[Well\|well]] on horseback; but this gallant<br>Had witchcraft in’t. He grew into his seat,<br>And to such wondrous doing brought his [[Horse\|horse]]<br>As had he been incorpsed and demi-natured<br>With the brave beast. So far he passed my thought<br>That I in forgery of shapes and tricks<br>Come short of what he did. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | A Norman was’t? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | A Norman. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Upon my life, Lamord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | The very same. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I know him [[Well\|well]]. He is the brooch indeed,<br>And gem, of all the nation. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | He made confession of you,<br>And gave you such a masterly report<br>For art and exercise in your defence,<br>And for your rapier most especially,<br>That he cried out ‘twould be a sight indeed<br>If one could match you. Sir, this report of his<br>Did [[Hamlet]] so envenom with his envy<br>That he could nothing do but wish and beg<br>Your sudden coming o’er to play with him.<br>Now, out of this— |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What out of this, my lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], was your father dear to you?<br>Or are you like the painting of a sorrow,<br>A face without a heart? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Why ask you this? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Not that I think you did not [[Love\|love]] your father,<br>But that I know [[Love\|love]] is begun by [[Time\|time]],<br>And that I see, in passages of proof,<br>[[Time]] qualifies the spark and [[Fire\|fire]] of it.<br>[[Hamlet]] comes back. What would you undertake<br>To show yourself your father’s son in deed<br>More than in words? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | To cut his throat i’th’ church. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | No place indeed should murder sanctuarize.<br>[[Revenge]] should have no bounds. But, good [[Laertes]],<br>Will you do this?—keep close within your chamber.<br>[[Hamlet]] returned shall know you are come [[Home\|home]].<br>We’ll put on those shall praise your excellence,<br>And set a [[Double\|double]] varnish on the fame<br>The Frenchman gave you; bring you, in fine, together,<br>And wager on your heads. He, being remiss,<br>Most generous, and free from all contriving,<br>Will not peruse the foils; so that with ease,<br>Or with a little shuffling, you may choose<br>A [[Sword\|sword]] unbated, and, in a pass of practice,<br>Requite him for your father. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I will do’t,<br>And for that purpose I’ll anoint my [[Sword\|sword]].<br>I bought an unction of a mountebank<br>So mortal that, but dip a knife in it,<br>Where it draws [[Blood\|blood]] no cataplasm so rare,<br>Collected from all simples that have virtue<br>Under the [[Moon\|moon]], can save the thing from [[Death\|death]]<br>That is but scratched withal. I’ll touch my point<br>With this contagion, that if I gall him slightly,<br>It may be [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let’s further think of this;<br>Weigh what convenience both of [[Time\|time]] and means<br>May fit us to our shape. If this should fail,<br>And that our drift look through our bad performance,<br>‘Twere better not essayed. Therefore this project<br>Should have a back or second that might hold<br>If this should blast in proof. Soft, let me see.<br>We’ll make a solemn wager on your cunnings ...<br>I ha’t! When in your motion you are hot and dry—<br>As make your bouts more violent to that end—<br>And that he calls for drink, I’ll have prepared him<br>A chalice for the nonce, whereon but sipping,<br>If he by chance escape your venomed stuck,<br>Our purpose may hold there.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How now, sweet [[Queen]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | One woe doth tread upon another’s heel,<br>So fast they follow. Your sister’s drowned, [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Drowned? O, where? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | There is a willow grows aslant a brook<br>That shows his hoar [[Leaves\|leaves]] in the glassy stream.<br>Therewith fantastic garlands did she make<br>Of crow-[[Flowers\|flowers]], nettles, daisies, and long purples,<br>That liberal shepherds give a grosser name,<br>But our cold maids do dead men’s fingers call them.<br>There on the pendent boughs her crownet weeds<br>Clamb’ring to hang, an envious sliver broke,<br>When down the weedy trophies and herself<br>Fell in the weeping brook. Her clothes spread wide,<br>And [[Mermaid\|mermaid]]-like a while they bore her up;<br>Which [[Time\|time]] she chanted snatches of old tunes,<br>As one incapable of her own distress,<br>Or like a creature native and endued<br>Unto that element. But long it could not be<br>Till that her garments, heavy with their drink,<br>Pulled the poor wretch from her melodious lay<br>To muddy [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Alas, then is she drowned. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Drowned, drowned. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Too much of [[Water\|water]] hast thou, poor [[Ophelia]],<br>And therefore I forbid my [[Tears\|tears]]. But yet<br>It is our trick; [[Nature\|nature]] her custom holds,<br>Let shame say what it will.<br>He weeps<br>When these are gone,<br>The woman will be out. Adieu, my lord.<br>I have a speech of [[Fire\|fire]] that fain would blaze,<br>But that this folly douts it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let’s follow, [[Gertrude]].<br>How much I had to do to calm his rage!<br>Now [[Fear\|fear]] I this will give it start again;<br>Therefore let’s follow. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter two Clowns carrying a spade and a pickaxe |
| FIRST CLOWN | Is she to be buried in Christian burial that wilfully seeks her own salvation? |
| SECOND CLOWN | I tell thee she is, and therefore make her [[Grave\|grave]] straight. The coroner hath sat on her, and finds it Christian burial. |
| FIRST CLOWN | How can that be unless she drowned herself in her own defence? |
| SECOND CLOWN | Why, ’tis found so. |
| FIRST CLOWN | It must be se offendendo, it cannot be else; for here lies the point: if I drown myself wittingly, it argues an act; and an act hath three branches: it is to act, to do, and to perform. Argal she drowned herself wittingly. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Nay, but hear you, Goodman Delver. |
| FIRST CLOWN | Give me leave. Here lies the [[Water\|water]]—good. Here stands the man—good. If the man go to this [[Water\|water]] and drown himself, it is, will he nill he, he goes. Mark you that. But if the [[Water\|water]] come to him and drown him, he drowns not himself; argal he that is not guilty of his own [[Death\|death]] shortens not his own life. |
| SECOND CLOWN | But is this law? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Ay, marry, is’t: coroner’s [[Quest\|quest]] law. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Will you ha’ the [[Truth\|truth]] on’t? If this had not been a gentlewoman, she should have been buried out o’ Christian burial. |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, there thou sayst, and the more pity that great folk should have count’nance in this world to drown or hang themselves more than their even Christian. Come, my spade. There is no ancient gentlemen but gardeners, ditchers, and gravemakers; they hold up Adam’s profession. |
| *(didascalia)* | First Clown digs |
| SECOND CLOWN | Was he a gentleman? |
| FIRST CLOWN | A was the first that ever bore arms. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Why, he had none. |
| FIRST CLOWN | What, art a heathen? How dost thou understand the Scripture? The Scripture says Adam digged. Could he dig without arms? I’ll put another question to thee. If thou answerest me not to the purpose, confess thyself— |
| SECOND CLOWN | Go to. |
| FIRST CLOWN | What is he that builds stronger than either the mason, the shipwright, or the carpenter? |
| SECOND CLOWN | The gallows-maker; for that frame outlives a thousand tenants. |
| FIRST CLOWN | I like thy wit [[Well\|well]], in good [[Faith\|faith]]. The gallows does [[Well\|well]]. But how does it [[Well\|well]]? It does [[Well\|well]] to those that do ill. Now thou dost ill to say the gallows is built stronger than the church, argal the gallows may do [[Well\|well]] to thee. To’t again, come. |
| SECOND CLOWN | ‘Who builds stronger than a mason, a shipwright, or a carpenter?’ |
| FIRST CLOWN | Ay, tell me that, and unyoke. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Marry, now I can tell. |
| FIRST CLOWN | To’t. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Mass, I cannot tell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] afar off |
| FIRST CLOWN | Cudgel thy brains no more about it, for your dull ass will not mend his pace with beating; and when you are asked this question next, say ‘a [[Grave\|grave]]-maker’; the houses that he makes lasts till doomsday. Go, get thee to Johan. Fetch me a stoup of liquor. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Second Clown |
| FIRST CLOWN | (Sings)<br>In youth when I did [[Love\|love]], did [[Love\|love]],<br>Methought it was very sweet<br>To contract-O-the [[Time\|time]] for-a-my behove,<br>O methought there-a-was nothing-a-meet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Has this fellow no feeling of his business that a sings at [[Grave\|grave]]-making? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Custom hath made it in him a property of easiness. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ‘Tis e’en so; the hand of little employment hath the daintier sense. |
| FIRST CLOWN | (sings)<br>But age with his stealing steps<br>Hath caught me in his clutch,<br>And hath shipped me intil the land,<br>As if I had never been such. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws up a skull |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That skull had a tongue in it and could sing once. How the knave jowls it to th’ ground as if ‘twere [[Cain]]’s jawbone, that did the first murder! This might be the pate of a politician which this ass o’er-offices, one that would circumvent God, might it not? [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] It might, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Or of a courtier, which could say ‘Good morrow, sweet lord. How dost thou, good lord?’ This might be my lord such a one, that praised my lord such a one’s [[Horse\|horse]] when a meant to beg it, might it not? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, e‘en so, and now my lady Worm’s, chapless, and knocked about the mazard with a sexton’s spade. Here’s fine revolution, an we had the trick to see’t. Did these bones cost no more the breeding but to play at loggats with ’em? Mine ache to think on’t. |
| FIRST CLOWN | (sings)<br>A pickaxe and a spade, a spade,<br>For and a shrouding-sheet;<br>O, a pit of clay for to be made<br>For such a guest is meet. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws up another skull |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | There’s another. Why might not that be the skull of a lawyer? Where be his quiddits now, his quillets, his cases, his tenures, and his tricks? Why does he suffer this rude knave now to knock him about the sconce with a dirty shovel, and will not tell him of his action of battery? H‘m! This fellow might be in ’s [[Time\|time]] a great buyer of land, with his statutes, his recognizances, his fines, his [[Double\|double]] vouchers, his recoveries. Is this the fine of his fines and the recovery of his recoveries, to have his fine pate full of fine dirt? Will his vouchers vouch him no more of his purchases, and [[Double\|double]] ones too, than the length and breadth of a pair of indentures? The very conveyances of his lands will hardly lie in this box; and must th’inheritor himself have no more, ha? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Not a jot more, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Is not parchment made of sheepskins? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Ay, my lord, and of calf-skins too. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | They are sheep and calves that seek out assurance in that. I will speak to this fellow. (To the First Clown) Whose [[Grave\|grave]]’s this, sirrah? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Mine, sir.<br>(Sings)<br>O, a pit of clay for to be made<br>For such a guest is meet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I think it be thine indeed, for thou liest in’t. |
| FIRST CLOWN | You lie out on‘t, sir, and therefore it is not yours. For my part, I do not lie in’t, and yet it is mine. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thou dost lie in‘t, to be in’t and say ’tis thine. ’Tis for the dead, not for the quick; therefore thou liest. |
| FIRST CLOWN | ‘Tis a quick lie, sir, ’twill away again from me to you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What man dost thou dig it for? |
| FIRST CLOWN | For no man, sir. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What woman, then? |
| FIRST CLOWN | For none, neither. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Who is to be buried in’t? |
| FIRST CLOWN | One that was a woman, sir; but, rest her [[Soul\|soul]], she’s dead. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How absolute the knave is! We must speak by the card, or equivocation will undo us. By the Lord, [[Horatio]], these three years I have taken note of it. The age is grown so picked that the toe of the peasant comes so near the heel of the courtier he galls his kibe. (To the First Clown) How long hast thou been a [[Grave\|grave]]-maker? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Of all the days i‘th’ year I came to’t that day that our last [[King]] [[Hamlet]] o’ercame Fortinbras. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How long is that since? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Cannot you tell that? Every [[Fool\|fool]] can tell that. It was the very day that young [[Hamlet]] was born—he that was mad and sent into England. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, marry, why was he sent into England? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, because a was mad. A shall recover his wits there; or if a do not, ’tis no great matter there. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why? |
| FIRST CLOWN | ’Twill not be seen in him there. There the men are as mad as he. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How came he mad? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Very strangely, they say. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How strangely? |
| FIRST CLOWN | [[Faith]], e’en with losing his wits. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Upon what ground? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, here in [[Denmark]]. I have been sexton here, man and boy, thirty years. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How long will a man lie i’th’ earth ere he rot? |
| FIRST CLOWN | I’[[Faith\|faith]], if a be not rotten before a die—as we have many pocky corpses nowadays, that will scarce hold the laying in—a will last you some eight year or nine year. A tanner will last you nine year. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why he more than another? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, sir, his hide is so tanned with his trade that a will keep out [[Water\|water]] a great while, and your [[Water\|water]] is a sore decayer of your whoreson dead [[Body\|body]]. Here’s a skull, now. This skull has lain in the earth three-and-twenty years. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Whose was it? |
| FIRST CLOWN | A whoreson mad fellow’s it was. Whose do you think it was? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, I know not. |
| FIRST CLOWN | A pestilence on him for a mad rogue—a poured a flagon of Rhenish on my head once! This same skull, sir, was Yorick’s skull, the [[King]]’s jester. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | This? |
| FIRST CLOWN | E’en that. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Let me see.<br>He takes the skull<br>Alas, poor Yorick. I knew him, [[Horatio]]—a fellow of infinite jest, of most excellent fancy. He hath borne me on his back a thousand times; and now, how abhorred my [[Imagination\|imagination]] is! My gorge rises at it. Here hung those lips that I have kissed I know not how oft. Where be your gibes now, your gambols, your [[songs]], your flashes of merriment that were wont to set the table on a roar? Not one now to mock your own grinning? Quite chop-fallen? Now get you to my lady’s chamber and tell her, let her paint an inch thick, to this favour she must come. Make her laugh at that. Prithee, [[Horatio]], tell me one thing. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What’s that, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Dost thou think Alexander looked o’ this fashion i’th’ earth? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | E’en so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And smelt so? Pah! |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws the skull down |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | E’en so, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | To what base uses we may return, [[Horatio]]! Why may not [[Imagination\|imagination]] trace the noble [[Dust\|dust]] of Alexander till a find it stopping a bung-hole? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | ’Twere to consider too curiously to consider so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, [[Faith\|faith]], not a jot; but to follow him thither with modesty enough, and likelihood to lead it, as thus: Alexander died, Alexander was buried, Alexander returneth into [[Dust\|dust]], the [[Dust\|dust]] is earth, of earth we make loam, and why of that loam whereto he was converted might they not stop a beer-barrel?<br>Imperial Caesar, dead and turned to clay,<br>Might stop a hole to keep the [[Wind\|wind]] away.<br>O, that that earth which kept the world in awe<br>Should patch a wall t’expel the [[Winter\|winter]]’s flaw!<br>But soft, but soft; aside.<br>[[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] stand aside. Enter [[King]]<br><br>[[Claudius]], [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]], [[Laertes]], and a coffin,<br><br>with a Priest and lords attendant<br>Here comes the [[King]],<br>The [[Queen]], the courtiers—who is that they follow,<br>And with such maimed rites? This doth betoken<br>The corpse they follow did with desp‘rate hand<br>Fordo it own life. ’Twas of some estate.<br>Couch we a while, and mark. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What ceremony else? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside to [[Horatio]])<br>That is [[Laertes]], a very noble youth. Mark. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What ceremony else? |
| PRIEST | Her obsequies have been as far enlarged<br>As we have warrantise. Her [[Death\|death]] was doubtful,<br>And but that great command o’ersways the order<br>She should in ground unsanctified have lodged<br>Till the last trumpet. For charitable prayers,<br>Shards, flints, and pebbles should be thrown on her,<br>Yet here she is allowed her virgin rites,<br>Her maiden strewments, and the bringing [[Home\|home]]<br>Of bell and burial. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Must there no more be done? |
| PRIEST | No more be done.<br>We should profane the service of the dead<br>To sing sage requiem and such rest to her<br>As to peace-parted souls. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Lay her i’th’ earth,<br>And from her fair and unpolluted flesh<br>May violets [[Spring\|spring]]. I tell thee, churlish priest,<br>A minist’ring angel shall my sister be<br>When thou liest howling. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside) What, the fair [[Ophelia]]! |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (scattering [[Flowers\|flowers]])<br>Sweets to the sweet. Farewell.<br>I hoped thou shouldst have been my [[Hamlet]]’s wife.<br>I thought thy bride-bed to have decked, sweet maid,<br>And not t’have strewed thy [[Grave\|grave]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O, treble woe<br>Fall ten times treble on that cursed head<br>Whose wicked deed thy most ingenious sense<br>Deprived thee of!—Hold off the earth a while,<br>Till I have caught her once more in mine arms.<br>He leaps into the [[Grave\|grave]]<br>Now pile your [[Dust\|dust]] upon the quick and dead<br>Till of this flat a mountain you have made<br>To o’ertop old Pelion, or the skyish head<br>Of blue Olympus. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (coming forward) What is he whose grief<br>Bears such an emphasis, whose phrase of sorrow<br>Conjures the wand’ring [[Stars\|stars]] and makes them stand<br>Like [[Wonder\|wonder]]-wounded hearers? This is I,<br>[[Hamlet]] the Dane. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Hamlet]] leaps in after [[Laertes]] |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | The devil take thy [[Soul\|soul]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thou pray’st not [[Well\|well]].<br>I prithee take thy fingers from my throat,<br>For though I am not splenative and rash,<br>Yet have I something in me dangerous,<br>Which let thy wiseness [[Fear\|fear]]. Away thy hand. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to Lords)<br>Pluck them asunder. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | [[Hamlet]], [[Hamlet]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL THE LORDS |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Gentlemen! |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) Good my lord, be quiet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, I will fight with him upon this theme<br>Until my eyelids will no longer wag. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O my son, what theme? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I loved [[Ophelia]]. Forty thousand brothers<br>Could not, with all their quantity of [[Love\|love]],<br>Make up my sum.—What wilt thou do for her? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, he is mad, [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Laertes]]) For [[Love\|love]] of God, forbear him. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]]) ‘Swounds, show me what thou’lt do.<br>Woot weep, woot fight, woot fast, woot tear thyself,<br>Woot drink up eisel, eat a crocodile?<br>I’ll do’t. Dost thou come here to whine,<br>To outface me with leaping in her [[Grave\|grave]]?<br>Be buried quick with her, and so will I.<br>And if thou prate of [[Mountains\|mountains]], let them throw<br>Millions of acres on us, till our ground,<br>Singeing his pate against the burning zone,<br>Make Ossa like a wart. Nay, an thou’lt mouth,<br>I’ll rant as [[Well\|well]] as thou. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] to [[Laertes]] This is mere [[Madness\|madness]], |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And thus a while the fit will work on him.<br>Anon, as patient as the female dove<br>When that her golden couplets are disclosed,<br>His [[Silence\|silence]] will sit drooping. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>Hear you, sir,<br>What is the reason that you use me thus?<br>I loved you ever. But it is no matter.<br>Let Hercules himself do what he may,<br>The cat will mew, and dog will have his day. Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I pray you, good [[Horatio]], wait upon him. Exit [[Horatio]]<br><br>(To [[Laertes]]).Strengthen your patience in our last<br>[[Night\|night]]’s speech.<br>We’ll put the matter to the present push.—<br>Good [[Gertrude]], set some watch over your son.—<br>This [[Grave\|grave]] shall have a living monument.<br>An hour of quiet shortly shall we see;<br>Till then, in patience our proceeding be. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | So much for this, sir. Now, let me see, the other.<br>You do remember all the circumstance? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Remember it, my lord! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, in my heart there was a kind of fighting<br>That would not let me [[Sleep\|sleep]]. Methought I lay<br>Worse than the mutines in the bilboes. Rashly—<br>And praised be rashness for it: let us know<br>Our indiscretion sometime serves us [[Well\|well]]<br>When our dear plots do pall, and that should teach us<br>There’s a divinity that shapes our ends,<br>Rough-hew them how we will— |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | That is most certain. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Up from my cabin,<br>My sea-gown scarfed about me in the dark,<br>Groped I to find out them, had my [[Desire\|desire]],<br>Fingered their packet, and in fine withdrew<br>To mine own room again, making so bold,<br>My fears forgetting manners, to unseal<br>Their grand commission; where I found, [[Horatio]]—<br>O royal knavery!—an exact command,<br>Larded with many several sorts of reasons<br>Importing [[Denmark]]’s health, and England’s, too,<br>With ho! such bugs and goblins in my life,<br>That on the supervise, no leisure bated,<br>No, not to stay the grinding of the axe,<br>My head should be struck off. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Is’t possible? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (giving it to him)<br>Here’s the commission. Read it at more leisure.<br>But wilt thou hear me how I did proceed? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I beseech you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Being thus benetted round with villainies—<br>Ere I could make a prologue to my brains,<br>They had begun the play—sat me down,<br>Devised a new commission, wrote it fair.<br>I once did hold it, as our statists do,<br>A baseness to write fair, and laboured much<br>How to forget that learning; but, sir, now<br>It did me yeoman’s service. Wilt thou know<br>Th’effect of what I wrote? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Ay, good my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | An earnest conjuration from the [[King]],<br>As England was his faithful tributary,<br>As [[Love\|love]] between them like the palm should flourish,<br>As peace should still her wheaten garland wear<br>And stand a comma ‘tween their amities,<br>And many such like ‘as’es of great charge,<br>That on the view and know of these contents,<br>Without debatement further more or less,<br>He should the bearers put to sudden [[Death\|death]],<br>Not shriving-[[Time\|time]] allowed. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | How was this sealed? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, even in that was heaven ordinant.<br>I had my father’s signet in my purse,<br>Which was the model of that Danish seal;<br>Folded the writ up in the form of th‘other,<br>Subscribed it, gave’t th’impression, placed it safely,<br>The changeling never known. Now the next day<br>Was our sea-fight; and what to this was sequent<br>Thou know’st already. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | So [[Guildenstern]] and [[Rosencrantz]] go to’t. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, man, they did make [[Love\|love]] to this employment.<br>They are not near my [[Conscience\|conscience]]. Their defeat<br>Doth by their own insinuation grow.<br>’Tis dangerous when the baser [[Nature\|nature]] comes<br>Between the pass and fell incensed points<br>Of mighty opposites. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Why, what a [[King\|king]] is this! |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Does it not, think‘st thee, stand me now upon—<br>He that hath killed my [[King\|king]] and whored my mother,<br>Popped in between th’election and my hopes,<br>Thrown out his angle for my proper life,<br>And with such coz’nage—is’t not perfect [[Conscience\|conscience]]<br>To quit him with this arm? And is’t not to be damned<br>To let this canker of our [[Nature\|nature]] come<br>In further evil? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | It must be shortly known to him from England<br>What is the issue of the business there. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It will be short. The interim’s mine,<br>And a man’s life’s no more than to say ‘one’.<br>But I am very sorry, good [[Horatio]],<br>That to [[Laertes]] I forgot myself;<br>For by the image of my cause I see<br>The portraiture of his. I’ll [[Court\|court]] his favours.<br>But sure, the bravery of his grief did put me<br>Into a tow’ring passion. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Peace, who comes here? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter young Osric, a courtier, taking off his hat |
| OSRIC | Your lordship is right welcome back to [[Denmark]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I humbly thank you, sir. (To [[Horatio]]) Dost know this [[Water\|water]]-fly? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | No, my good lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thy state is the more gracious, for ‘tis a vice to know him. He hath much land, and fertile. Let a beast be lord of beasts, and his crib shall stand at the [[King\|king]]’s mess. ’Tis a chuff, but, as I say, spacious in the possession of dirt. |
| OSRIC | Sweet lord, if your [[Friendship\|friendship]] were at leisure I should impart a thing to you from his majesty. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I will receive it, sir, with all diligence of spirit.<br>Put your bonnet to his right use; ’tis for the head. |
| OSRIC | I thank your lordship, ’tis very hot. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, believe me, ’tis very cold. The [[Wind\|wind]] is northerly. |
| OSRIC | It is indifferent cold, my lord, indeed. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Methinks it is very sultry and hot for my complexion. |
| OSRIC | Exceedingly, my lord. It is very sultry, as ’twere—I cannot tell how. But, my lord, his majesty bade me signify to you that a has laid a great wager on your head. Sir, this is the matter. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I beseech you, remember. |
| OSRIC | Nay, good my lord, for mine ease, in good [[Faith\|faith]]. Sir, you are not ignorant of what excellence [[Laertes]] is at his weapon. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What’s his weapon? |
| OSRIC | Rapier and dagger. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That’s two of his weapons. But [[Well\|well]]. |
| OSRIC | The [[King]], sir, hath wagered with him six Barbary horses, against the which he imponed, as I take it, six French rapiers and poniards, with their assigns as girdle, hanger, or so. Three of the carriages, in [[Faith\|faith]], are very dear to fancy, very responsive to the hilts, most delicate carriages, and of very liberal conceit. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What call you the carriages? |
| OSRIC | The carriages, sir, are the hangers. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The phrase would be more germane to the matter if we could carry cannon by our sides. I would it might be hangers till then. But on: six Barbary horses against six French swords, their assigns, and three liberal-conceited carriages—that’s the French bet against the Danish. Why is this ‘imponed’, as you call it? |
| OSRIC | The [[King]], sir, hath laid, sir, that in a dozen passes between you and him he shall not exceed you three hits. He hath on’t twelve for nine, and it would come to immediate trial if your lordship would vouchsafe the answer. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How if I answer no? |
| OSRIC | I mean, my lord, the opposition of your person in trial. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, I will walk here in the hall. If it please his majesty, ‘tis the breathing [[Time\|time]] of day with me. Let the foils be brought; the gentleman willing, an the [[King]] hold his purpose, I will win for him an I can. If not, I’ll gain nothing but my shame and the odd hits. OSRIC Shall I re-deliver you e’en so? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | To this effect, sir; after what flourish your [[Nature\|nature]] will. |
| OSRIC | I commend my duty to your lordship. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Yours, yours. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Osric |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | He does [[Well\|well]] to commend it himself; there are no tongues else for ’s turn. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | This lapwing runs away with the shell on his head. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | A did comply with his dug before a sucked it. Thus has he—and many more of the same bevy that I know the drossy age dotes on—only got the tune of the [[Time\|time]] and outward habit of encounter, a kind of yeasty collection which carries them through and through the most fanned and winnowed opinions; and do but blow them to their trial, the bubbles are out. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | You will lose this wager, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I do not think so. Since he went into France, I have been in continual practice. I shall win at the odds. But thou wouldst not think how all here about my heart—but it is no matter. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Nay, good my lord— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | It is but foolery, but it is such a kind of gain-giving as would perhaps trouble a woman. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | If your mind dislike anything, obey it. I will forestall their repair hither, and say you are not fit. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Not a whit. We defy augury. There’s a special [[Providence\|providence]] in the fall of a sparrow. If it be now, ’tis not to come. If it be not to come, it will be now. If it be not now, yet it will come. The readiness is all. Since no man has aught of what he [[Leaves\|leaves]], what is’t to leave betimes? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]], [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]], [[Laertes]], and |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | lords, with Osric and other attendants with |
| *(didascalia)* | trumpets, drums, cushions, foils, and gauntlets; a |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | table, and flagons of wine on it |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Come, [[Hamlet]], come, and take this hand from me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>Give me your pardon, sir. I’ve done you wrong;<br>But pardon’t as you are a gentleman.<br>This presence knows,<br>And you must needs have heard, how I am punished<br>With sore distraction. What I have done<br>That might your [[Nature\|nature]], [[Honour\|honour]], and exception<br>Roughly awake, I here proclaim was [[Madness\|madness]].<br>Was’t [[Hamlet]] wronged [[Laertes]]? Never [[Hamlet]].<br>If [[Hamlet]] from himself be ta‘en away,<br>And when he’s not himself does wrong [[Laertes]],<br>Then [[Hamlet]] does it not, [[Hamlet]] denies it.<br>Who does it then? His [[Madness\|madness]]. If’t be so,<br>[[Hamlet]] is of the faction that is wronged.<br>His [[Madness\|madness]] is poor [[Hamlet]]’s enemy.<br>Sir, in this audience<br>Let my disclaiming from a purposed evil<br>Free me so far in your most generous thoughts<br>That I have shot mine arrow o’er the [[house]]<br>And hurt my brother. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I am satisfied in [[Nature\|nature]],<br>Whose motive in this case should stir me most<br>To my [[Revenge\|revenge]]. But in my terms of [[Honour\|honour]]<br>I stand aloof, and will no reconcilement<br>Till by some elder masters of known [[Honour\|honour]]<br>I have a voice and precedent of peace<br>To keep my name ungored; but till that [[Time\|time]]<br>I do receive your offered [[Love\|love]] like [[Love\|love]],<br>And will not wrong it. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I do embrace it freely,<br>And will this brothers’ wager frankly play.—<br>(To attendants) Give us the foils. Come on. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | (to attendants)<br>Come, one for me. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I’ll be your foil, [[Laertes]]. In mine ignorance<br>Your skill shall, like a star i’th’ darkest [[Night\|night]],<br>Stick fiery off indeed. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | You mock me, sir. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, by this hand. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Give them the foils, young Osric. Cousin [[Hamlet]],<br>You know the wager? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Very [[Well\|well]], my lord.<br>Your grace hath laid the odds o’th’ weaker side. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I do not [[Fear\|fear]] it; I have seen you both.<br>But since he is bettered, we have therefore odds. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | (taking a foil)<br>This is too heavy; let me see another. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (taking a foil)<br>This likes me [[Well\|well]]. These foils have all a length? |
| OSRIC | Ay, my good lord.<br>[[Hamlet]] and [[Laertes]] prepare to play |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to attendants)<br>Set me the stoups of wine upon that table.<br>If [[Hamlet]] give the first or second hit,<br>Or quit in answer of the third exchange,<br>Let all the battlements their ordnance [[Fire\|fire]].<br>The [[King]] shall drink to [[Hamlet]]’s better breath,<br>And in the cup an [[Union\|union]] shall he throw<br>Richer than that which four successive kings<br>In [[Denmark]]’s [[Crown\|crown]] have worn. Give me the cups,<br>And let the kettle to the trumpet speak,<br>The trumpet to the cannoneer without,<br>The cannons to the heavens, the heaven to earth,<br><br>‘Now the [[King]] drinks to [[Hamlet]]’. |
| *(didascalia)* | Trumpets the while he drinks |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Come, begin.<br>And you, the judges, bear a wary eye. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]]) Come on, sir. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Come, my lord.<br>They play |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | One. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | No. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to Osric) Judgement. |
| OSRIC | A hit, a very palpable hit. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | [[Well]], again. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Stay. Give me drink. [[Hamlet]], this pearl is thine.<br>Here’s to thy health.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Drum and trumpets sound, and shot goes off Give him the cup. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I’ll play this bout first. Set it by a while.—<br>Come.<br>They play again<br>Another hit. What say you? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | A touch, a touch, I do confess. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Our son shall win. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | He’s fat and scant of breath.—<br>Here, [[Hamlet]], take my napkin. Rub thy brows.<br>The [[Queen]] carouses to thy fortune, [[Hamlet]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Good madam. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Gertrude]], do not drink. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I will, my lord, I pray you pardon me.<br>She drinks, then offers the cup to [[Hamlet]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (aside)<br>It is the poisoned cup; it is too late. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I dare not drink yet, madam; by and by. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) Come, let me wipe thy face. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | (aside to [[Claudius]]) My lord, I’ll hit him now. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (aside to [[Laertes]]) I do not think’t. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | (aside)<br>And yet ‘tis almost ’gainst my [[Conscience\|conscience]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Come for the third, [[Laertes]], you but dally.<br>I pray you pass with your best violence.<br>I am afeard you make a wanton of me. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Say you so? Come on.<br>They play |
| OSRIC | Nothing neither way. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | (to [[Hamlet]])<br>Have at you now! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Laertes]] wounds [[Hamlet]]. In scuffling, they change |
| *(didascalia)* | rapiers, and [[Hamlet]] wounds [[Laertes]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to attendants)<br>Part them, they are incensed. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>Nay, come again. |
| *(didascalia)* | The [[Queen]] falls down |
| OSRIC | Look to the [[Queen]] there, ho! |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | They bleed on both sides. (To [[Hamlet]]) How is’t, my lord? |
| OSRIC | How is’t, [[Laertes]]? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Why, as a woodcock to mine own springe, Osric.<br><br>I am justly killed with mine own treachery. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How does the [[Queen]]? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | She swoons to see them bleed. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | No, no, the drink, the drink! O my dear [[Hamlet]], |
| *(didascalia)* | The drink, the drink—I am poisoned. She dies |
| *(didascalia)* | O villainy! Ho! Let the door be locked! Exit Osric |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Treachery, seek it out. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | It is here, [[Hamlet]]. [[Hamlet]], thou art slain.<br>No med’cine in the world can do thee good.<br>In thee there is not half an hour of life.<br>The treacherous instrument is in thy hand,<br>Unbated and envenomed. The foul practice<br>Hath turned itself on me. Lo, here I lie,<br>Never to rise again. Thy mother’s poisoned.<br>I can no more. The [[King]], the [[King]]’s to blame. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The point envenomed too? Then, venom, to thy work.<br>He hurts [[King]] [[Claudius]] |
| ALL THE COURTIERS | Treason, treason! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O yet defend me, friends! I am but hurt. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Here, thou incestuous, murd’rous, damned Dane,<br>Drink off this potion. Is thy [[Union\|union]] here?<br>Follow my mother. [[King]] [[Claudius]] dies |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | He is justly served.<br>It is a [[Poison\|poison]] tempered by himself.<br>Exchange [[Forgiveness\|forgiveness]] with me, noble [[Hamlet]].<br>Mine and my father’s [[Death\|death]] come not upon thee,<br>Nor thine on me. He dies |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Heaven make thee free of it! I follow thee.<br>I am dead, [[Horatio]]. Wretched [[Queen]], adieu!<br>You that look pale and tremble at this chance,<br>That are but mutes or audience to this act,<br>Had I but [[Time\|time]]—as this fell sergeant [[Death]]<br>Is strict in his arrest—O, I could tell you—<br>But let it be. [[Horatio]], I am dead,<br>Thou liv’st. Report me and my cause aright<br>To the unsatisfied. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Never believe it.<br>I am more an antique Roman than a Dane.<br>Here’s yet some liquor left. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | As thou’rt a man,<br>Give me the cup. Let go. By heaven, I’ll ha’t.<br>O God, [[Horatio]], what a wounded name,<br>Things standing thus unknown, shall live behind me!<br>If thou didst ever hold me in thy heart,<br>Absent thee from felicity a while,<br>And in this harsh world draw thy breath in [[Pain\|pain]]<br>To tell my story.<br>March afar off, and shout within<br>What warlike noise is this? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Osric |
| OSRIC | Young Fortinbras, with conquest come from Poland,<br>To th’ambassadors of England gives<br>This warlike volley. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | O, I die, [[Horatio]]!<br>The potent [[Poison\|poison]] quite o‘ercrows my spirit.<br>I cannot live to hear the news from England,<br>But I do prophesy th’election lights<br>On Fortinbras. He has my dying voice.<br>So tell him, with th’occurrents, more and less,<br>Which have solicited. The rest is [[Silence\|silence]].<br>O, O, O, O!<br>He dies |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Now cracks a noble heart. Good [[Night\|night]], sweet prince,<br>And flights of angels sing thee to thy rest.—<br>Why does the drum come hither? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Fortinbras with the English Ambassadors, with a drummer, colours, and attendants |
| FORTINBRAS | Where is this sight? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What is it ye would see?<br>If aught of woe or [[Wonder\|wonder]], cease your search. |
| FORTINBRAS | This quarry cries on havoc. O proud [[Death\|death]],<br>What feast is toward in thine eternal cell<br>That thou so many princes at a shot<br>So bloodily hast struck! |
| AMBASSADOR | The sight is dismal,<br>And our affairs from England come too late.<br>The ears are senseless that should give us hearing<br>To tell him his commandment is fulfilled,<br>That [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] are dead.<br>Where should we have our thanks? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Not from his mouth,<br>Had it th‘ability of life to thank you.<br>He never gave commandment for their [[Death\|death]].<br>But since so jump upon this bloody question<br>You from the Polack wars, and you from England,<br>Are here arrived, give order that these bodies<br>High on a stage be placed to the view;<br>And let me speak to th’ yet unknowing world<br>How these things came about. So shall you hear<br>Of carnal, bloody, and unnatural acts,<br>Of accidental judgements, casual slaughters,<br>Of deaths put on by cunning and forced cause;<br>And, in this upshot, purposes mistook<br>Fall’n on th’inventors’ heads. All this can I<br>Truly deliver. |
| FORTINBRAS | Let us haste to hear it,<br>And call the noblest to the audience.<br>For me, with sorrow I embrace my fortune.<br>I have some rights of [[Memory\|memory]] in this kingdom,<br>Which now to claim my vantage doth invite me. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Of that I shall have also cause to speak,<br>And from his mouth whose voice will draw on more.<br>But let this same be presently performed,<br>Even whiles men’s minds are wild, lest more<br>mischance<br>On plots and errors happen. |
| FORTINBRAS | Let four captains<br>Bear [[Hamlet]] like a soldier to the stage,<br>For he was likely, had he been put on,<br>To have proved most royally; and for his passage,<br>The soldiers’ music and the rites of war<br>Speak loudly for him.<br>Take up the [[Body\|body]]. Such a sight as this<br>Becomes the field, but here shows much amiss.<br>Go, bid the soldiers shoot. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt, marching, with the bodies; after the which, a peal of ordnance are shot off |
| ADDITIONAL PASSAGES | A. Just before the second entrance of the [[Ghost]] in 1.1 (l. 106.1), Q2 has these additional lines: |
| BARNARDO | I think it be no other but e’en so.<br>[[Well]] may it sort that this portentous figure<br>Comes armed through our watch so like the [[King\|king]]<br>That was and is the question of these wars. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A mote it is to trouble the mind’s eye.<br>In the most high and palmy state of Rome,<br>A little ere the mightiest Julius fell,<br>The graves stood tenantless, and the sheeted dead<br>Did squeak and gibber in the Roman streets<br>At [[Stars\|stars]] with trains of [[Fire\|fire]], and dews of [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Disasters in the sun; and the moist star,<br>Upon whose influence Neptune’s empire stands,<br>Was sick almost to doomsday with eclipse.<br>And even the like precurse of feared events,<br>As harbingers preceding still the fates,<br>And prologue to the omen coming on,<br>Have heaven and earth together demonstrated<br>Unto our climature and countrymen.<br><br>B. Just before the entrance of the [[Ghost]] in 1.4 (l. 18.1),<br>Q2 has these additional lines continuing [[Hamlet]]’s speech:<br>This heavy-headed revel east and west<br>Makes us traduced and taxed of other nations.<br>They clepe us drunkards, and with swinish phrase<br>Soil our addition; and indeed it takes<br>From our achievements, though performed at height,<br>So, oft it chances in particular men<br>That, for some vicious mole of [[Nature\|nature]] in them—<br>As in their birth, wherein they are not guilty,<br>Since [[Nature\|nature]] cannot choose his origin,<br>By the o‘ergrowth of some complexion,<br>Oft breaking down the pales and forts of reason,<br>Or by some habit that too much o’erleavens<br>The form of plausive manners—that these men,<br>Carrying, I say, the stamp of one defect,<br>Being [[Nature\|nature]]’s livery or fortune’s star,<br>His virtues else be they as pure as grace,<br>As infinite as man may undergo,<br>Shall in the general censure take corruption<br>From that particular fault. The dram of evil<br>Doth all the noble substance over-daub<br>To his own scandal.<br><br>C. After 1.4.55, Q2 has these additional lines continuing [[Horatio]]’s speech:<br>The very place puts toys of desperation,<br>Without more motive, into every brain<br>That looks so many fathoms to the sea<br>And hears it roar beneath.<br><br>D. After 3.2.163, Q2 has this additional couplet concluding the Player [[Queen]]’s speech:<br>Where [[Love\|love]] is great, the littlest doubts are [[Fear\|fear]];<br>Where little fears grow great, great [[Love\|love]] grows there.<br><br>E. After 3.2.208, Q2 has this additional couplet in the middle of the Player [[Queen]]’s speech:<br>To desperation turn my trust and [[Hope\|hope]];<br>An anchor’s cheer in [[Prison\|prison]] be my scope.<br><br>F. After ‘this?’ in 3.4.70, Q2 has this more expansive version of [[Hamlet]]’s lines of which F retains only ‘what devil . . . blind’:<br><br>Sense sure you have,<br>Else could you not have motion; but sure that sense<br>Is apoplexed, for [[Madness\|madness]] would not err,<br>Nor sense to ecstasy was ne’er so thralled<br>But it reserved some quantity of choice<br>To serve in such a difference. What devil was’t<br>That thus hath cozened you at hoodman-blind?<br>Eyes without feeling, feeling without sight,<br>Ears without hands or eyes, smelling sans all,<br>Or but a sickly part of one true sense<br>Could not so mope.<br><br>G. After 3.4.151, Q2 has this more expansive version of [[Hamlet]]’s lines of which F retains only ‘refrain . . . abstinence’:<br>That [[Monster\|monster]] custom, who all sense doth eat,<br>Of habits devilish, is angel yet in this:<br>That to the use of actions fair and good<br>He likewise gives a frock or livery<br>That aptly is put on. Refrain tonight,<br>And that shall lend a kind of easiness<br>To the next abstinence, the next more easy—<br>For use almost can change the stamp of [[Nature\|nature]]—<br>And either in the devil, or throw him out<br>With wondrous potency.<br><br>H. At 3.4.185, Q2 has these additional lines before ‘This man . . .’: |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | There’s letters sealed, and my two schoolfellows—<br>Whom I will trust as I will adders fanged—<br>They bear the mandate, they must sweep my way<br>And marshal me to knavery. Let it work,<br>For ‘tis the sport to have the engineer<br>Hoised with his own petard; and’t shall go hard<br>But I will delve one yard below their mines<br>And blow them at the [[Moon\|moon]]. O, ’tis most sweet<br>When in one line two crafts directly meet.<br><br>1. After ‘done’ in 4.1.39, Q2 has these additional lines continuing the [[King]]’s speech (the first three words are an editorial conjecture):<br>So envious slander,<br>Whose whisper o’er the world’s diameter,<br><br>As level as the cannon to his blank,<br>Transports his poisoned shot, may miss our name<br>And hit the woundless air.<br>J. Q2 has this more expansive version of the ending of 4.4: |
| CAPTAIN | I will do’t, my lord. |
| FORTINBRAS | Go softly on. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with his army |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]], [[Rosencrantz]], [[Guildenstern]], etc. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to the Captain) Good sir, whose powers are these? |
| CAPTAIN | They are of Norway, sir. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How purposed, sir, I pray you? |
| CAPTAIN | Against some part of Poland. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Who commands them, sir? |
| CAPTAIN | The nephew to old Norway, Fortinbras. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Goes it against the main of Poland, sir, Or for some frontier? |
| CAPTAIN | Truly to speak, and with no addition,<br>We go to gain a little patch of ground<br>That hath in it no profit but the name.<br>To pay five ducats, five, I would not farm it,<br>Nor will it yield to Norway or the Pole<br>A ranker rate, should it be sold in fee. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why then, the Polack never will defend it. |
| CAPTAIN | Yes, it is already garrisoned. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Two thousand souls and twenty thousand ducats<br>Will now [[debate]] the question of this straw.<br>This is th’imposthume of much wealth and peace,<br>That inward breaks and shows no cause without<br>Why the man dies. I humbly thank you, sir. |
| CAPTAIN | God buy you, sir. Exit |
| [[Rosencrantz\|ROSENCRANTZ]] | Will’t please you go, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I’ll be with you straight. Go a little before. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Hamlet]] |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How all occasions do inform against me<br>And spur my dull [[Revenge\|revenge]]! What is a man<br>If his chief good and market of his [[Time\|time]]<br>Be but to [[Sleep\|sleep]] and feed?—a beast, no more.<br>Sure, he that made us with such large discourse,<br>Looking before and after, gave us not<br>That capability and god-like reason<br>To fust in us unused. Now whether it be<br>Bestial oblivion, or some craven scruple<br>Of thinking too precisely on th‘event—<br>A thought which, quartered, hath but one part wisdom<br>And ever three parts coward—I do not know<br>Why yet I live to say ‘This thing’s to do’,<br>Sith I have cause, and will, and strength, and means,<br>To do’t. Examples gross as earth exhort me,<br>Witness this army of such mass and charge,<br>Led by a delicate and tender prince,<br>Whose spirit with divine [[Ambition\|ambition]] puffed<br>Makes mouths at the invisible event,<br>Exposing what is mortal and unsure<br>To all that fortune, [[Death\|death]], and danger dare,<br>Even for an eggshell. Rightly to be great<br>Is not to stir without great argument,<br>But greatly to find quarrel in a straw<br>When [[Honour\|honour]]’s at the stake. How stand I, then,<br>That have a father killed, a mother stained,<br>Excitements of my reason and my [[Blood\|blood]],<br>And let all [[Sleep\|sleep]] while, to my shame, I see<br>The imminent [[Death\|death]] of twenty thousand men<br>That, for a fantasy and trick of fame,<br>Go to their graves like beds, fight for a plot<br>Whereon the numbers cannot try the cause,<br>Which is not tomb enough and continent<br>To hide the slain. O, from this [[Time\|time]] forth<br>My thoughts be bloody or be nothing worth! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | K. After ‘accident’ at 4.7.67, Q2 has these additional lines: |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | My lord, I will be ruled,<br>The rather if you could devise it so<br>That I might be the organ. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | It falls right.<br>You have been talked of, since your travel, much,<br>And that in [[Hamlet]]’s hearing, for a quality<br>Wherein they say you shine. Your sum of parts<br>Did not together pluck such envy from him<br>As did that one, and that, in my regard,<br>Of the unworthiest siege. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What part is that, my lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | A very ribbon in the cap of youth,<br>Yet needful too, for youth no less becomes<br>The light and careless livery that it wears<br>Than settled age his sables and his weeds<br>Importing health and graveness.<br><br>L. After ‘match you’ at 4.7.85, Q2 has these additional lines continuing the [[King]]’s speech:<br>Th’escrimers of their nation<br>He swore had neither motion, guard, nor eye<br>If you opposed them.<br>M. After 4.7.96, Q2 has these additional lines continuing the [[King]]’s speech:<br>There lives within the very flame of [[Love\|love]]<br>A kind of wick or snuff that will abate it,<br>And nothing is at a like goodness still,<br>For goodness, growing to a plurisy, |
| *(didascalia)* | Dies in his own too much. That we would do |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | We should do when we would, for this ‘would’ changes,<br>And hath abatements and delays as many<br>As there are tongues, are hands, are accidents;<br>And then this ‘should’ is like a spendthrift’s sigh,<br>That hurts by easing. But to the quick of th’ulcer—<br><br>N. After ‘Sir’ at 5.2.107, Q2 has these lines (in place of F’s ‘you are not ignorant of what excellence [[Laertes]] is at his weapon’):<br>here is newly come to [[Court\|court]] [[Laertes]], believe me, an absolute gentleman, full of most excellent differences, of very soft society and great showing. Indeed, to speak feelingly of him, he is the card or calendar of gentry, for you shall find in him the continent of what part a gentleman would see. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Sir, his definement suffers no perdition in you, though I know to divide him inventorially would dizzy th’arithmetic of [[Memory\|memory]], and yet but yaw neither in respect of his quick sail. But in the verity of extolment, I take him to be a [[Soul\|soul]] of great article, and his infusion of such dearth and rareness as, to make true diction of him, his semblable is his [[Mirror\|mirror]], and who else would trace him his umbrage, nothing more. |
| OSRIC | Your lordship speaks most infallibly of him. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | The concernancy, sir? Why do we wrap the gentleman in our more rawer breath? |
| OSRIC | Sir? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Is’t not possible to understand in another tongue? You will to’t, sir, rarely. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What imports the nomination of this gentleman? OSRIC Of [[Laertes]]? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (aside to [[Hamlet]]) His purse is empty already; all ’s golden words are spent. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to Osric) Of him, sir. |
| OSRIC | I know you are not ignorant— |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I would you did, sir; yet, in [[Faith\|faith]], if you did it would not much approve me. [[Well]], sir? |
| OSRIC | You are not ignorant of what excellence [[Laertes]] is. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I dare not confess that, lest I should compare with him in excellence. But to know a man [[Well\|well]] were to know himself. |
| OSRIC | I mean, sir, for his weapon. But in the imputation laid on him by them, in his meed he’s unfellowed.<br><br>O. After 5.2.118, Q2 has the following additional speech: |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (aside to [[Hamlet]]) I knew you must be edified by the margin ere you had done.<br><br>P. After 5.2.154, Q2 has the following (in place of F’s ‘[[Horatio\|HORATIO]] You will lose this wager, my lord’): |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Lord |
| LORD | (to [[Hamlet]]) My lord, his majesty commended him to you by young Osric, who brings back to him that you attend him in the hall. He sends to know if your pleasure hold to play with [[Laertes]], or that you will take longer [[Time\|time]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I am constant to my purposes; they follow the [[King]]’s pleasure. If his fitness speaks, mine is ready, now or whensoever, provided I be so able as now. |
| LORD | The [[King]] and [[Queen]] and all are coming down. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | In happy [[Time\|time]]. |
| LORD | The [[Queen]] desires you to use some gentle entertainment to [[Laertes]] before you fall to play. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | She [[Well\|well]] instructs me. Exit Lord |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | You will lose, my lord. |
| TWELFTH | [[Night\|NIGHT]], the end of the Christmas season, was traditionally a [[Time\|time]] of revelry and topsy-turvydom; Shakespeare’s title for a play in which a servant aspires to his mistress’s hand has no more specific reference. It was thought appropriate to the festive occasion of Candlemas (2 February) 1602 when, in the first known allusion to it, John Manningham, a law student of the Middle Temple in [[London]], noted ‘at our feast we had a play called [[Twelfth Night]], or What You Will’. References to ‘the Sophy’—the Shah of Persia (2.5.174; 3.4.271)—probably post-date Sir Robert [[Shirley]]’s return from Persia, in a [[Ship\|ship]] named The Sophy, in 1599; and ‘the new map with the augmentation of the Indies’ (3.2.75) appears to be one published in 1599 and reissued in 1600. Shakespeare may have picked up the name [[Orsino]] for his young duke from a Tuscan nobleman whom [[Queen Elizabeth]] entertained at Whitehall with a play performed by Shakespeare’s company on [[Twelfth Night]] 1601. Probably he wrote [[Twelfth Night]] during that year.<br>[[Twelfth Night]]’s romantic setting is Illyria, the Greek and Roman name for Adriatic territory roughly corresponding to the former Yugoslavia. Manningham had noted that the play was ‘much like The [[Comedy]] of Errors or Menaechmi in Plautus’, thinking no [[Doubt\|doubt]] of the confusions created by identical twins. Shakespeare may also have known an anonymous Italian [[Comedy\|comedy]], GI’Ingannati (The Deceived Ones), acted in 1531 and first printed in 1537, which influenced a number of other plays and prose tales including Barnaby Riche’s story of Apolonius and Silla printed as part of Riche’s Farewell to Military Profession (1581). Riche gave Shakespeare his main plot of a shipwrecked girl ([[Viola]]) who, disguised as a boy (Cesario), serves a young Duke ([[Orsino]]) and undertakes [[Love\|love]]-errands on his behalf to a noble lady ([[Olivia]]) who falls in [[Love\|love]] with her but mistakenly marries her twin brother ([[Sebastian]]). Shakespeare idealizes Riche’s characters and purges the story of some of its explicit [[Sexuality\|sexuality]]: Riche’s [[Olivia]], for example, is pregnant before [[Marriage\|marriage]], and his [[Viola]] reveals her [[Identity\|identity]], in a manner impractical for a boy actor, by stripping to the waist. Shakespeare complicates the plot by giving [[Olivia]] a reprobate uncle, [[Sir Toby Belch]], and two additional suitors, the asinine [[Sir Andrew Aguecheek]] and her steward, [[Malvolio]], tricked by members of her household into believing that she loves him. More important to the play than to the plot is the entirely Shakespearian clown, [[Feste]], a wry and oblique commentator whose wit in folly is opposed to [[Malvolio]]’s folly in wit.<br>[[Twelfth Night]] is the consummation of Shakespeare’s romantic [[Comedy\|comedy]], a play of wide emotional range, extending from the robust, brilliantly orchestrated [[Humour\|humour]] of the scene of midnight revelry (2.2) to the rapt [[Wonder\|wonder]] of the antiphon of recognition (5.1.224-56) between the reunited twins. In performance the balance shifts, favouring sometimes the exposure and celebration of folly, at other times the poignancy of unattained [[Love\|love]] and of unheeded wisdom; but few other plays have so consistently provided theatrical pleasure of so high an order. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>[[Orsino\|ORSINO]], Duke of Illyria |
| SECOND OFFICER | [[Viola\|VIOLA]], a lady, later disguised as Cesario<br>A CAPTAIN<br>[[Sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]], her twin brother<br>[[Antonio\|ANTONIO]], another sea-captain<br>[[Olivia\|OLIVIA]], a Countess<br>[[Maria\|MARIA]], her waiting-gentlewoman |
| SIR TOBY | Belch, [[Olivia]]’s kinsman |
| SIR ANDREW | Aguecheek, companion of Sir Toby<br>[[Malvolio\|MALVOLIO]], [[Olivia]]’s steward<br>FABIAN, a member of [[Olivia]]’s household |
| [[Feste\|FESTE]] | the Clown, her jester<br>A PRIEST<br>A SERVANT of [[Olivia]]<br>Musicians, sailors, lords, attendants |

| Speaker | Line |
