| [[Hamlet|HAMLET]] | Seguitelo passo passo. Sospingetelo con [[Speed|prestezza]] a bordo. 56<br>Non indugiate. Voglio che parta stanotte.<br>Via, ché tutto è sigillato e compiuto<br>quanto altro attiene all’affare. Vi prego, fate presto. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce [[Guildenstern]] |
| [[Hamlet|HAMLET]] | E tu, Inghilterra, se il mio [[Love|amore]] tieni in qualche conto—<br>come la mia grande potenza può fartene accorta,<br>giacché la tua cicatrice appare ancora viva e rossa<br>dopo la [[Sword|spada]] danese, e il tuo libero timore<br>ci rende omaggio—non porrai in freddo<br>il nostro sovrano decreto, che pienamente comporta,<br>con lettere che a tale effetto scongiurano,<br>l’immediata [[Death|morte]] di [[Hamlet]]. Fallo, Inghilterra,<br>ché come la febbre etica nel mio [[Blood|sangue]] egli infuria,<br>e tu devi guarirmi. Finché non so che è compiuto,<br>qualunque sia la mia sorte, le mie gioie non son mai cominciate. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_4" data-title="Hamlet — Act IV, Scene 4" data-chapter="Scene 4" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,character/hamlet"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Fortinbras with an army over the stage |
| FORTINBRAS | Go, captain, from me greet the Danish [[King\|king]].<br>Tell him that by his licence Fortinbras<br>Claims the conveyance of a promised march<br>Over his kingdom. You know the rendezvous.<br>If that his majesty would aught with us,<br>We shall express our duty in his eye,<br>And let him know so. |
| CAPTAIN | I will do’t, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| FORTINBRAS | Go safely on. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt marching |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Fortinbras con un esercito attraversando la scena |
| FORTINBRAS | Andate, capitano, e salutate da parte mia il [[King|re]] di Danimarca.<br>Ditegli che, col suo permesso, Fortinbras<br>reclama il transito d’una marcia promessa<br>attraverso il suo regno. Conoscete il luogo del convegno.<br>Se sua maestà volesse alcunché da noi,<br>gli esprimeremo il nostro dovere al suo cospetto,<br>e così glielo faremo sapere. |
| CAPITANO | Lo farò, mio signore. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| FORTINBRAS | Procedete sicuri. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono marciando |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_5" data-title="Hamlet — Act IV, Scene 5" data-chapter="Scene 5" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/crown,motif/death,motif/desire,motif/flowers,motif/grave,motif/love,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/poison,motif/rose,motif/snow,motif/soul,motif/tears,motif/well,motif/wheel,motif/window,concept/charity,concept/conscience,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/guilt,concept/hope,concept/jealousy,concept/madness,concept/mercy,concept/nature,concept/revenge,concept/wonder,setting/denmark,character/claudius,character/gertrude,character/hamlet,character/horatio,character/laertes,character/ophelia,character/polonius,character/queen,character/valentine"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] and [[Horatio]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | I will not speak with her. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | She is importunate,<br>Indeed distraught. Her mood will needs be pitied. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | What would she have? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | She speaks much of her father, says she hears<br>There’s tricks i’th’ world, and hems, and beats her<br>heart,<br>Spurns enviously at straws, speaks things in [[Doubt\|doubt]]<br>That carry but half sense. Her speech is nothing,<br>Yet the unshapèd use of it doth move<br>The hearers to collection. They aim at it,<br>And botch the words up fit to their own thoughts,<br>Which, as her winks and nods and gestures yield them,<br>Indeed would make one think there might be thought,<br>Though nothing sure, yet much unhappily. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | ’Twere good she were spoken with, for she may strew<br>Dangerous conjectures in ill-breeding minds.<br>Let her come in. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Horatio]] withdraws to admit [[Ophelia]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | To my sick [[Soul\|soul]], as sin’s true [[Nature\|nature]] is,<br>Each toy seems prologue to some great amiss.<br>So full of artless [[Jealousy\|jealousy]] is [[Guilt\|guilt]],<br>It spills itself in fearing to be spilt. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Ophelia]] mad, her hair down, with a lute |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Where is the beauteous majesty of [[Denmark]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | How now, [[Ophelia]]? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>How should I your true [[Love\|love]] know<br>From another one?—<br>By his cockle hat and staff,<br>And his sandal shoon. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, sweet lady, what imports this [[Song\|song]]? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Say you? Nay, pray you, mark. (Sings)<br>He is dead and gone, lady,<br>He is dead and gone.<br>At his head a grass-green turf,<br>At his heels a stone. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Nay, but [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Pray you, mark.<br>(Sings)<br>White his shroud as the mountain [[Snow\|snow]]— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alas, look here, my lord. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>Larded with sweet [[Flowers\|flowers]],<br>Which bewept to the [[Grave\|grave]] did—not—go<br>With true-[[Love\|love]] showers. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How do ye, pretty lady? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | [[Well]], God’ield you. They say the owl was a baker’s daughter. Lord, we know what we are, but know not what we may be. God be at your table! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) Conceit upon her father. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Pray you, let’s have no words of this, but when they ask you what it means, say you this. (Sings)<br>Tomorrow is Saint [[Valentine]]’s day,<br>All in the morning betime,<br>And I a maid at your [[Window\|window]]<br>To be your [[Valentine]].<br>Then up he [[Rose\|rose]], and donned his clothes,<br>And dupped the chamber door;<br>Let in the maid, that out a maid<br>Never departed more. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Pretty [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | Indeed, la? Without an oath, I’ll make an end on’t.<br>(Sings) By Gis, and by Saint [[Charity]],<br>Alack, and fie for shame!<br>Young men will do’t if they come to‘t,<br>By Cock, they are to blame.<br>Quoth she ‘Before you tumbled me,<br>You promised me to wed.’<br>So would I ‘a’ done, by yonder sun,<br>An thou hadst not come to my bed. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Gertrude]]) How long hath she been thus? |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | I [[Hope\|hope]] all will be [[Well\|well]]. We must be patient. But I cannot choose but weep to think they should lay him i’th’ cold ground. My brother shall know of it. And so I thank you for your good counsel. Come, my coach! Good [[Night\|night]], ladies, good [[Night\|night]], sweet ladies, good [[Night\|night]], good [[Night\|night]]. Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to [[Horatio]])<br>Follow her close. Give her good watch, I pray you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Horatio]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, this is the [[Poison\|poison]] of deep grief! It springs<br>All from her father’s [[Death\|death]]. O [[Gertrude]], [[Gertrude]],<br>When sorrows come they come not single spies,<br>But in battalions. First, her father slain;<br>Next, your son gone, and he most violent author<br>Of his own just remove; the people muddied,<br>Thick and unwholesome in their thoughts and whispers<br>For good [[Polonius]]’ [[Death\|death]]; and we have done but<br>greenly<br>In hugger-mugger to inter him; poor [[Ophelia]]<br>Divided from herself and her fair judgement,<br>Without the which we are pictures or mere beasts;<br>Last, and as much containing as all these,<br>Her brother is in secret come from France,<br>Feeds on this [[Wonder\|wonder]], keeps himself in clouds,<br>And wants not buzzers to infect his ear<br>With pestilent speeches of his father’s [[Death\|death]];<br>Wherein necessity, of matter beggared,<br>Will nothing stick our persons to arraign<br>In ear and ear. O my dear [[Gertrude]], this,<br>Like to a murd’ring-piece, in many places<br>Gives me superfluous [[Death\|death]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Alack, what noise is this? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Where is my Switzers? Let them guard the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What is the matter? |
| MESSENGER | Save yourself, my lord.<br>The ocean, overpeering of his list,<br>Eats not the flats with more impetuous haste<br>Than young [[Laertes]], in a riotous head,<br>O‘erbears your officers. The rabble call him lord,<br>And, as the world were now but to begin,<br>Antiquity forgot, custom not known,<br>The ratifiers and props of every word,<br>They cry ‘Choose we! [[Laertes]] shall be [[King\|king]].’<br>Caps, hands, and tongues applaud it to the clouds,<br>‘[[Laertes]] shall be [[King\|king]], [[Laertes]] [[King\|king]].’ |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | How cheerfully on the false trail they cry! |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O, this is counter, you false Danish [[dogs]]! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | The doors are broke. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Laertes]] with his followers at the door |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Where is the [[King]]?—Sirs, stand you all without. |
| ALL HIS FOLLOWERS | No, let’s come in. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I pray you, give me leave. |
| ALL HIS FOLLOWERS | We will, we will. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I thank you. Keep the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | exeunt followers |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O thou vile [[King\|king]],<br>Give me my father. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Calmly, good [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | That drop of [[Blood\|blood]] that’s calm proclaims me bastard,<br>Cries cuckold to my father, brands the harlot<br>Even here between the chaste unsmirchèd brow<br>Of my true mother. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | What is the cause, [[Laertes]],<br>That thy rebellion looks so giant-like?—<br>Let him go, [[Gertrude]]. Do not [[Fear\|fear]] our person.<br>There’s such divinity doth hedge a [[King\|king]]<br>That treason can but peep to what it would,<br>Acts little of his will.—Tell me, [[Laertes]],<br>Why thou art thus incensed.—Let him go, [[Gertrude]].—<br>Speak, man. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Where is my father? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Dead. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>But not by him. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let him demand his fill. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | How came he dead? I’ll not be juggled with.<br>To hell, allegiance! Vows to the blackest devil!<br>[[Conscience]] and grace to the profoundest pit!<br>I dare damnation. To this point I stand,<br>That both the worlds I give to negligence,<br>Let come what comes. Only I’ll be revenged<br>Most throughly for my father. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Who shall stay you? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | My will, not all the world;<br>And for my means, I’ll husband them so [[Well\|well]]<br>They shall go far with little. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Good [[Laertes]],<br>If you [[Desire\|desire]] to know the certainty<br>Of your dear father’s [[Death\|death]], is’t writ in your [[Revenge\|revenge]]<br>That, sweepstake, you will draw both friend and foe,<br>Winner and loser? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | None but his enemies. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Will you know them then? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | To his good friends thus wide I’ll ope my arms,<br>And, like the kind life-rend’ring pelican,<br>Repast them with my [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Why, now you speak<br>Like a good [[Child\|child]] and a true gentleman.<br>That I am guiltless of your father’s [[Death\|death]],<br>And am most sensibly in grief for it,<br>It shall as level to your judgement pierce<br>As day does to your eye. |
| *(didascalia)* | A noise within |
| VOICES | (within) Let her come in. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | How now, what noise is that? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Ophelia]] as before |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O heat dry up my brains! [[Tears]] seven times salt<br>Burn out the sense and virtue of mine eye!<br>By heaven, thy [[Madness\|madness]] shall be paid by weight<br>Till our scale turns the beam. O [[Rose\|rose]] of May,<br>Dear maid, kind sister, sweet [[Ophelia]]!<br>O heavens, is’t possible a young maid’s wits<br>Should be as mortal as an old man’s life?<br>[[Nature]] is fine in [[Love\|love]], and where ’tis fine<br>It sends some precious instance of itself<br>After the thing it loves. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>They bore him barefaced on the bier,<br>Hey non nony, nony, hey nony,<br>And on his [[Grave\|grave]] rained many a tear—<br>Fare you [[Well\|well]], my dove. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Hadst thou thy wits and didst persuade [[Revenge\|revenge]],<br>It could not move thus. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | You must sing ‘Down, a-down’, and you, ‘Call him a-down-a’. O, how the [[Wheel\|wheel]] becomes it! It is the false steward that stole his master’s daughter. [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] This nothing’s more than matter. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | There’s rosemary, that’s for remembrance. Pray, [[Love\|love]], remember. And there is pansies; that’s for thoughts. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | A document in [[Madness\|madness]]—thoughts and remembrance fitted. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | There’s fennel for you, and columbines. There’s rue for you, and here’s some for me. We may call it herb-grace o’ Sundays. O, you must wear your rue with a difference. There’s a daisy. I would give you some violets, but they withered all when my father died. They say a made a good end.<br>(Sings) For bonny sweet Robin is all my joy. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Thought and affliction, passion, hell itself<br>She turns to favour and to prettiness. |
| [[Ophelia\|OPHELIA]] | (sings)<br>And will a not come again,<br>And will a not come again?<br>No, no, he is dead,<br>Go to thy [[Death\|death]]-bed,<br>He never will come again.<br>His beard as white as [[Snow\|snow]],<br>All flaxen was his poll.<br>He is gone, he is gone,<br>And we cast away moan.<br>God ‘a’ [[Mercy\|mercy]] on his [[Soul\|soul]].<br>And of all Christian souls, I pray God. God b’wi’ ye. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Ophelia]] and [[Gertrude]] |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Do you see this, O God? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], I must commune with your grief,<br>Or you deny me right. Go but apart,<br>Make choice of whom your wisest friends you will,<br>And they shall hear and judge ’twixt you and me.<br>If by direct or by collateral hand<br>They find us touched, we will our kingdom give,<br>Our [[Crown\|crown]], our life, and all that we call ours,<br>To you in satisfaction. But if not,<br>Be you content to lend your patience to us,<br>And we shall jointly labour with your [[Soul\|soul]]<br>To give it due content. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Let this be so.<br>His means of [[Death\|death]], his obscure burial—<br>No trophy, [[Sword\|sword]], nor hatchment o‘er his bones,<br>No noble rite nor formal ostentation—<br>Cry to be heard, as ’twere from heaven to earth,<br>That I must call’t in question. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | So you shall;<br>And where th’offence is, let the great axe fall.<br>I pray you go with me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano la [[Queen|Regina]] [[Gertrude]] e [[Horatio]] |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Non voglio parlarle. |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | È importuna,<br>e invero stravolta. Il suo stato muove a pietà. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Che cosa vuole? |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | Molto parla di suo padre, dice d’aver sentito<br>che vi son inganni al mondo, e fa hm, e si batte<br>il petto,<br>s’adira per un fuscello, dice cose in [[Doubt|dubbio]]<br>che han solo mezzo senso. Il suo dire è nulla,<br>eppure quell’uso informe di esso muove<br>chi ascolta a raccoglierne un senso. Vi mirano,<br>e rattoppano le parole a misura dei propri pensieri,<br>le quali, come i suoi cenni e i suoi gesti le porgono,<br>farebbero in verità pensare che vi sia da pensare,<br>nulla di certo, eppur molto, e assai infelicemente. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Sarebbe bene parlarle, ché può spargere<br>pericolose congetture in menti malnate.<br>Fatela entrare. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Horatio]] si ritira per far entrare [[Ophelia]] |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Alla mia [[Soul|anima]] inferma, com’è vera [[Nature|natura]] del peccato,<br>ogni inezia par prologo a qualche gran sciagura.<br>Tanto è piena d’inetto [[Jealousy|sospetto]] la [[Guilt|colpa]],<br>che si versa da sé nel temere d’esser versata. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Ophelia]] pazza, coi capelli sciolti, con un liuto |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Dov’è la bella maestà di [[Denmark|Danimarca]]? |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Ebbene, [[Ophelia]]? |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | (canta)<br>Come dal vostro vero [[Love|amore]]<br>saprò distinguere un altro?—<br>Dal cappello a conchiglie e dal bordone,<br>e dai sandali ai piedi. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Ahimè, dolce signora, che significa questo [[Song|canto]]? |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Dite? No, vi prego, ascoltate. (Canta)<br>È morto e andato, signora,<br>è morto e andato.<br>Al capo una zolla verde d’erba,<br>ai piedi una pietra. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Ma no, [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Vi prego, ascoltate.<br>(Canta)<br>Bianco il suo sudario come la [[Snow|neve]] dei monti— |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra il [[King|Re]] [[Claudius]] |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Ahimè, guardate qui, mio signore. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | (canta)<br>Cosparso di dolci [[Flowers|fiori]],<br>che alla [[Grave|tomba]] non—andò—pianto<br>da piogge di vero [[Love|amore]]. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Come state, graziosa signora? |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | [[Well|Bene]], Dio vi ricompensi. Dicono che la civetta era la figlia d’un fornaio. Signore, sappiamo ciò che siamo, ma non sappiamo ciò che potremmo essere. Dio sia alla vostra mensa! |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | (a [[Gertrude]]) Fantastica sul padre. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Vi prego, non facciamone parola, ma quando vi chiedano che voglia dire, rispondete così. (Canta)<br>Domani è il giorno di San [[Valentine|Valentino]],<br>di buon mattino per tempo,<br>e io fanciulla alla vostra [[Window|finestra]]<br>per essere la vostra [[Valentine|Valentina]].<br>Allora egli si [[Rose|levò]], e indossò le vesti,<br>e schiuse la porta della camera;<br>fece entrar la fanciulla, che fanciulla<br>non ne uscì mai più. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Graziosa [[Ophelia]]— |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Davvero, eh? Senza giuramento, la finirò.<br>(Canta) Per Gesù, e per Santa [[Charity|Carità]],<br>ahimè, e vergogna, che vergogna!<br>I giovani lo fanno se ci arrivano,<br>per Dio, la colpa è loro.<br>Diss’ella: ‘Prima che mi rovesciassi,<br>promettesti di sposarmi.’<br>L’avrei ben fatto, per quel sole lassù,<br>se al mio letto tu non venivi. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | (a [[Gertrude]]) Da quanto tempo è così? |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | [[Hope|Spero]] che tutto andrà [[Well|bene]]. Bisogna aver pazienza. Ma non posso non piangere al pensiero che lo deporranno nella fredda terra. Mio fratello lo saprà. E dunque vi ringrazio del vostro buon consiglio. Venga la mia carrozza! Buona [[Night|notte]], signore, buona [[Night|notte]], dolci signore, buona [[Night|notte]], buona [[Night|notte]]. Esce |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | (a [[Horatio]])<br>Seguitela da presso. Vegliate bene su di lei, vi prego. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce [[Horatio]] |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Oh, questo è il [[Poison|veleno]] d’un dolore profondo! Nasce<br>tutto dalla [[Death|morte]] del padre. O [[Gertrude]], [[Gertrude]],<br>quando vengono i dolori non vengono come spie isolate,<br>ma in battaglioni. Prima, il padre ucciso;<br>poi, vostro figlio partito, e lui violentissimo autore<br>del proprio giusto allontanamento; il popolo torbido,<br>denso e malsano nei pensieri e nei bisbigli<br>per la [[Death|morte]] del buon [[Polonius]]; e noi abbiamo agito da<br>sprovveduti<br>a seppellirlo di soppiatto; la povera [[Ophelia]]<br>divisa da se stessa e dal suo bel giudizio,<br>senza il quale siamo immagini o mere bestie;<br>infine, e grave quanto tutto questo insieme,<br>suo fratello è tornato in segreto dalla Francia,<br>si nutre di questo [[Wonder|stupore]], si tiene fra le nubi,<br>e non gli mancano ronzii che gl’infettino l’orecchio<br>con discorsi pestilenziali sulla [[Death|morte]] del padre;<br>nei quali la necessità, mendica di materia,<br>non esiterà punto ad accusare la nostra persona<br>d’orecchio in orecchio. O mia cara [[Gertrude]], questo,<br>come una bocca da fuoco a mitraglia, in molti punti<br>mi dà una [[Death|morte]] superflua. |
| *(didascalia)* | Rumore da dentro |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Ahimè, che rumore è questo? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Dove sono i miei Svizzeri? Guardino la porta. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra un Messaggero |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Che cosa succede? |
| MESSAGGERO | Salvatevi, mio signore.<br>L’oceano, traboccando oltre il suo confine,<br>non divora le piane con più impetuosa furia<br>di quanto il giovane [[Laertes]], a capo d’una rivolta,<br>travolga i vostri ufficiali. La plebaglia lo chiama signore,<br>e, come se il mondo cominciasse solo adesso,<br>dimenticata l’antichità, ignota l’usanza,<br>che ratificano e sorreggono ogni parola,<br>gridano ‘Scegliamo noi! [[Laertes]] sia [[King|re]].’<br>Berretti, mani e lingue lo acclamano fino alle nubi,<br>‘[[Laertes]] sia [[King|re]], [[Laertes]] [[King|re]].’ |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Con che allegria latrano sulla falsa traccia! |
| *(didascalia)* | Rumore da dentro |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Oh, seguite a ritroso, falsi [[dogs|cani]] danesi! |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Le porte sono sfondate. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Laertes]] coi suoi seguaci alla porta |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Dov’è il [[King|Re]]?—Signori, restate tutti fuori. |
| TUTTI I SUOI SEGUACI | No, entriamo anche noi. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Vi prego, lasciatemi solo. |
| TUTTI I SUOI SEGUACI | Sta bene, sta bene. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Vi ringrazio. Sorvegliate la porta. |
| *(didascalia)* | escono i seguaci |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | O tu, vile [[King|re]],<br>rendimi mio padre. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Con calma, buon [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Quella goccia di [[Blood|sangue]] che stia calma mi proclama bastardo,<br>grida cornuto a mio padre, marchia meretrice<br>proprio qui, sulla casta fronte immacolata<br>della mia vera madre. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Qual è la causa, [[Laertes]],<br>che la tua ribellione ha aspetto di gigante?—<br>Lascialo andare, [[Gertrude]]. Non [[Fear|temere]] per la nostra persona.<br>Tale divinità fa siepe attorno a un [[King|re]]<br>che il tradimento può appena adocchiare ciò che vorrebbe,<br>e poco compie del suo volere.—Dimmi, [[Laertes]],<br>perché sei tanto infiammato.—Lascialo andare, [[Gertrude]].—<br>Parla, uomo. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Dov’è mio padre? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Morto. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | (a [[Laertes]])<br>Ma non per mano sua. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Lo si lasci domandare a sazietà. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Come è morto? Non voglio esser giocato.<br>All’inferno, fedeltà! I voti al più nero demonio!<br>[[Conscience|Coscienza]] e grazia nell’abisso più profondo!<br>Sfido la dannazione. A questo punto sto fermo:<br>che entrambi i mondi abbandono alla noncuranza,<br>venga ciò che verrà. Solo, sarò vendicato<br>fino in fondo per mio padre. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Chi vi tratterrà? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | La mia volontà, non il mondo intero;<br>e quanto ai miei mezzi, li amministrerò così [[Well|bene]]<br>che andranno lontano con poco. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Buon [[Laertes]],<br>se [[Desire|desiderate]] conoscere la certezza<br>della [[Death|morte]] del vostro caro padre, è scritto nella vostra [[Revenge|vendetta]]<br>che, facendo piazza pulita, trascinerete amico e nemico,<br>vincitore e perdente? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Nessuno se non i suoi nemici. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Saprete allora riconoscerli? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Ai suoi buoni amici aprirò così larghe le braccia,<br>e, come il pio pellicano che dona la vita,<br>li nutrirò col mio [[Blood|sangue]]. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Ecco, ora parlate<br>da buon [[Child|figlio]] e da vero gentiluomo.<br>Che io sia innocente della [[Death|morte]] di vostro padre,<br>e ne provi il più sensibile dolore,<br>penetrerà nel vostro giudizio così diritto<br>come il giorno nel vostro occhio. |
| *(didascalia)* | Rumore da dentro |
| VOCI | (da dentro) Fatela entrare. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Ebbene, che rumore è quello? |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Ophelia]] come prima |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | O calore, disseccami il cervello! [[Tears|Lacrime]] sette volte salate<br>bruciate il senso e la virtù del mio occhio!<br>Per il cielo, la tua [[Madness|follia]] sarà pagata a peso<br>finché il nostro piatto non pieghi la stadera. O [[Rose|rosa]] di maggio,<br>cara fanciulla, buona sorella, dolce [[Ophelia]]!<br>O cieli, è possibile che il senno d’una giovane<br>sia mortale come la vita d’un vecchio?<br>La [[Nature|natura]] è sottile nell’[[Love|amore]], e dov’è sottile<br>manda qualche prezioso pegno di sé<br>dietro la cosa che ama. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | (canta)<br>Lo portarono a viso scoperto sulla bara,<br>ehi non nonny, nonny, ehi nonny,<br>e sulla sua [[Grave|tomba]] piovve più d’una lacrima—<br>Sta’ [[Well|bene]], colomba mia. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Avessi il tuo senno e mi spingessi alla [[Revenge|vendetta]],<br>non potresti commuovermi così. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Voi dovete cantare ‘Giù, a-giù’, e voi, ‘Chiamatelo a-giù-a’. Oh, come le sta bene la [[Wheel|ruota]]! È il falso castaldo che rubò la figlia del padrone. [[Laertes|LAERTES]] Questo nulla è più che sostanza. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Ecco il rosmarino, è per il ricordo. Ti prego, [[Love|amore]], ricorda. E ci sono le viole del pensiero; sono per i pensieri. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Un insegnamento nella [[Madness|follia]]—pensieri e ricordo bene appaiati. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | Ecco il finocchio per voi, e le aquilegie. Ecco la ruta per voi, e un poco ne serbo per me. La possiamo chiamare erba della grazia della domenica. Oh, voi dovete portare la vostra ruta in altra maniera. Ecco una margherita. Vi darei delle violette, ma appassirono tutte quando mio padre morì. Dicono che fece una buona fine.<br>(Canta) Ché il bel dolce Robin è tutta la mia gioia. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Pensiero e afflizione, passione, l’inferno stesso<br>ella muta in grazia e in leggiadria. |
| [[Ophelia|OPHELIA]] | (canta)<br>E non tornerà più,<br>e non tornerà più?<br>No, no, egli è morto,<br>va’ al tuo letto di [[Death|morte]],<br>egli non tornerà mai più.<br>La sua barba bianca come [[Snow|neve]],<br>tutto di lino il suo capo.<br>È andato, è andato,<br>e noi gettiamo via il lamento.<br>Dio abbia [[Mercy|pietà]] della sua [[Soul|anima]].<br>E di tutte le anime cristiane, ne prego Dio. Dio sia con voi. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono [[Ophelia]] e [[Gertrude]] |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Vedi questo, o Dio? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], devo aver parte al vostro dolore,<br>o mi negate un diritto. Ritiratevi in disparte,<br>scegliete quelli che volete fra i vostri amici più saggi,<br>ed essi udranno e giudicheranno fra voi e me.<br>Se per mano diretta o indiretta<br>ci trovino implicati, daremo il nostro regno,<br>la nostra [[Crown|corona]], la nostra vita, e tutto ciò che diciamo nostro,<br>a voi in riparazione. Ma se no,<br>contentatevi di prestarci la vostra pazienza,<br>e insieme opereremo con la vostra [[Soul|anima]]<br>per darle la giusta soddisfazione. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Sia dunque così.<br>Il modo della sua [[Death|morte]], l’oscura sepoltura—<br>né trofeo, né [[Sword|spada]], né stemma sulle sue ossa,<br>né nobile rito né solenne pompa—<br>gridano d’esser uditi, come dal cielo alla terra,<br>sì ch’io debba chiederne conto. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | E così sarà;<br>e dov’è il delitto, cada la grande scure.<br>Vi prego, venite con me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_6" data-title="Hamlet — Act IV, Scene 6" data-chapter="Scene 6" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/death,motif/ship,concept/mercy,setting/ship,character/guildenstern,character/hamlet,character/horatio,character/rosencrantz"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Horatio]] with a Servant |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What are they that would speak with me? |
| SERVANT | Sailors, sir. They say they have letters for you. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Let them come in. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Servant |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | I do not know from what part of the world<br>I should be greeted if not from Lord [[Hamlet]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Sailors |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | A SAILOR God bless you, sir. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Let him bless thee too.<br>A SAILOR A shall, sir, an’t please him. There’s a letter for you, sir. It comes from th’ambassador that was bound for England—if your name be [[Horatio]], as I am let to know it is. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (reads) ‘[[Horatio]], when thou shalt have overlooked this, give these fellows some means to the [[King]]. They have letters for him. Ere we were two days old at sea, a pirate of very warlike appointment gave us chase. Finding ourselves too slow of sail, we put on a compelled valour, and in the grapple I boarded them. On the instant they got clear of our [[Ship\|ship]], so I [[Alone\|alone]] became their prisoner. They have dealt with me like thieves of [[Mercy\|mercy]]; but they knew what they did: I am to do a good turn for them. Let the [[King]] have the letters I have sent, and repair thou to me with as much haste as thou wouldst fly [[Death\|death]]. I have words to speak in thine ear will make thee dumb, yet are they much too light for the bore of the matter. These good fellows will bring thee where I am. [[Rosencrantz]] and [[Guildenstern]] hold their course for England. Of them I have much to tell thee. Farewell.<br>He that thou knowest thine,<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>Come, I will give you way for these your letters,<br>And do’t the speedier that you may direct me<br>To him from whom you brought them. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Horatio]] con un servo |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | Chi sono costoro che vogliono parlarmi? |
| SERVO | Marinai, signore. Dicono d’aver lettere per voi. |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | Falli entrare. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce il servo |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | Non so da quale parte del mondo<br>mi debba venire un saluto, se non da messer [[Hamlet]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano i marinai |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | UN MARINAIO Dio vi benedica, signore. |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | Che benedica anche te.<br>UN MARINAIO Lo farà, signore, se così gli piace. C’è una lettera per voi, signore. Viene dall’ambasciatore che era diretto in Inghilterra—se il vostro nome è [[Horatio]], come mi si dà a intendere che sia. |
| [[Horatio|HORATIO]] | (legge) ‘[[Horatio]], quando avrai scorso questa, procura a costoro qualche modo d’accedere al [[King|re]]. Hanno lettere per lui. Prima che fossimo due giorni in mare, un pirata d’armamento assai bellicoso ci diede la caccia. Trovandoci troppo lenti di vela, ci vestimmo d’un valore forzato, e nell’arrembaggio io saltai a bordo loro. Sull’istante si liberarono della nostra [[Ship|nave]], sicché io [[Alone|solo]] rimasi loro prigioniero. Si sono comportati con me da ladroni di [[Mercy|misericordia]]; ma sapevano quel che facevano: dovrò render loro un buon servigio. Fa’ che il [[King|re]] abbia le lettere che ho mandato, e vieni da me con la stessa fretta con cui fuggiresti la [[Death|morte]]. Ho parole da dirti all’orecchio che ti faranno ammutolire, eppure sono troppo leggere per il calibro della faccenda. Questi bravi uomini ti condurranno dove mi trovo. [[Rosencrantz]] e [[Guildenstern]] tengono la rotta per l’Inghilterra. Di loro ho molto da raccontarti. Addio.<br>Colui che sai esser tuo,<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>Venite, vi darò modo di recapitare queste vostre lettere,<br>e tanto più in fretta perché possiate guidarmi<br>a colui dal quale le portaste. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_7" data-title="Hamlet — Act IV, Scene 7" data-chapter="Scene 7" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/fire,motif/flowers,motif/home,motif/horse,motif/leaves,motif/love,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/soul,motif/spring,motif/tears,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/conscience,concept/fear,concept/hope,concept/nature,concept/revenge,character/claudio,character/claudius,character/gertrude,character/hamlet,character/laertes,character/mermaid,character/ophelia,character/queen"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Claudius]] and [[Laertes]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Now must your [[Conscience\|conscience]] my acquittance seal,<br>And you must put me in your heart for friend,<br>Sith you have heard, and with a knowing ear,<br>That he which hath your noble father slain<br>Pursued my life. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | It [[Well\|well]] appears. But tell me<br>Why you proceeded not against these feats,<br>So crimeful and so capital in [[Nature\|nature]],<br>As by your safety, wisdom, all things else,<br>You mainly were stirred up. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, for two special reasons,<br>Which may to you perhaps seem much unsinewed,<br>And yet to me they’re strong. The [[Queen]] his mother<br>Lives almost by his looks; and for myself—<br>My virtue or my plague, be it either which—<br>She’s so conjunctive to my life and [[Soul\|soul]]<br>That, as the star moves not but in his sphere,<br>I could not but by her. The other motive<br>Why to a public count I might not go<br>Is the great [[Love\|love]] the general gender bear him,<br>Who, dipping all his faults in their affection,<br>Would, like the [[spring]] that turneth wood to stone,<br>[[convert\|Convert]] his guilts to graces; so that my arrows,<br>Too slightly timbered for so loud a [[Wind\|wind]],<br>Would have reverted to my bow again,<br>And not where I had aimed them. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | And so have I a noble father lost,<br>A sister driven into desp’rate terms,<br>Who has, if praises may go back again,<br>Stood challenger, on mount, of all the age<br>For her perfections. But my [[Revenge\|revenge]] will come. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Break not your sleeps for that. You must not think<br>That we are made of stuff so flat and dull<br>That we can let our beard be shook with danger,<br>And think it pastime. You shortly shall hear more.<br>I loved your father, and we [[Love\|love]] ourself.<br>And that, I [[Hope\|hope]], will teach you to imagine— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger with letters |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How now? What news? |
| MESSENGER | Letters, my lord, from [[Hamlet]].<br>This to your majesty; this to the [[Queen]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | From [[Hamlet]]? Who brought them? |
| MESSENGER | Sailors, my lord, they say. I saw them not.<br>They were given me by [[Claudio]]. He received them. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], you shall hear them.—Leave us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Messenger |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (Reads) ‘High and mighty, you shall know I am set naked on your kingdom. Tomorrow shall I beg leave to see your kingly eyes, when I shall, first asking your pardon, thereunto recount th’occasions of my sudden and more strange return.<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>What should this mean? Are all the rest come back? Or is it some abuse, and no such thing? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Know you the hand? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | ’Tis [[Hamlet]]’s character.<br>‘Naked’—and in a postscript here he says<br>‘[[Alone]]’. Can you advise me? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I’m lost in it, my lord. But let him come.<br>It warms the very sickness in my heart<br>That I shall live and tell him to his teeth,<br>‘Thus diddest thou’. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | If it be so, [[Laertes]]—<br>As how should it be so, how otherwise?—<br>Will you be ruled by me? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | If so you’ll not o’errule me to a peace. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | To thine own peace. If he be now returned,<br>As checking at his voyage, and that he means<br>No more to undertake it, I will work him<br>To an exploit, now ripe in my device,<br>Under the which he shall not choose but fall;<br>And for his [[Death\|death]] no [[Wind\|wind]] of blame shall breathe;<br>But even his mother shall uncharge the practice<br>And call it accident. Some two months since<br>Here was a gentleman of Normandy.<br>I’ve seen myself, and served against, the French,<br>And they can [[Well\|well]] on horseback; but this gallant<br>Had witchcraft in’t. He grew into his seat,<br>And to such wondrous doing brought his [[Horse\|horse]]<br>As had he been incorpsed and demi-natured<br>With the brave beast. So far he passed my thought<br>That I in forgery of shapes and tricks<br>Come short of what he did. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | A Norman was’t? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | A Norman. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Upon my life, Lamord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | The very same. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I know him [[Well\|well]]. He is the brooch indeed,<br>And gem, of all the nation. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | He made confession of you,<br>And gave you such a masterly report<br>For art and exercise in your defence,<br>And for your rapier most especially,<br>That he cried out ‘twould be a sight indeed<br>If one could match you. Sir, this report of his<br>Did [[Hamlet]] so envenom with his envy<br>That he could nothing do but wish and beg<br>Your sudden coming o’er to play with him.<br>Now, out of this— |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What out of this, my lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], was your father dear to you?<br>Or are you like the painting of a sorrow,<br>A face without a heart? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Why ask you this? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Not that I think you did not [[Love\|love]] your father,<br>But that I know [[Love\|love]] is begun by [[Time\|time]],<br>And that I see, in passages of proof,<br>[[Time]] qualifies the spark and [[Fire\|fire]] of it.<br>[[Hamlet]] comes back. What would you undertake<br>To show yourself your father’s son in deed<br>More than in words? |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | To cut his throat i’th’ church. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | No place indeed should murder sanctuarize.<br>[[Revenge]] should have no bounds. But, good [[Laertes]],<br>Will you do this?—keep close within your chamber.<br>[[Hamlet]] returned shall know you are come [[Home\|home]].<br>We’ll put on those shall praise your excellence,<br>And set a [[Double\|double]] varnish on the fame<br>The Frenchman gave you; bring you, in fine, together,<br>And wager on your heads. He, being remiss,<br>Most generous, and free from all contriving,<br>Will not peruse the foils; so that with ease,<br>Or with a little shuffling, you may choose<br>A [[Sword\|sword]] unbated, and, in a pass of practice,<br>Requite him for your father. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | I will do’t,<br>And for that purpose I’ll anoint my [[Sword\|sword]].<br>I bought an unction of a mountebank<br>So mortal that, but dip a knife in it,<br>Where it draws [[Blood\|blood]] no cataplasm so rare,<br>Collected from all simples that have virtue<br>Under the [[Moon\|moon]], can save the thing from [[Death\|death]]<br>That is but scratched withal. I’ll touch my point<br>With this contagion, that if I gall him slightly,<br>It may be [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let’s further think of this;<br>Weigh what convenience both of [[Time\|time]] and means<br>May fit us to our shape. If this should fail,<br>And that our drift look through our bad performance,<br>‘Twere better not essayed. Therefore this project<br>Should have a back or second that might hold<br>If this should blast in proof. Soft, let me see.<br>We’ll make a solemn wager on your cunnings ...<br>I ha’t! When in your motion you are hot and dry—<br>As make your bouts more violent to that end—<br>And that he calls for drink, I’ll have prepared him<br>A chalice for the nonce, whereon but sipping,<br>If he by chance escape your venomed stuck,<br>Our purpose may hold there.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | How now, sweet [[Queen]]? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | One woe doth tread upon another’s heel,<br>So fast they follow. Your sister’s drowned, [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Drowned? O, where? |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | There is a willow grows aslant a brook<br>That shows his hoar [[Leaves\|leaves]] in the glassy stream.<br>Therewith fantastic garlands did she make<br>Of crow-[[Flowers\|flowers]], nettles, daisies, and long purples,<br>That liberal shepherds give a grosser name,<br>But our cold maids do dead men’s fingers call them.<br>There on the pendent boughs her crownet weeds<br>Clamb’ring to hang, an envious sliver broke,<br>When down the weedy trophies and herself<br>Fell in the weeping brook. Her clothes spread wide,<br>And [[Mermaid\|mermaid]]-like a while they bore her up;<br>Which [[Time\|time]] she chanted snatches of old tunes,<br>As one incapable of her own distress,<br>Or like a creature native and endued<br>Unto that element. But long it could not be<br>Till that her garments, heavy with their drink,<br>Pulled the poor wretch from her melodious lay<br>To muddy [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Alas, then is she drowned. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Drowned, drowned. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Too much of [[Water\|water]] hast thou, poor [[Ophelia]],<br>And therefore I forbid my [[Tears\|tears]]. But yet<br>It is our trick; [[Nature\|nature]] her custom holds,<br>Let shame say what it will.<br>He weeps<br>When these are gone,<br>The woman will be out. Adieu, my lord.<br>I have a speech of [[Fire\|fire]] that fain would blaze,<br>But that this folly douts it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | Let’s follow, [[Gertrude]].<br>How much I had to do to calm his rage!<br>Now [[Fear\|fear]] I this will give it start again;<br>Therefore let’s follow. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[King|il Re]] [[Claudius]] e [[Laertes]] |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Ora la vostra [[Conscience|coscienza]] deve sigillare il mio proscioglimento,<br>E dovete accogliermi nel cuore come amico,<br>Poiché avete udito, e con orecchio consapevole,<br>Che colui che ha ucciso il vostro nobile padre<br>Attentava alla mia vita. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | [[Well|Bene]] appare. Ma ditemi<br>Perché non procedeste contro tali atti,<br>Così criminosi e capitali per [[Nature|natura]],<br>Come dalla vostra sicurezza, saggezza e ogni altra cosa<br>Eravate potentemente spinto. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Oh, per due ragioni particolari,<br>Che a voi forse parranno assai senza nerbo,<br>E che pure a me son forti. La [[Queen|Regina]] sua madre<br>Vive quasi dei suoi sguardi; e quanto a me—<br>Mia virtù o mia piaga, sia l’una o l’altra—<br>Ella è così congiunta alla mia vita e alla mia [[Soul|anima]]<br>Che, come l’astro non si muove se non nella sua sfera,<br>Io non potrei muovermi se non per lei. L’altro motivo<br>Per cui non potei venire a un pubblico processo<br>È il grande [[Love|amore]] che il volgo gli porta,<br>Il quale, immergendo tutte le sue colpe nel proprio affetto,<br>Come la [[spring|sorgente]] che muta il legno in pietra,<br>[[convert|Convertirebbe]] le sue colpe in grazie; sicché le mie frecce,<br>Troppo leggere d’asta per un [[Wind|vento]] così forte,<br>Sarebbero tornate al mio stesso arco,<br>E non dove le avevo mirate. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | E così ho perduto un nobile padre,<br>Una sorella sospinta a disperate condizioni,<br>Che aveva, se le lodi possono tornare indietro,<br>Sfidato dall’alto tutta l’età presente<br>Per le sue perfezioni. Ma la mia [[Revenge|vendetta]] verrà. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Non perdete il sonno per questo. Non dovete credere<br>Che siamo fatti di stoffa così fiacca e ottusa<br>Da lasciarci scuotere la barba dal pericolo<br>E stimarlo un passatempo. Presto udrete di più.<br>Amavo vostro padre, e [[Love|amiamo]] noi stessi.<br>E ciò, [[Hope|spero]], v’insegnerà a immaginare— |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra un Messaggero con lettere |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Ebbene? Che notizie? |
| MESSAGGERO | Lettere, mio signore, da [[Hamlet]].<br>Questa a vostra maestà; questa alla [[Queen|Regina]]. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Da [[Hamlet]]? Chi le ha portate? |
| MESSAGGERO | Marinai, mio signore, così dicono. Io non li ho veduti.<br>Mi furono date da [[Claudio]]. Egli le ricevette. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], le udrete.—Lasciateci. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce il Messaggero |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | (Legge) ‘Alto e potente, saprete che sono stato posto nudo nel vostro regno. Domani chiederò licenza di vedere i vostri occhi regali, quando, chiedendo dapprima il vostro perdono, esporrò le occasioni del mio subitaneo e più che strano ritorno.<br>[[Hamlet]].’<br>Che vorrà dir questo? Sono tornati tutti gli altri? O è qualche inganno, e nulla di simile? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Conoscete la scrittura? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | È il carattere di [[Hamlet]].<br>‘Nudo’—e in un poscritto qui dice<br>‘[[Alone|Solo]]’. Potete consigliarmi? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Mi ci perdo, mio signore. Ma che venga.<br>Riscalda proprio il male del mio cuore<br>Il pensiero che vivrò per dirgli in faccia<br>‘Così tu facesti’. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Se è così, [[Laertes]]—<br>E come potrebbe esser così, come altrimenti?—<br>Vi lascerete guidare da me? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Purché non mi guidiate a una pace. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Alla vostra propria pace. Se ora è tornato,<br>Rifiutando il suo viaggio, e se intende<br>Non intraprenderlo più, io lo indurrò<br>A un’impresa, ora matura nel mio disegno,<br>Sotto la quale non potrà che cadere;<br>E per la sua [[Death|morte]] nessun [[Wind|vento]] di biasimo spirerà;<br>Ma perfino sua madre assolverà la trama<br>E la chiamerà accidente. Or son due mesi<br>Era qui un gentiluomo di Normandia.<br>Io stesso ho veduto, e ho combattuto contro, i Francesi,<br>E sanno [[Well|bene]] stare a cavallo; ma quel galante<br>Aveva in ciò della stregoneria. Cresceva tutt’uno con la sella,<br>E a tali prodigiose prove conduceva il suo [[Horse|cavallo]]<br>Come s’ei fosse incorporato e mezzo naturato<br>Con la brava bestia. Tanto superò il mio pensiero<br>Che io, nel foggiare figure e destrezze,<br>Resto al di sotto di quel ch’egli fece. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Era un Normanno? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Un Normanno. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Sulla mia vita, Lamord. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Proprio lui. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Lo conosco [[Well|bene]]. È davvero il fermaglio,<br>E la gemma, di tutta la nazione. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Egli fece di voi confessione,<br>E vi diede un elogio così magistrale<br>Per l’arte e l’esercizio nella vostra difesa,<br>E per lo stocco vostro specialmente,<br>Che gridò che sarebbe uno spettacolo davvero<br>Se alcuno potesse misurarsi con voi. Signore, questo suo elogio<br>Avvelenò [[Hamlet]] a tal punto d’invidia<br>Che altro non poté fare che desiderare e implorare<br>Il vostro subitaneo ritorno per gareggiare con lui.<br>Ora, da questo— |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Che cosa, da questo, mio signore? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | [[Laertes]], vi era caro vostro padre?<br>O siete voi come la pittura d’un dolore,<br>Un volto senza cuore? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Perché chiedete questo? |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Non ch’io pensi che non [[Love|amaste]] vostro padre,<br>Ma perché so che l’[[Love|amore]] è generato dal [[Time|tempo]],<br>E perché vedo, in prove d’esperienza,<br>Che il [[Time|tempo]] ne attenua la scintilla e il [[Fire|fuoco]].<br>[[Hamlet]] ritorna. Che cosa intraprendereste<br>Per mostrarvi figlio di vostro padre in atto<br>Più che in parole? |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Tagliargli la gola in chiesa. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Nessun luogo davvero dovrebbe far santuario all’omicidio.<br>La [[Revenge|vendetta]] non dovrebbe aver confini. Ma, buon [[Laertes]],<br>Volete far questo?—tenetevi chiuso nella vostra camera.<br>[[Hamlet]], tornato, saprà che siete giunto a [[Home|casa]].<br>Metteremo su gente che lodi la vostra eccellenza,<br>E porremo una [[Double|doppia]] vernice sulla fama<br>Che il Francese vi diede; vi metteremo, infine, a fronte,<br>E scommetteremo sulle vostre teste. Egli, essendo negligente,<br>Generosissimo, e alieno da ogni macchinazione,<br>Non esaminerà i fioretti; sicché con facilità,<br>O con un piccolo scambio, potrete scegliere<br>Una [[Sword|spada]] non spuntata, e, in una botta d’esercizio,<br>Ripagarlo per vostro padre. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Lo farò,<br>E a tal fine ungerò la mia [[Sword|spada]].<br>Comprai un unguento da un ciarlatano<br>Così mortale che, basta immergervi un coltello,<br>E dove esso trae [[Blood|sangue]] nessun cataplasma, per raro che sia,<br>Raccolto da tutti i semplici che hanno virtù<br>Sotto la [[Moon|luna]], può salvare dalla [[Death|morte]] la creatura<br>Che ne sia appena graffiata. Toccherò la mia punta<br>Con questo contagio, sì che, se lo scalfisco appena,<br>Possa esser [[Death|morte]]. |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Pensiamoci ancora;<br>Pesiamo quale convenienza di [[Time|tempo]] e di mezzi<br>Possa adattarci al nostro disegno. Se questo fallisse,<br>E il nostro intento trasparisse dalla cattiva esecuzione,<br>Meglio non averlo tentato. Perciò questo progetto<br>Dovrebbe avere un sostegno o un secondo che tenga<br>Se il primo scoppiasse alla prova. Piano, lasciate ch’io veda.<br>Faremo una solenne scommessa sulle vostre destrezze ...<br>L’ho trovato! Quando nel moto sarete caldo e assetato—<br>E rendete a tal fine più violenti i vostri assalti—<br>Ed egli chiederà da bere, io gli avrò preparato<br>Un calice per l’occasione, che, appena sorseggiato,<br>Se per caso sfuggisse alla vostra stoccata avvelenata,<br>Il nostro scopo potrà reggersi là.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Queen|la Regina]] [[Gertrude]] |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Ebbene, dolce [[Queen|Regina]]? |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Una sciagura calca il calcagno di un’altra,<br>Tanto in fretta si seguono. Vostra sorella è annegata, [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Annegata? Oh, dove? |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | C’è un salice che cresce obliquo sopra un ruscello<br>E mostra le sue canute [[Leaves|foglie]] nella corrente vitrea.<br>Con esse ella intrecciava fantastiche ghirlande<br>Di [[Flowers|fiori]] di ranuncolo, ortiche, margherite e lunghi orchidei,<br>Cui i licenziosi pastori danno un nome più grossolano,<br>Ma che le nostre caste fanciulle chiaman dita di morto.<br>Là, sui rami pendenti, mentre s’arrampicava<br>Per appendervi le sue corone d’erbe, un ramo invidioso si ruppe,<br>E giù i trofei d’erbe ed ella stessa<br>Caddero nel ruscello piangente. Le vesti si distesero ampie,<br>E come [[Mermaid|sirena]] per un poco la sostennero;<br>Nel qual [[Time|tempo]] ella cantava brani d’antiche arie,<br>Come chi non sente la propria sciagura,<br>O come creatura nata e adatta<br>A quell’elemento. Ma lungo non poté durare<br>Finché le sue vesti, pesanti dell’acqua bevuta,<br>Trassero la povera infelice dal suo melodioso canto<br>A una fangosa [[Death|morte]]. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Ahimè, dunque è annegata. |
| [[Queen|REGINA]] [[Gertrude|GERTRUDE]] | Annegata, annegata. |
| [[Laertes|LAERTES]] | Troppa [[Water|acqua]] hai avuto, povera [[Ophelia]],<br>E perciò vieto le mie [[Tears|lacrime]]. Eppure<br>È il nostro vezzo; la [[Nature|natura]] tiene la sua usanza,<br>Dica la vergogna ciò che vuole.<br>Piange<br>Quando queste saran finite,<br>La donna sarà fuori di me. Addio, mio signore.<br>Ho un discorso di [[Fire|fuoco]] che vorrebbe divampare,<br>Se non che questa follia lo spegne. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| [[King|RE]] [[Claudius|CLAUDIUS]] | Seguiamolo, [[Gertrude]].<br>Quanto ho dovuto fare per calmare la sua furia!<br>Ora [[Fear|temo]] che questo la faccia ripartire;<br>Perciò seguiamolo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_5--scene_1" data-title="Hamlet — Act V, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,topos/quest,archetype/fool,archetype/king,motif/death,motif/dust,motif/flowers,motif/grave,motif/home,motif/horse,motif/love,motif/night,motif/silence,motif/soul,motif/spring,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/wind,motif/winter,concept/body,concept/faith,concept/fear,concept/imagination,concept/madness,concept/truth,concept/wonder,setting/denmark,setting/mountains,character/cain,character/claudius,character/gertrude,character/hamlet,character/horatio,character/laertes,character/ophelia,character/queen"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter two Clowns carrying a spade and a pickaxe |
| FIRST CLOWN | Is she to be buried in Christian burial that wilfully seeks her own salvation? |
| SECOND CLOWN | I tell thee she is, and therefore make her [[Grave\|grave]] straight. The coroner hath sat on her, and finds it Christian burial. |
| FIRST CLOWN | How can that be unless she drowned herself in her own defence? |
| SECOND CLOWN | Why, ’tis found so. |
| FIRST CLOWN | It must be se offendendo, it cannot be else; for here lies the point: if I drown myself wittingly, it argues an act; and an act hath three branches: it is to act, to do, and to perform. Argal she drowned herself wittingly. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Nay, but hear you, Goodman Delver. |
| FIRST CLOWN | Give me leave. Here lies the [[Water\|water]]—good. Here stands the man—good. If the man go to this [[Water\|water]] and drown himself, it is, will he nill he, he goes. Mark you that. But if the [[Water\|water]] come to him and drown him, he drowns not himself; argal he that is not guilty of his own [[Death\|death]] shortens not his own life. |
| SECOND CLOWN | But is this law? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Ay, marry, is’t: coroner’s [[Quest\|quest]] law. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Will you ha’ the [[Truth\|truth]] on’t? If this had not been a gentlewoman, she should have been buried out o’ Christian burial. |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, there thou sayst, and the more pity that great folk should have count’nance in this world to drown or hang themselves more than their even Christian. Come, my spade. There is no ancient gentlemen but gardeners, ditchers, and gravemakers; they hold up Adam’s profession. |
| *(didascalia)* | First Clown digs |
| SECOND CLOWN | Was he a gentleman? |
| FIRST CLOWN | A was the first that ever bore arms. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Why, he had none. |
| FIRST CLOWN | What, art a heathen? How dost thou understand the Scripture? The Scripture says Adam digged. Could he dig without arms? I’ll put another question to thee. If thou answerest me not to the purpose, confess thyself— |
| SECOND CLOWN | Go to. |
| FIRST CLOWN | What is he that builds stronger than either the mason, the shipwright, or the carpenter? |
| SECOND CLOWN | The gallows-maker; for that frame outlives a thousand tenants. |
| FIRST CLOWN | I like thy wit [[Well\|well]], in good [[Faith\|faith]]. The gallows does [[Well\|well]]. But how does it [[Well\|well]]? It does [[Well\|well]] to those that do ill. Now thou dost ill to say the gallows is built stronger than the church, argal the gallows may do [[Well\|well]] to thee. To’t again, come. |
| SECOND CLOWN | ‘Who builds stronger than a mason, a shipwright, or a carpenter?’ |
| FIRST CLOWN | Ay, tell me that, and unyoke. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Marry, now I can tell. |
| FIRST CLOWN | To’t. |
| SECOND CLOWN | Mass, I cannot tell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince [[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] afar off |
| FIRST CLOWN | Cudgel thy brains no more about it, for your dull ass will not mend his pace with beating; and when you are asked this question next, say ‘a [[Grave\|grave]]-maker’; the houses that he makes lasts till doomsday. Go, get thee to Johan. Fetch me a stoup of liquor. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Second Clown |
| FIRST CLOWN | (Sings)<br>In youth when I did [[Love\|love]], did [[Love\|love]],<br>Methought it was very sweet<br>To contract-O-the [[Time\|time]] for-a-my behove,<br>O methought there-a-was nothing-a-meet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Has this fellow no feeling of his business that a sings at [[Grave\|grave]]-making? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Custom hath made it in him a property of easiness. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | ‘Tis e’en so; the hand of little employment hath the daintier sense. |
| FIRST CLOWN | (sings)<br>But age with his stealing steps<br>Hath caught me in his clutch,<br>And hath shipped me intil the land,<br>As if I had never been such. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws up a skull |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | That skull had a tongue in it and could sing once. How the knave jowls it to th’ ground as if ‘twere [[Cain]]’s jawbone, that did the first murder! This might be the pate of a politician which this ass o’er-offices, one that would circumvent God, might it not? [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] It might, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Or of a courtier, which could say ‘Good morrow, sweet lord. How dost thou, good lord?’ This might be my lord such a one, that praised my lord such a one’s [[Horse\|horse]] when a meant to beg it, might it not? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, e‘en so, and now my lady Worm’s, chapless, and knocked about the mazard with a sexton’s spade. Here’s fine revolution, an we had the trick to see’t. Did these bones cost no more the breeding but to play at loggats with ’em? Mine ache to think on’t. |
| FIRST CLOWN | (sings)<br>A pickaxe and a spade, a spade,<br>For and a shrouding-sheet;<br>O, a pit of clay for to be made<br>For such a guest is meet. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws up another skull |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | There’s another. Why might not that be the skull of a lawyer? Where be his quiddits now, his quillets, his cases, his tenures, and his tricks? Why does he suffer this rude knave now to knock him about the sconce with a dirty shovel, and will not tell him of his action of battery? H‘m! This fellow might be in ’s [[Time\|time]] a great buyer of land, with his statutes, his recognizances, his fines, his [[Double\|double]] vouchers, his recoveries. Is this the fine of his fines and the recovery of his recoveries, to have his fine pate full of fine dirt? Will his vouchers vouch him no more of his purchases, and [[Double\|double]] ones too, than the length and breadth of a pair of indentures? The very conveyances of his lands will hardly lie in this box; and must th’inheritor himself have no more, ha? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Not a jot more, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Is not parchment made of sheepskins? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | Ay, my lord, and of calf-skins too. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | They are sheep and calves that seek out assurance in that. I will speak to this fellow. (To the First Clown) Whose [[Grave\|grave]]’s this, sirrah? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Mine, sir.<br>(Sings)<br>O, a pit of clay for to be made<br>For such a guest is meet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I think it be thine indeed, for thou liest in’t. |
| FIRST CLOWN | You lie out on‘t, sir, and therefore it is not yours. For my part, I do not lie in’t, and yet it is mine. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thou dost lie in‘t, to be in’t and say ’tis thine. ’Tis for the dead, not for the quick; therefore thou liest. |
| FIRST CLOWN | ‘Tis a quick lie, sir, ’twill away again from me to you. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What man dost thou dig it for? |
| FIRST CLOWN | For no man, sir. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | What woman, then? |
| FIRST CLOWN | For none, neither. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Who is to be buried in’t? |
| FIRST CLOWN | One that was a woman, sir; but, rest her [[Soul\|soul]], she’s dead. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How absolute the knave is! We must speak by the card, or equivocation will undo us. By the Lord, [[Horatio]], these three years I have taken note of it. The age is grown so picked that the toe of the peasant comes so near the heel of the courtier he galls his kibe. (To the First Clown) How long hast thou been a [[Grave\|grave]]-maker? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Of all the days i‘th’ year I came to’t that day that our last [[King]] [[Hamlet]] o’ercame Fortinbras. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How long is that since? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Cannot you tell that? Every [[Fool\|fool]] can tell that. It was the very day that young [[Hamlet]] was born—he that was mad and sent into England. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Ay, marry, why was he sent into England? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, because a was mad. A shall recover his wits there; or if a do not, ’tis no great matter there. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why? |
| FIRST CLOWN | ’Twill not be seen in him there. There the men are as mad as he. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How came he mad? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Very strangely, they say. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How strangely? |
| FIRST CLOWN | [[Faith]], e’en with losing his wits. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Upon what ground? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, here in [[Denmark]]. I have been sexton here, man and boy, thirty years. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | How long will a man lie i’th’ earth ere he rot? |
| FIRST CLOWN | I’[[Faith\|faith]], if a be not rotten before a die—as we have many pocky corpses nowadays, that will scarce hold the laying in—a will last you some eight year or nine year. A tanner will last you nine year. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why he more than another? |
| FIRST CLOWN | Why, sir, his hide is so tanned with his trade that a will keep out [[Water\|water]] a great while, and your [[Water\|water]] is a sore decayer of your whoreson dead [[Body\|body]]. Here’s a skull, now. This skull has lain in the earth three-and-twenty years. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Whose was it? |
| FIRST CLOWN | A whoreson mad fellow’s it was. Whose do you think it was? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Nay, I know not. |
| FIRST CLOWN | A pestilence on him for a mad rogue—a poured a flagon of Rhenish on my head once! This same skull, sir, was Yorick’s skull, the [[King]]’s jester. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | This? |
| FIRST CLOWN | E’en that. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Let me see.<br>He takes the skull<br>Alas, poor Yorick. I knew him, [[Horatio]]—a fellow of infinite jest, of most excellent fancy. He hath borne me on his back a thousand times; and now, how abhorred my [[Imagination\|imagination]] is! My gorge rises at it. Here hung those lips that I have kissed I know not how oft. Where be your gibes now, your gambols, your [[songs]], your flashes of merriment that were wont to set the table on a roar? Not one now to mock your own grinning? Quite chop-fallen? Now get you to my lady’s chamber and tell her, let her paint an inch thick, to this favour she must come. Make her laugh at that. Prithee, [[Horatio]], tell me one thing. |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | What’s that, my lord? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Dost thou think Alexander looked o’ this fashion i’th’ earth? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | E’en so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And smelt so? Pah! |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws the skull down |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | E’en so, my lord. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | To what base uses we may return, [[Horatio]]! Why may not [[Imagination\|imagination]] trace the noble [[Dust\|dust]] of Alexander till a find it stopping a bung-hole? |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | ’Twere to consider too curiously to consider so. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | No, [[Faith\|faith]], not a jot; but to follow him thither with modesty enough, and likelihood to lead it, as thus: Alexander died, Alexander was buried, Alexander returneth into [[Dust\|dust]], the [[Dust\|dust]] is earth, of earth we make loam, and why of that loam whereto he was converted might they not stop a beer-barrel?<br>Imperial Caesar, dead and turned to clay,<br>Might stop a hole to keep the [[Wind\|wind]] away.<br>O, that that earth which kept the world in awe<br>Should patch a wall t’expel the [[Winter\|winter]]’s flaw!<br>But soft, but soft; aside.<br>[[Hamlet]] and [[Horatio]] stand aside. Enter [[King]]<br><br>[[Claudius]], [[Queen]] [[Gertrude]], [[Laertes]], and a coffin,<br><br>with a Priest and lords attendant<br>Here comes the [[King]],<br>The [[Queen]], the courtiers—who is that they follow,<br>And with such maimed rites? This doth betoken<br>The corpse they follow did with desp‘rate hand<br>Fordo it own life. ’Twas of some estate.<br>Couch we a while, and mark. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What ceremony else? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside to [[Horatio]])<br>That is [[Laertes]], a very noble youth. Mark. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | What ceremony else? |
| PRIEST | Her obsequies have been as far enlarged<br>As we have warrantise. Her [[Death\|death]] was doubtful,<br>And but that great command o’ersways the order<br>She should in ground unsanctified have lodged<br>Till the last trumpet. For charitable prayers,<br>Shards, flints, and pebbles should be thrown on her,<br>Yet here she is allowed her virgin rites,<br>Her maiden strewments, and the bringing [[Home\|home]]<br>Of bell and burial. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Must there no more be done? |
| PRIEST | No more be done.<br>We should profane the service of the dead<br>To sing sage requiem and such rest to her<br>As to peace-parted souls. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | Lay her i’th’ earth,<br>And from her fair and unpolluted flesh<br>May violets [[spring]]. I tell thee, churlish priest,<br>A minist’ring angel shall my sister be<br>When thou liest howling. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (aside) What, the fair [[Ophelia]]! |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (scattering [[Flowers\|flowers]])<br>Sweets to the sweet. Farewell.<br>I hoped thou shouldst have been my [[Hamlet]]’s wife.<br>I thought thy bride-bed to have decked, sweet maid,<br>And not t’have strewed thy [[Grave\|grave]]. |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | O, treble woe<br>Fall ten times treble on that cursed head<br>Whose wicked deed thy most ingenious sense<br>Deprived thee of!—Hold off the earth a while,<br>Till I have caught her once more in mine arms.<br>He leaps into the [[Grave\|grave]]<br>Now pile your [[Dust\|dust]] upon the quick and dead<br>Till of this flat a mountain you have made<br>To o’ertop old Pelion, or the skyish head<br>Of blue Olympus. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (coming forward) What is he whose grief<br>Bears such an emphasis, whose phrase of sorrow<br>Conjures the wand’ring [[Stars\|stars]] and makes them stand<br>Like [[Wonder\|wonder]]-wounded hearers? This is I,<br>[[Hamlet]] the Dane. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Hamlet]] leaps in after [[Laertes]] |
| [[Laertes\|LAERTES]] | The devil take thy [[Soul\|soul]]. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Thou pray’st not [[Well\|well]].<br>I prithee take thy fingers from my throat,<br>For though I am not splenative and rash,<br>Yet have I something in me dangerous,<br>Which let thy wiseness [[Fear\|fear]]. Away thy hand. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | (to Lords)<br>Pluck them asunder. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | [[Hamlet]], [[Hamlet]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL THE LORDS |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | Gentlemen! |
| [[Horatio\|HORATIO]] | (to [[Hamlet]]) Good my lord, be quiet. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | Why, I will fight with him upon this theme<br>Until my eyelids will no longer wag. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | O my son, what theme? |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | I loved [[Ophelia]]. Forty thousand brothers<br>Could not, with all their quantity of [[Love\|love]],<br>Make up my sum.—What wilt thou do for her? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | O, he is mad, [[Laertes]]. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Gertrude\|GERTRUDE]] | (to [[Laertes]]) For [[Love\|love]] of God, forbear him. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]]) ‘Swounds, show me what thou’lt do.<br>Woot weep, woot fight, woot fast, woot tear thyself,<br>Woot drink up eisel, eat a crocodile?<br>I’ll do’t. Dost thou come here to whine,<br>To outface me with leaping in her [[Grave\|grave]]?<br>Be buried quick with her, and so will I.<br>And if thou prate of [[Mountains\|mountains]], let them throw<br>Millions of acres on us, till our ground,<br>Singeing his pate against the burning zone,<br>Make Ossa like a wart. Nay, an thou’lt mouth,<br>I’ll rant as [[Well\|well]] as thou. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] to [[Laertes]] This is mere [[Madness\|madness]], |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | And thus a while the fit will work on him.<br>Anon, as patient as the female dove<br>When that her golden couplets are disclosed,<br>His [[Silence\|silence]] will sit drooping. |
| [[Hamlet\|HAMLET]] | (to [[Laertes]])<br>Hear you, sir,<br>What is the reason that you use me thus?<br>I loved you ever. But it is no matter.<br>Let Hercules himself do what he may,<br>The cat will mew, and dog will have his day. Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Claudius\|CLAUDIUS]] | I pray you, good [[Horatio]], wait upon him. Exit [[Horatio]]<br><br>(To [[Laertes]]).Strengthen your patience in our last<br>[[Night\|night]]’s speech.<br>We’ll put the matter to the present push.—<br>Good [[Gertrude]], set some watch over your son.—<br>This [[Grave\|grave]] shall have a living monument.<br>An hour of quiet shortly shall we see;<br>Till then, in patience our proceeding be. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
